# Flyology Object Storage correctness review — findings

Date: 2026-09-06

Every finding below was filed as a GitHub issue on flyology-ada/flyology-object-storage (#13 to #74,
filed 2026-09-07, each labelled `bug` plus its severity `P0`, `P1` or `P2`, and `documentation` where
the drift is documentary); the finding-to-issue mapping is in `review/ISSUES.md`, and the Codex triage
brief in `review/TRIAGE_FOR_CODEX.md`.

## Scope and method

This document consolidates a correctness review of Flyology Object Storage, an
Ada 2022 S3 client and pluggable object-storage server, at commit `5fe6e3e`
(origin/main on 2026-09-06). The review covered the core crate under `src/`
(about 257,000 lines, of which 79,000 are the generated botocore descriptor),
the SQLite backend crate under `sqlite/`, the server executable under
`server/`, and the generator tooling under `tools/`. Fifteen per-area
discovery reviews produced candidate findings. Every candidate was then
verified adversarially by an independent agent, which re-derived each claim
from the source rather than from the discovery text. Where the claim depended
on S3 wire semantics, the verifier read the pinned botocore S3 model
(`service-2.json` at botocore commit `36c34f15`), the repository's own
compatibility matrix and qualification documents, and, for interop claims,
drove the pinned MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS images or a stock boto3 client
against the repository's own memory server. Only confirmed findings are listed
here. Each confirmed finding has a runnable reproduction program that asserts
the correct behaviour and fails on the current sources.

The severity rubric used throughout:

- **P0** — memory or aliasing unsafety; security-relevant (signature
  verification bypass, path traversal or symlink escape, credential or secret
  exposure, authorisation bypass); data loss or durability violation (an
  acknowledged write that is not durable or is silently corrupted); hang or
  deadlock in normal operation; or an interop-breaking protocol violation that
  a stock client or server rejects.
- **P1** — wrong result or unexpected exception on valid input via a reachable
  path, or a documented contract violated (`.ads` comment, README,
  compatibility matrix, qualification document).
- **P2** — edge-case incorrectness, wrong diagnostics, or contract drift
  needing unusual input.

Documented policy values (limits, defaults, capacities) were not counted as
bugs unless they break a protocol rule or a documented invariant; they are
listed under "Observations not counted" in each area section.

The severities in this document are the verifier's corrected ones. Where a
verifier raised, lowered or re-scoped a discovery severity, that is recorded in
the finding's Notes.

## Baselines

All maintained gates were run before review work started (details and logs in
`review/baseline-notes.md`): root build, root test suite (with
`FLYOLOGY_S3_SERVICE_MODEL` set as CI does), SQLite tests, the GNATprove gate
(999 of 999 checks proved), the repository integrity check, and the botocore
model verifier all pass. The Docker conformance scripts fail against all three
third-party servers at the same exchange; that failure is itself a finding
(CLIENT-LOW-1). The corpus passes against the three Flyology backends. The two
s5cmd-driven CI jobs cannot fail as written (BASELINE-2).

## How to reproduce

One reproduction harness was added, `tests/repros`, an Alire crate pinned to
the root crate. Each program asserts the correct behaviour, so on the current
sources it prints `BUG REPRODUCED` and exits 1. After a fix it prints `PASS`
and exits 0. A hang is reported as exit 124 by the harness `timeout` wrapper.
Exit 2 means a build failure.

| Harness | Run one program | Run all |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tests/repros` (Ada programs) | `./run.sh repro_<id>` | `./run-all.sh` |
| `tests/repros/shell` (shell programs) | `./repro_<id>.sh` | (run each) |
| `tests/repros/python` (stock-client probes) | see each file's header | (run each) |

`run.sh` takes an optional second argument, a time limit in seconds (default
60), and serializes builds with a lock directory. Programs whose name ends in
`_probe` pass on the current sources and exist to show that a suspected defect
is absent; `run-all.sh` skips them. Programs that need a Docker server read the
endpoint from environment variables named in their header comment.

`src/repro_support.ad?` provides `Check`/`Note`/`Finish` and raw HTTP
response helpers. `src/repro_s3_server.ad?` provides an in-process S3
application over the memory backend with SigV4-signed raw requests, a
loopback listener, and direct access to the backend store.

The harness directory and the `review/` reports are untracked additions. No
library, test, or script was modified by the review.

| CHECKSUM | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | CHECKSUM-2 duplicates SERVER-1 (primary) and contributes the boto3 evidence, `repro_checksum_2` and `tests/repros/python/checksum_boto3_probe.py` |
| BACKEND-MEMORY | 0 | 2 | 0 | 2 | BACKEND-MEMORY-3 is the backend half of SERVER-2, which is primary and carries the count; it contributes `repro_backend_memory_3` and the MinIO delete-marker oracle |

| CLIENT-OBJECTS | 2 | 0 | 1 | 3 | CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 and CLIENT-OBJECTS-2 raised from P1 to P0 by the verifier; CLIENT-OBJECTS-3 added by the verifier from the discovery report's second uncounted observation |
| CLIENT-BUCKETS | 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 raised from P2 to P1 by the verifier on the strength of the MinIO and in-repo server 403 captures; CLIENT-BUCKETS-3 added by the verifier from the discovery report's first uncounted observation, verdict DOCUMENTED LIMITATION but counted as confirmed because the documented rule breaks a public parameter against a matrix-listed server; CLIENT-BUCKETS-2's mechanism lives in `Client.Low_Level` and is counted only here, because the public promise it breaks is this area's convenience `Head` and CLIENT-LOW did not report it |
| TRANSFERS | 0 | 0 | 1 | 1 | TRANSFERS-1 confirmed with one sub-claim refuted (the wrong text is not reachable from `Transfer_Many`) |
| S3-DOMAIN-A | 2 | 4 | 1 | 7 | S3-DOMAIN-A-2 raised from P1 to P0 by the verifier, because `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65` claims a six-server oracle for an operation whose first undelimited page no pinned third-party server can deliver, and the CI lane that would have caught it is switched off rather than fixed; S3-DOMAIN-A-5 raised from P2 to P0 because the condition the discovery report itself set for P0 — a stock client that omits `xmlns` on this body — was met by `mc version enable`; S3-DOMAIN-A-6 and S3-DOMAIN-A-7 added by the verifier from the discovery report's first and fourth uncounted observations, after live evidence contradicted the reasons they were set aside |
| S3-DOMAIN-B | 2 | 2 | 5 | 9 | S3-DOMAIN-B-1 and S3-DOMAIN-B-2 raised from P1 to P0 by the verifier after driving the named stock clients at the real application and capturing their bodies off the wire; S3-DOMAIN-B-8 added by the verifier (found while verifying S3-DOMAIN-B-7, whose MinIO response was rejected before the checksum rule was reached) and S3-DOMAIN-B-9 promoted by the verifier from the discovery report's fourth uncounted observation once MinIO was shown to produce the empty tag set; the mandatory `Content-MD5` that stops boto3 and aws-cli on the DeleteObjects route is SERVER-1 and is counted in that area only |
| SERVER-EXE | 0 | 1 | 4 | 5 | SERVER-EXE-5 added by the verifier from the discovery report's observations list, where the missing files-root lock was recorded and deferred to the FILES area; no discovery finding was refuted and no severity changed |
| MODEL | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | none |
| BASELINE | 0 | 1 | 0 | 1 | BASELINE-1 was reclassified as a client defect and is filed as CLIENT-LOW-1; it is not listed here |
| **Total** | **13** | **20** | **29** | **62** | see the area rows |

Duplicates are not counted as findings. A partially confirmed finding is counted once.

## WIRE: wire core, XML boundary, request targets, byte ranges, IMF dates, root validators (WIRE-1, WIRE-1b, WIRE-2 .. WIRE-4)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage.ad?`, `-s3-wire_core.ad?`, `-s3-xml.ad?`, `-s3-xml_writers.ad?`, `-s3-strict_xml_codecs.ad?`, `-s3-requests.ad?`, `-s3-core.ad?` and `-s3-imf_dates.ad?`, with the listing serializers in `-s3-listings.adb` and the request handlers in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` read to establish reachability.

### WIRE-1: A C0-control byte in an accepted object key makes every listing without `encoding-type=url` answer 500

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:228-234` (`Escape`) and `:252-253` (`Escape_Text`); key contract at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:672-675` and `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-490`; percent decoding and the only key gate at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-requests.adb:62-88` and `:171-173`; serializer at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1033-1034`; the `Encoded` no-op at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10657-10660`, repeated at `:10793`, `:10960` and `:11200`; the narrow local handler at `:10763-10768` and the catch-all at `:18056-18066`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_wire_1.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Valid_Object_Key` accepts every non-NUL byte, so a key holding U+0001 is stored and stays individually addressable, but `XML.Escape` raises `XML_Error` for every byte below 32 except TAB, LF and CR, and `Escape_Text` is the only text escaper the listing serializers use. Serializing a page that contains the key therefore raises inside `Listings.Serialize_*` before `Apps.Respond` is reached, and the handler's catch-all turns it into 500 InternalError. ListObjects v1, ListObjectsV2 and ListObjectVersions for that bucket page fail for every client that does not send `encoding-type=url`, deterministically, until the key is deleted; ListMultipartUploads and ListParts use the same `Encoded` shape. DeleteObjects cannot clean up: a request document carrying the byte raw or as `&#1;` is rejected 400 MalformedXML by the crate's own `Test_Valid_Chars`, so only `DELETE /bucket/a%01b` removes it. The pinned botocore model's `EncodingType` shape documents that an object key can contain any Unicode character and that the parameter is optional, so the keys themselves are valid input.

**Notes:** `aws-cli` sends `encoding-type=url` on every listing and never reaches it, which is why the verifier kept P1 rather than P0; boto3 and the Flyology client's own default listing calls do not send it. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour, so no test needs changing; `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9223-9238` asserts only that the escaper must raise for NUL. A fix needs an externally visible maintainer decision between three options: narrow `Valid_Object_Key` to reject C0 controls, which changes a documented `.ads` contract and rejects keys AWS accepts; percent-encode unescapable bytes in the listing serializers regardless of `EncodingType`, which changes response bytes; or map the failure to a defined 4xx. WIRE-1b and WIRE-4 are siblings that share the same escaper asymmetry.

---

### WIRE-1b: A key with a non-UTF-8 byte produces a 200 listing whose body is not the UTF-8 it declares

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report listed this only as a secondary effect of WIRE-1 and assigned no severity; the verifier raised it to a distinct finding because the mechanism is silent success rather than 500).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-490` (no UTF-8 requirement in the key contract); `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:228-234` (`Escape` rejects only bytes below 32); existing assertion that the crate's own reader must refuse such a document at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9219-9221`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_wire_1b.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** Because the key contract admits arbitrary non-NUL bytes and the escaper guards only the C0 range, a key containing a byte that is not valid UTF-8 is copied verbatim into a listing whose XML declaration says `encoding="UTF-8"`. The server answers 200 and the response passes its own framing checks, but the document is not well formed: every conforming parser, including this crate's `S3.XML.Parse`, rejects the whole page, so the client sees a parse failure rather than a server error and cannot tell which key caused it. It is deterministic and confined to buckets holding such a key, and `encoding-type=url` avoids it. The pinned botocore model describes keys as Unicode and responses as UTF-8 encoded, and a raw `0xFF` is neither.

**Notes:** Only a hand-built signed request can create the key, because botocore and every SDK that encodes keys from Unicode strings cannot emit a lone `0xFF`; that input unusualness is why the severity is P2 even though the consequence is worse than WIRE-1's. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour. A fix shares WIRE-1's maintainer decision: whether `Valid_Object_Key` gains a UTF-8 requirement, which is an `.ads` contract change, or the serializers escape unrepresentable bytes unconditionally. Counted as a separate finding from WIRE-1, not as a duplicate.

---

### WIRE-2: The entity-tag list parser rejects RFC-valid empty list elements as `Invalid_Request`

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:182-186` (element at end of field), `:197-201` (element that is neither `W/` nor a quote), `:235-249` (`Valid := True` only when the field ends after a tag); consumers `Evaluate_Object_Write_Conditions` at `:250-275`, `Valid_Object_Read_Entity_Tag_Condition` at `:278-288` and `Evaluate_Object_Read_Conditions` at `:298-336`; server admission at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16706-16718`; contract at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:126-130`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_wire_2.adb`, exit status 1, nine failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Read_Entity_Tag_List` sets `Valid` only when the field ends immediately after a quoted tag, so any empty list element, whether a trailing comma, a leading comma or `",,"` between two tags, makes the whole field `Invalid_Request`. The server turns that into 400 InvalidArgument for HeadObject and GetObject conditions and into `Invalid_Request` for PutObject write conditions, instead of evaluating the request against the tags that are present. RFC 9110 section 5.6.1 defines the recipient list form `[ element ] *( OWS "," OWS [ element ] )` and section 5.6.1.2 requires a recipient to parse and ignore a reasonable number of empty list elements, so a field a compliant intermediary may legitimately produce is answered 400 rather than 200 or 412. It is deterministic and affects only recipients, since the RFC forbids senders from generating an empty element.

**Notes:** The verifier could not settle S3's own tolerance: the pinned model types `IfMatch` as an unconstrained header string, and no live server or MinIO run was made, so a maintainer may prefer to match S3 rather than the RFC. That is the only open question; the RFC statements above are cited from memory and were marked unverified. No existing test pins the current rejection: `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:3763-3765` asserts a valid list with HT and a weak tag, `:3770-3772` rejects `*, "etag"` and `:3773-3775` rejects an oversized field. The fix is local, needs no new public constant or contract change, and cannot unbound the parser because the field is already capped by `Maximum_Condition_Length` and the loop by `Remaining_Items`.

---

### WIRE-3: A Range field whose unit is not `bytes` is answered 400 instead of being ignored

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report listed the Range policy under observations not counted and handed disposition to the server area; the verifier counted the unrecognized-unit case as a finding because RFC 9110 states a MUST there rather than a MAY, and nothing in the repository documents the 400).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16839-16847` (HeadObject) and `:17226-17234` (GetObject), the only two consumers of the classification; parser contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:172-178`, multi-range rejection documented at `:174-176`; parser-level test coverage at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:8787-8794`; documentation at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:736-737` and `:907`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_wire_3.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Parse_Range_Header` folds every shape it cannot serve into one `Malformed_Range` status, and both server call sites answer 400 InvalidRequest for all of them. For an unrecognized range unit RFC 9110 section 14.2 requires the field to be ignored and the representation returned normally, so a client sending `Range: items=0-1` gets 400 here. The same 400 covers multi-range, which is a deliberate parser decision documented in `core.ads:174-176` but whose server mapping is documented nowhere; `curl -r 0-1,4-5` is the realistic stock client that reaches it. The behaviour is deterministic and applies to HEAD as well as GET. Both external facts, the RFC 9110 requirement and the belief that AWS S3 returns 200 with the whole object for Range fields it cannot use, are recorded by the verifier as from memory and unverified; no live S3, MinIO or RustFS server was run.

**Notes:** No test asserts the server's 400: a repository-wide grep for "Range header is malformed" finds no test, and the compatibility matrix documents only the supported shapes. Two maintainer decisions are needed and neither should be taken in passing: whether `Range_Parse_Status` gains a third value distinguishing "ignore this field" from "reject this request", which is a public enumeration change in a SPARK-proved `.ads`, and which of the remaining malformed shapes should be ignored rather than rejected, which should be pinned against a live S3 or the pinned MinIO image rather than from memory. The inverted range `bytes=5-1` is a separate case that currently parses and yields 416 with `Content-Range: bytes */10`; it was not checked against any real server and is not counted.

---

### WIRE-4: A key containing CR is silently reported as a different key by every listing without `encoding-type=url`

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report named the mechanism in one line as a secondary effect of WIRE-1 with no severity and no repro; the verifier scored it above WIRE-1b because the key is reachable from a stock SDK).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:228-234`, where `Escape` exempts `Character'Val (13)` from the C0 rejection; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1033-1034`, where `Element` applies only `Escape_Text`; key contract at `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-490`; percent decoding at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-requests.adb:73-77`; existing listing coverage at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:3129-3292`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_wire_4.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** `XML.Escape` deliberately allows TAB, LF and CR through unescaped, which is correct for arbitrary text but wrong for a key, because XML 1.0 requires a parser to normalize a literal CR in character data to LF before handing it to the application. A key stored as `a\rb` is therefore reported by ListObjects v1, ListObjectsV2 and ListObjectVersions as `a\nb` whenever `encoding-type=url` is absent. The server returns 200, the document is well formed, and nothing reports an error; the client gets a key it cannot address, and a subsequent GET, HEAD or DELETE of `a%0Ab` answers 404. The same substitution corrupts a `NextMarker` derived from such a key, so a paginating client can skip or repeat entries. It is fully deterministic and reachable from a stock SDK, because botocore percent-encodes a key containing `\r` as `%0D`. The repro confirms the normalization empirically through the crate's own `S3.XML.Parse`, so the claim does not rest on the verifier's memory of the XML specification.

**Notes:** Not P0 because no stock client rejects the response and there is no data loss; the object remains stored under its real key and reachable by anyone who knows it. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour. The fix is narrower than WIRE-1's and needs no contract change if it is confined to the listing serializers, emitting `&#13;` and, for symmetry, `&#9;` and `&#10;` for the characters XML normalization would alter; whether that escaping applies to every `Escape_Text` caller or only to key-bearing elements is a maintainer decision about the crate's response bytes.

---

## SIGV4: signing, verification, admission and credentials (SIGV4-1, SIGV4-2)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4.ad?`, `-s3-sigv4_encoding.ad?`, `-s3-sigv4_verification.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.ad?` and `-server-static_credentials.ad?`, with the admission call site, body-hash enforcement and exception handlers in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` and the SigV4 sections of `docs/compatibility/s3.md`.

### SIGV4-1: A far-future `x-amz-date` escapes `Verify_Request` as `Ada.Calendar.Time_Error` and is answered 500 InternalError instead of 403 RequestTimeTooSkewed

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:157-165` (the skew test in a `declare` block outside `Timestamp_Time`), with `:57-69` (`Timestamp_Time` and its narrow handler), `:224-227` (`Verify_Request`'s `when Constraint_Error` handler) and `:209` (the credential provider is invoked only afterwards); no upper year bound at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_encoding.adb:190-195`; call site at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:6697`, catch-all at `:18056-18065`, and the mapping that should have run at `:1893-1897`; public contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.ads:41-48` and `:56-60`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_sigv4_1.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Valid_Timestamp` puts no upper bound on the year, so an `x-amz-date` anywhere in `Ada.Calendar.Year_Number` survives admission and is converted to a `Time`. The skew test then evaluates `Request_Time - Now` outside `Timestamp_Time`'s guard. With 64-bit `Duration` the difference overflows for any request time more than 292.277 years from the trusted clock, and GNAT's `"-" (Time, Time)` raises `Ada.Calendar.Time_Error`, which `Verify_Request`'s `when Constraint_Error` handler does not catch. The exception leaves the public `Verify_Request`, reaches `Handle`'s catch-all and becomes 500 InternalError for any routed request, sent to an unauthenticated peer, instead of the 403 RequestTimeTooSkewed the mapping exists to produce. It is fully deterministic and needs no valid credentials, because the check runs before the credential provider is invoked. The toolchain facts were re-derived from the toolchain the harness resolves: `Year_Number` is 1901 .. 2399 in `a-calend.ads:48`, `Duration_32_Bits` is False in `system.ads:154`, and `a-calend.adb`'s `"-"` converts any internal `Constraint_Error` into `Time_Error`, so `Constraint_Error` is never what escapes.

**Notes:** The verifier considered P1, on the grounds that the GNATdoc says the clock skew "is checked" and `Outcome_Status` has a dedicated `Request_Time_Too_Skewed` value, so an exception leaving the public function violates the documented contract and a third-party server built directly on `Authentication` would see it propagate into its own connection handler; P2 was kept because the in-tree caller contains it, the connection and process survive, and no valid request reaches the path. Years 0001 to 1900 are the same family already handled correctly: they fail `Year_Number` inside `Timestamp_Time` and yield 400 AuthorizationHeaderMalformed. No existing test asserts either case; `rg RequestTimeTooSkewed tests/ server/tests/` finds no assertion and no test sends an `x-amz-date` outside 2013. A fix needs one maintainer decision: give `Valid_Timestamp` an explicit accepted-year range, which introduces a new public policy bound in a SPARK unit and so falls under the repository's rule on specification values, or widen `Verify_Request`'s handler to `Constraint_Error | Ada.Calendar.Time_Error` and return `Request_Time_Too_Skewed`, which introduces no new interface value. The claim that aws-cli and boto3 retry 500 with backoff is the verifier's own knowledge and was not measured.

---

### SIGV4-2: `Static_Credentials.Create` accepts principals of 257 to 1,024 bytes that admission then rejects, so every correctly signed request is answered 403 SignatureDoesNotMatch

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report said P2; the verifier raised it because the failing path takes valid input, an ordinary correctly signed request, and returns a wrong result, and because `Create`'s own GNATdoc promises to reject a field that is too long).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-static_credentials.adb:31-48` (`Create` checks only empty, `> Maximum_Principal_Bytes` and control bytes) and `:80-88` (`Authenticate` returns the long principal once `Allowed`); constants at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-static_credentials.ads:34-36` and the unmet `@exception` promise at `:26`; the 256-byte rejection at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:18-31` and the collapse into one status at `:213-217`; the rendering at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1907-1910`; the layer contract that motivates 256 at `flyology_http` 0.1.3 `flyology-http-server-applications.ads:279-283`; documentation that publishes neither bound at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.ads:17-20`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_sigv4_2.adb` with helper `tests/repros/src/repro_sigv4_support.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Static_Credentials` bounds the principal at `Maximum_Principal_Bytes` of 1,024 and `Create` enforces only that, while `Authentication.Valid_Principal` rejects anything over 256 bytes because that is `flyology_http`'s `Set_Principal` contract. An operator who configures a static identity with a 257 to 1,024-byte principal, such as a tenant URN, a DN or an e-mail-style identifier, gets a provider `Create` accepted and a server on which every correctly signed request fails. The failure is deterministic and total: `Authenticate` verifies the signature successfully and returns the long principal, then admission collapses the length rejection into the same `Credential_Rejected` status a genuinely bad signature produces, and the S3 layer renders it as 403 SignatureDoesNotMatch with "The request signature does not match". Nothing in the response and nothing in either specification points at the principal, so the operator debugs a signature problem that does not exist.

**Notes:** There is a second route to the same defect, established by reading rather than by the repro: leaving `Principal` empty makes `Access_Key` the principal, and `Valid_Access_Key` accepts up to 1,024 alphanumerics, so an access key of 257 to 477 characters fails identically, 477 being the ceiling `Parse_Credential`'s 512-byte credential-text bound at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_verification.adb:75` imposes for a `us-east-1`/`s3` scope. There is no existing test on `Static_Credentials`, so nothing asserts the wrong behaviour; the corpus uses `Principal => "test-principal"` and the harness `"qualification"`. The verifier records the discovery's P2 as defensible because a principal above 256 bytes is an unusual configuration. A fix needs one maintainer decision, because both bounds are interface values: either `Maximum_Principal_Bytes` drops to the 256 the HTTP layer imposes, so a visible constant changes value and `Create` starts raising `Invalid_Credentials` for inputs it used to accept, or `Authentication` stops treating an over-long principal as `Credential_Rejected` and reports it distinctly, which needs a new `Outcome_Status` literal and a new S3 error mapping. Documenting 256 alone would leave `Create`'s `@exception Invalid_Credentials ... too long` promise unmet.

---

## SERVER: S3 application, routing, authorisation and operations (SERVER-1 .. SERVER-5)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-server.ads`, `-server-metadata_results.ads` and `-server-object_lambda_responses.ads`, with `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb`, `src/flyology-object_storage.ads` (the public `Status` type), `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb` (generation selection), the callers `tests/src/s3_server_harness.ad?` and `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json`.

### SERVER-1: Content-MD5 is mandatory on DeleteObjects, PutBucketVersioning, PutObjectTagging and the other `requestChecksumRequired` routes, so stock aws-cli v2 is rejected

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1; the verifier raised it because the rubric's P0 covers an interop-breaking protocol violation that a stock client rejects, and a stock unconfigured aws-cli v2.36.34 was observed authenticating successfully against this server and then being refused on three ordinary operations — not a model-driven-SDK hypothetical).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:17674-17683` (DeleteObjects: `MD5_Count /= 1 or else not Valid_Base64` answers 400 `InvalidRequest`), with the flexible-checksum verifier that never gets to substitute at `:17617-17670` and `:17713-17723`; `:10358-10369` (PutBucketVersioning, same gate); `:15883-15904` (object tagging, where `Algorithm_Count = 1 or else Has_Checksum_Header` answers 501 `NotImplemented` *before* the Content-MD5 gate, so no model-conformant request is accepted at all); the accept-either pattern the crate already uses elsewhere at `:5165-5200` (`Read_Bucket_Scalar_Control`'s `Require_Checksum`); documentation at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:506-508` and `:526-527`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_server_1.adb`, exit status 1, five failed expectations, with helper `tests/repros/src/repro_server_1_live.adb` (a listener holder for the stock-client run, not an assertion).

**What is wrong:** For every operation the pinned model marks `requestChecksumRequired: true`, the server treats Content-MD5 as the only acceptable integrity header rather than as one of several, and the gate runs before the already-implemented flexible-checksum verdict. A request carrying a correct `x-amz-checksum-crc32` or `-crc64nvme` is answered 400 `InvalidRequest` and nothing is deleted or configured; object tagging additionally answers 501 to any request carrying a flexible checksum at all. Since AWS turned on default integrity protection, stock aws-cli v2 sends `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC64NVME` plus `x-amz-checksum-crc64nvme` and no Content-MD5 on exactly these operations, so unmodified `aws s3api delete-objects`, `put-bucket-versioning`, `put-bucket-tagging` and `put-object-tagging` all fail, and by inspection so do the other `requestChecksumRequired` routes. It is fully deterministic. The pinned model settles the input side: `ContentMD5` is in no such operation's `required` list, and for `DeleteObjects` and `PutBucketLifecycleConfiguration` the member does not exist in the input shape at all, so a client generated from this model has no modelled way to send it; the model's own `ChecksumAlgorithm` documentation says the flexible checksum is how `requestChecksumRequired` is satisfied.

**Notes:** Evidence is a stock aws-cli v2.36.34 driven against the crate's own loopback listener with only `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and `AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` set and no checksum configuration; `list-objects-v2`, `s3 cp`, `s3api put-object` and single-key `s3 rm` all succeed in the same session, so authentication, signing and routing are fine and only these routes fail. CHECKSUM-2 is the same defect seen from a stock boto3 1.43.89 client and adds the sharper sub-case that `DeleteObjectsRequest` has no `ContentMD5` member, so DeleteObjects is unreachable from a stock SDK under any client configuration; SERVER-1 is the primary finding. Existing tests pass only because they never exercise the model-conformant shape: `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:16039-16045` asserts `InvalidRequest` for a request with no integrity header at all, the ten-algorithm loop at `:16080-16096` leaves `Include_MD5` at its default `True`, and `tests/scripts/run-s3-server-slice.sh:107` drives DeleteObjects only through s5cmd v2.3.0 on aws-sdk-go v1, which still sends Content-MD5. This is not recorded as a documented limitation: `docs/compatibility/s3.md:506-508` states the rule as intended behaviour but never discloses that a model-driven client cannot comply. A fix is a maintainer policy decision, not a mechanical change: whether "at least one of Content-MD5 or a verified flexible checksum" replaces "Content-MD5 required" on these routes, and whether the object-tagging 501 at `:15889` becomes an accept path; `docs/compatibility/s3.md:506-508` and `:526-527` and the qualification prose would have to be restated with it.

---

### SERVER-2: Reading a delete marker loses `x-amz-delete-marker`, and a read of the marker's own version is 404 instead of 405

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report said P2; the verifier raised it because the state is not unusual input — after any DELETE of any key in a versioned bucket that key's current version is a delete marker, so every subsequent GET or HEAD takes this path — and because the response is indistinguishable from "this key never existed". The missing-header half alone would be P2; the 404-instead-of-405 half is the reason for P1).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1929-1936` (`Send_Backend_Error` maps `Not_Found` to 404 `NoSuchBucket`/`NoSuchKey` with no marker-aware branch); the read arms that forward the plain result at `:17253-17262` (GetObject) and `:16880-16957` (HeadObject); the backend collapse at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:318-331` (`Selected_Object_Index` returns 0 when the selected generation `Is_Delete_Marker`); the public `Status` enumeration with no delete-marker outcome at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-62`; the only `x-amz-delete-marker` emitters are the DeleteObject arm and the WriteGetObjectResponse forwarding table.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_server_2.adb`, exit status 1, seven failed expectations; controls confirm the DELETE itself reports `x-amz-delete-marker: true` with a version id, a live key still reads 200, and a key that never existed reads 404 with no marker header.

**What is wrong:** The backend collapses "the selected generation is a delete marker" onto the same `Natural` 0 it uses for "no such generation", so the server sees `Not_Found` and emits a bare 404 `NoSuchKey`. Two documented behaviours are lost: a read of a key whose current version is a delete marker does not carry `x-amz-delete-marker: true`, and a read of the marker's own version returns 404 instead of 405 `Method Not Allowed` with `Last-Modified`. Any versioning-aware client — replication, backup, or a tool walking ListObjectVersions and reading each generation — cannot distinguish "deleted" from "never existed", and cannot tell that the version it asked for is a marker. Everyone using a versioned bucket hits the first case on the very next read after any DELETE, deterministically. The pinned model's `GetObjectOutput.DeleteMarker` documentation states both behaviours verbatim, including the 405 and the `Last-Modified` header, and `HeadObjectOutput.DeleteMarker` carries the same binding.

**Notes:** BACKEND-MEMORY-3 is the backend half of this defect, verified at the backend interface with `repro_backend_memory_3` and with the pinned MinIO container as an oracle (HEAD of a marker-hidden key returns 404 with `x-amz-delete-marker: true`; HEAD and GET of the exact marker version return 405); SERVER-2 is the primary finding. This is not a documented limitation: `docs/compatibility/s3.md:59` and `:851-858` list exclusions that do not include delete-marker reads, and `:539-541` documents publishing and removing markers with no read-side caveat. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour and none asserts the right one — `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:18196-18206` and `:17754` cover only the DeleteObject response headers — but `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb` does require a simple delete to make marker reads `Not_Found`, so a fix must update that expectation. The fix cannot be made in the server alone and needs a maintainer decision on the public API: either a new `Status` literal (for example `Delete_Marker`) in the visible type at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-62`, or an out-parameter on the backend read operations carrying the marker's presence and `Last_Modified`; either way every backend and the conformance suite must be extended together.

---

### SERVER-3: HeadObject emits `x-amz-version-id: null` for an unversioned object while PutObject and GetObject omit it

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report listed this under observations not counted as "harmless to clients, inconsistent between the two reads"; the verifier counted it because it also deviates from the crate's own documented three-way rule, and kept it at P2 because no client behaviour changes as a result).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16619-16622` (HeadObject's `Set_Common_Headers` writes the header unconditionally, substituting the literal `null`); the conditional GetObject path at `:4189-4194` (`Response_IO.Begin_Object`); the crate's own rule at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450`; the identical model bindings for `HeadObjectOutput.VersionId` and `GetObjectOutput.VersionId` in `review/baseline/service-2.json`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_server_3.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; controls confirm PutObject omits the header for unversioned publication, the two reads agree on a real version id in a versioned bucket, and an explicit `?versionId=null` selection reports `null` on both reads.

**What is wrong:** `docs/compatibility/s3.md:446-450` defines three distinct cases — omit for unversioned publication, the exact opaque identity for enabled publication, `null` for suspended publication — and HeadObject reports an unversioned object with the spelling reserved for a suspended bucket, while GetObject writes the header only when a version exists or the request asked for one. Anyone doing HEAD-then-GET on the same key in a never-versioned bucket sees two different answers, deterministically. The model gives the two reads no licence to differ: both members carry the same header binding and documentation.

**Notes:** This behaviour masks two client-side defects in CI. The client's `Head_Object_Response_Bound` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658`, and the same predicate at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:139-155`) and `Get_Object_Response_Bound` (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-137`) reject a 200 that carries no `x-amz-version-id` after a `versionId=null` selection, and this server always supplies one — HEAD unconditionally at `:16619-16622`, GET through the `Has_Version_ID` branch at `:4192` — so the corpus passes against Flyology while all three third-party servers fail at that exchange. Those are CLIENT-LOW-1 (formerly BASELINE-1) and CLIENT-OBJECTS-1, and SERVER-3 is why neither shows up in the in-tree lanes. A maintainer changing the emitted header must therefore sequence it with the client predicates and confirm that no downstream oracle in `tests/` or the six-server comparison depends on the current spelling: the verifier's proposed fix (make HEAD match `:4189-4194`) keeps the `null` for an explicit `versionId=null` request and so leaves the lanes green, but any change that also dropped the header in that case would turn them red until the client is fixed. The verifier deliberately did not check MinIO, since the finding rests on the crate's disagreement with itself and with its own compatibility document rather than on AWS behaviour; the recollection that AWS omits the header on never-versioned buckets is unverified and not load-bearing. No existing test asserts either behaviour; the fix is a one-line change at `:16619` to match `:4189-4194`.

---

### SERVER-4: CompleteMultipartUpload `<Location>` is a path, not the URI the model documents

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report listed it under observations not counted, as "clients only echo it"; the verifier counted it because it is a modelled output member carrying a value of the wrong form on an ordinary success path, and kept it at P2 because no stock client dereferences it).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:13193-13197`, where the completion result is built with `Location => "/" & Bucket & "/" & URI_Encode (Key)`; the serializer at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart.adb:1125`; `shapes.CompleteMultipartUploadOutput.members.Location` in `review/baseline/service-2.json`, documented as "The URI that identifies the newly created object"; `docs/compatibility/s3.md:71`, which claims the operation without excluding the result URI.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_server_4.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation; controls confirm the completion is 200 and that `<Bucket>`, `<Key>` and the composite `<ETag>` are present and correct.

**What is wrong:** Every successful CompleteMultipartUpload returns `<Location>/bucket/key</Location>` where the pinned model documents a URI; RFC 3986 requires a scheme, so this is a relative reference. A caller that treats the member as a URI — resolving it, storing it as a canonical object address, or comparing it with what AWS or MinIO returned — must know the endpoint out of band to make it usable. It affects every multipart completion and is deterministic. The model constrains `Location` only as a plain string, so the divergence is against the documentation rather than against a binding.

**Notes:** The crate documents nothing about `Location`. Fixing it needs a maintainer decision rather than an edit, because building an absolute URI requires choosing a scheme and this crate has none to use: `Apps.Request_Scheme` is `Plain_HTTP` for every request, since `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:168-170` and `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb:89-91` both call `Apps.Create` without a `Scheme` argument. The choice is between a configured public base URI, deriving one from the signed `Host` header, or keeping the path deliberately and documenting it as a boundary in `docs/compatibility/s3.md`. No existing test asserts either form. The same always-`Plain_HTTP` constraint is what stops SERVER-5 short of a mechanical fix.

---

### SERVER-5: The modern default streaming payload sentinel is reported as `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed`

**Severity:** P2 — wrong diagnostics (the discovery report listed the `aws-chunked` rejection under observations not counted; the verifier counted only the diagnostic and refuted the discovery's premise that such a request receives 501).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:139-140`, where `Payload_Hash /= SigV4.Unsigned_Payload and then not Valid_SHA256_Hex (Payload_Hash)` is one disjunct of the `elsif` chain evaluated at `:131-147` that returns `Malformed_Credentials`, before any signature verification (which begins at `:176`); the rendering at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1889-1892`; the arm that would answer correctly at `:14891-14895` (PutObject) and its UploadPart twin at `:12617-12621`; the HTTPS gates at `:6733` and `:6874`; documentation at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:342-345` and `:457-460`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_server_5.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations; controls confirm that the same `aws-chunked` request with an ordinary hex payload hash gets 501 `NotImplemented`, that an ordinary PutObject succeeds, and that a well-shaped but wrong payload hash is still `XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch`.

**What is wrong:** `Verify_Request` treats "the payload hash is neither 64 hex digits nor `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`" as the same failure as a broken Authorization header, and the server reports that as 400 `AuthorizationHeaderMalformed` with "The authorization header is malformed". Since AWS turned on default integrity protection, aws-cli v2 and boto3 send `x-amz-content-sha256: STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` for PutObject and UploadPart over HTTPS — captured on the wire from aws-cli/2.36.34 through a TLS sniffer — so an operator who terminates TLS in front of this server sees every upload fail with a message accusing their credentials, while the real cause, an unimplemented payload encoding that the crate reports correctly one branch away as 501 `NotImplemented "aws-chunked payloads are not implemented"`, is never mentioned. It is deterministic for anyone in that deployment and invisible to anyone speaking plain HTTP, where the same client sends a fixed-length body with a header checksum that the server accepts.

**Notes:** Partially confirmed: rejecting `aws-chunked` framing is a documented limitation (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:342-345`, `:457-460`), so only the diagnostic is counted, and the discovery report's premise that such a request receives 501 is refuted — it receives 400, because the payload-hash shape check runs inside `Verify_Request` and never reaches the `aws-chunked` arm. It is not P0 because a stock PutObject over the transport this crate actually ships (plain HTTP) does not use `aws-chunked` and succeeds; the failing shape appears only when the client's endpoint URL is `https://`, which for this crate means a TLS terminator, since nothing in the repository passes `Scheme => Secure_HTTPS`. The repro substitutes the sentinel into an otherwise ordinary signed request, which is sound because the server decides before checking the signature, and necessary because the crate's own `S3.SigV4.Sign` raises `Invalid_Signing_Input` for that sentinel. The compatibility document words its exclusion as *SigV4* streaming, while `STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` is the unsigned variant, and `:343` positively claims physical checksum trailers, so the document does not clearly cover the modern default and says nothing about the status code. No existing test covers a non-hex payload-hash sentinel. A fix needs a maintainer decision on where the boundary belongs: either `Verify_Request` gains a distinct status for a recognised-but-unimplemented streaming sentinel, which is a new literal in a public `Authentication` type, or the S3 application inspects `x-amz-content-sha256` before authenticating, which changes what is decided before the signature is verified.

---

## CHECKSUM: S3 checksums, CRC combination, policy, streaming engine, xxHash bridge and trailers (CHECKSUM-1, CHECKSUM-2)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-checksums.ad?`, `-s3-checksum_crc.ad?`, `-s3-checksum_policy.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-checksum_engine.ad?`, `src/flyology_object_storage_xxhash.c`, `vendor/xxhash/`, `docs/architecture/s3-checksums.md`, `tests/src/s3_checksum_corpus.adb` and `tools/generate_s3_checksum_corpus.py`, with the PutObject and document checksum preflights in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb`, the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json` and the qualification documents read as the contract.

### CHECKSUM-1: PutObject answers `InvalidRequest` where the pinned model documents `BadDigest` for an SDK-algorithm / value-header mismatch

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14812-14821`, the branch inside `Checksum_Value_Header_Count = 1` that answers 400 `InvalidRequest` "The checksum algorithm and value header differ" when the SDK algorithm header names a different algorithm than the value header, with the selector set at `:14786-14802`; the same shape in `Put_Object_Annotation` at `:15553-15562`; the 18 sites that do emit `BadDigest` (`:1986`, `:3864`, `:5263`, `:5345`, `:8243`, `:8255`, `:10191`, `:10206`, `:10403`, `:10418`, `:13129`, `:13810`, `:15935`, `:16468`, `:17689`, `:17721`, `:18017`, `:18026`); `shapes.PutObjectRequest.members.ChecksumAlgorithm` documentation in `review/baseline/service-2.json`; the existing assertion at `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6428` (fixture at `:6334-6341`, message at `:6431`); `docs/qualification/put-object.md:33`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_checksum_1.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; control 1 shows the identical request with a matching selector is accepted with 200, and control 2 shows a genuine value/content mismatch on the same operation is answered `BadDigest`.

**What is wrong:** When a PutObject request carries both `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: <A>` and a value header `x-amz-checksum-<B>` for a different algorithm B, the preflight answers 400 `InvalidRequest`, while the pinned model's documentation for `PutObjectRequest.ChecksumAlgorithm` says Amazon S3 fails that request "with a BadDigest error". The consequence is purely which `<Code>` a caller or a conformance harness keyed on the error code observes: the status is 400 either way, the object is not stored either way, and botocore raises `ClientError` for both. It is deterministic and reachable by any client that sets both headers inconsistently. The model gives UploadPart a different rule — an individual checksum makes S3 ignore the algorithm parameter — which the server implements separately, so the PutObject branch is not a shared-policy consequence.

**Notes:** Two sub-claims partly refute the finding and are why it stays P2 and rests on prose only. First, `BadDigest` is not a shape anywhere in the pinned model, and PutObject's modelled `errors` list is exactly `InvalidRequest`, `InvalidWriteOffset`, `TooManyParts` and `EncryptionTypeMismatch`, so the code the server emits is PutObject's own modelled error and `BadDigest` appears only inside documentation strings. Second, the model's error table defines `BadDigest` as "The Content-MD5 you specified did not match what we received", a content mismatch, which is exactly the meaning this server reserves it for; reserving `BadDigest` for a real digest mismatch and `InvalidRequest` for a malformed header combination is a defensible reading of the same document. `Put_Object_Annotation` uses the same mapping but is a Flyology extension with no model text. An existing test pins the current code at `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6428`, so a change would need that assertion edited; no documented deliberate deviation exists in `docs/qualification/put-object.md` or `docs/compatibility/s3.md`. A fix is a maintainer decision because it changes an externally visible error code.

---

### CHECKSUM-2: Operations the model marks `requestChecksumRequired` reject every stock SDK request, because the server demands Content-MD5 and ignores the `x-amz-checksum-<alg>` group the SDK sends instead

**Severity:** P0 (not in the discovery report; found by the stock-client probe this area required). This is the same defect as SERVER-1, which is the primary finding; it is counted there, not here.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10352-10370` (`Put_Bucket_Versioning`'s Content-MD5 precondition, which runs before the branch at `:10376-10412` that reads the document and calls `Verify_Document_Checksum`); `:8013-8019` (`Put_Bucket_Tagging`, same precondition); `:17683` (DeleteObjects, "DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header"); `:5033-5140` (`Verify_Document_Checksum`, which already parses and verifies the whole `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm` / `x-amz-checksum-<alg>` / `x-amz-trailer` group for all ten algorithms); the 27 operations carrying `httpChecksum.requestChecksumRequired: true` in `review/baseline/service-2.json`, and `DeleteObjectsRequest`, which has no `ContentMD5` member at all.

**Repro:** duplicate of SERVER-1; it nonetheless contributes its own evidence — `tests/repros/src/repro_checksum_2.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations, and the stock-client probe `tests/repros/python/checksum_boto3_probe.py`, exit status 1 on cases (e) `delete_objects` and (f) `put_bucket_versioning`. SERVER-1's own evidence is aws-cli v2.36.34.

**What is wrong:** Since botocore 1.36 the default `request_checksum_calculation` is `when_supported`, so a stock AWS SDK satisfies an operation's `requestChecksumRequired` trait by sending `x-amz-sdk-checksum-algorithm: CRC32` together with `x-amz-checksum-crc32`, and sends no Content-MD5 at all. The server's preflight for those operations requires exactly one syntactically valid Content-MD5 and returns 400 `InvalidRequest` before reaching the verifier that already implements the checksum group. Every one of the 27 `requestChecksumRequired` operations is therefore uncallable from a current boto3, aws-cli v2 or any other modern SDK using default settings; it is fully deterministic, since the guard is a pure header check with no timing or backend dependence. The only workaround is setting `request_checksum_calculation=when_required` *and* passing `ContentMD5=` by hand, which is impossible for `DeleteObjects`, whose pinned request shape has no `ContentMD5` member — so `DeleteObjects` is unreachable from a stock SDK under any configuration. Object-path checksums are unaffected: the probe's `put_object`, `create_multipart_upload`, `upload_part`, `complete_multipart_upload`, `head_object` and `get_object` calls were all accepted.

**Notes:** SERVER-1 is the primary finding and carries the disposition; this entry exists to record the boto3-side evidence and the `DeleteObjects` sharpening. Wire evidence: boto3/botocore 1.43.89 on Python 3.13.7, resolved from PyPI by `uv run --python 3.13 --with boto3` with nothing installed system-wide, driven against the repository's prebuilt `tests/bin/s3_memory_server` (not rebuilt, no Docker); the exact captured request headers and the exact 400 response bodies are in the verification report. The requirement is *described* in `docs/compatibility/s3.md:638`, `docs/qualification/bucket-versioning.md:47`, `docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md:51` and `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md:11`, but always as Content-MD5 *plus* the checksum group and never as an acknowledged interop boundary, so it is not a documented limitation; those documents and their corpora would need updating alongside any fix. The probe also settled two open discovery questions in the crate's favour, recorded under coverage evidence below.

---

## BACKEND-MEMORY: memory backend, backend interface and the shared listing builders (BACKEND-MEMORY-1 .. BACKEND-MEMORY-3)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ad?`, `-backends-listing.ad?`, `-backends-bucket_listing.ad?`, `-backends-multipart_listing.ad?` and `docs/architecture/memory-backend.md`, with `src/flyology-object_storage.ads` for the public `Status` and information types, the identical self-copy predicates in the files and SQLite backends, the ListMultipartUploads and CopyObject arms of `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb`, the qualification documents, and the pinned botocore model.

### BACKEND-MEMORY-1: ListMultipartUploads paired cursor skips same-key uploads whose SHA-256 upload IDs sort against initiation order

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-multipart_listing.adb:84-91`, where the exclusive marker test is `Candidate_Key = After_Key and then (After_ID'Length = 0 or else Candidate_ID <= After_ID)` — purely lexical on the upload ID, with no reference to `Initiated`; the page order at `:17-30`, which sorts same-key candidates by `Initiated` first and breaks equal-time ties with the upload ID; the cursor construction from the last returned candidate at `:141-149`; the SHA-256 upload-ID derivation at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:2830-2833` and the whole-second clock at `:207-208`; the server's straight pass-through of the markers at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11186-11196` and `:11240-11246`; the contract stating the two rules independently at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2711-2715`; the requirement the behaviour violates at `docs/qualification/list-multipart-uploads-preparation.md:42-45`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_memory_1.adb` with helper `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_memory_support.ad?`, exit status 1, eleven failed expectations — 5 of 6 uploads lost at the backend interface and 4 of 6 over the wire.

**What is wrong:** The builder sorts same-key candidates by initiation time, filters them by lexical upload ID, and builds the continuation cursor from the last returned candidate; the three rules are mutually inconsistent whenever the two orders disagree, which for this backend is the normal case, because upload IDs are SHA-256 digests and carry no relation to creation order. A client that paginates ListMultipartUploads with the markers the service itself returned silently loses every same-key upload whose ID sorts at or before the marker, and the pages report `IsTruncated false`, so nothing tells the client anything is missing. Anyone using `max-uploads` on a key with several concurrent uploads hits it — multi-writer retry loops, orphan-upload sweepers and lifecycle cleanup are exactly the callers that paginate — and `max-uploads` is a modelled request member from 1 through 1,000, so the page bound is not unusual input. It is fully deterministic for a given bucket, key and counter triple, and invisible only when all same-key uploads land in the same wall-clock second. The qualification document explicitly forbids this: a service-produced marker pair "must always resume without skipping, repeating, or looping".

**Notes:** Existing coverage passes while asserting a weaker property: `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:3318-3328` creates all five uploads in one tight loop, so the two same-key uploads share an `Initiated` value and the page order falls through to the upload-ID tie break, which coincides with the marker rule, and the marker assertion at `:3379-3389` deliberately uses smaller and larger IDs rather than creation order. No test creates same-key uploads across a second boundary, and no server-side reconciliation exists. The shared builder is also used by the files and SQLite backends, so a fix belongs in `multipart_listing.adb` rather than in one store. A fix needs a maintainer decision on which of the two S3 rules wins: either the page order for a shared key becomes lexical upload ID, which matches the marker but diverges from AWS's stated initiation-time ordering and from the sentence at `backends.ads:2712-2714`, or the marker filter resolves `After_ID` to its initiation time and compares on the same `(Initiated, Upload_ID)` key the page order uses, which matches AWS's ordering but makes the marker meaningful only while the referenced upload still exists — raising the same unknown-cursor policy question already pinned for `ListObjectVersions` at `memory.adb:2687-2690`.

---

### BACKEND-MEMORY-2: Same-key CopyObject with an exact or null source selector (version restore) is rejected as `Invalid_Request`

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1; its confidence was Medium only because AWS acceptance of a versioned self-copy was from memory, and that has now been measured against a real server).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:4977-4986`, the guard that compares source and destination bucket and key and the four directive fields and returns `Invalid_Request` before `Fetch_For_Copy` at `:4988`, never consulting `Options.Source_Selector`; the identical predicate, verbatim, at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:7602-7611` and `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2436-2445`; the server parsing that hands the selector through unaltered at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14080-14101`; the `Copy_Object` contract, which states no self-copy rule at all, at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:1969-2010`; the published claim at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:61` and the requirement at `docs/qualification/copy-object.md:39`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_memory_2.adb`, exit status 1, eight failed expectations, covering the exact-version and null-version restores at the backend interface and the same restore over the in-process S3 application; the control that a same-key copy with no selector is still `Invalid_Request` passes, matching AWS and MinIO.

**What is wrong:** The self-copy guard compares only the source and destination bucket and key and the four directive fields and never looks at the source selector, so the operation S3 clients use to restore a previous version — CopyObject with `x-amz-copy-source: /bucket/key?versionId=OLD` and the same destination bucket and key — is answered `Invalid_Request` and HTTP 400 before the source snapshot is even fetched, and no generation is published. Every version-aware caller hits it: `aws s3api copy-object`, the AWS console's restore-previous-version action, and any lifecycle or rollback tooling built on the same idiom. It is fully deterministic and identical in all three backends, so the published compatibility claim of "retained exact source generations" with a "repeated six-server byte oracle" is not met for this input. Measured on the pinned MinIO release: a same-key copy naming `versionId=<v1>`, `versionId=null` or `versionId=<the current version>` all return 200, while a same-key copy with no version named returns 400 `InvalidRequest` — so the reference server's rule is "reject only when no source version is named", exactly the discriminator this predicate omits.

**Notes:** The MinIO evidence comes from the image digest pinned by `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh`, started by hand as a single detached container and driven with the `aws` CLI; the script itself was not run, so no `alr -n build` was triggered. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour, the case is simply uncovered: `tests/src/copy_object_conformance.adb` uses no selector, and `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:335-345` and `:810-825` copy exact and null sources only to a different bucket and key. A fix is a maintainer decision about the guard's scope rather than a mechanical change: the narrow reading measured on MinIO is "reject only when `Options.Source_Selector.Kind = Current_Version`", which also permits an explicit copy of the current version onto itself, while a stricter reading would additionally reject an exact selector that resolves to the current generation. The choice is externally visible policy and belongs in the `Copy_Object` contract at `backends.ads:1969-2010`. Because the predicate is duplicated verbatim in the files and SQLite backends, one decision must be applied at all three sites together.

---

### BACKEND-MEMORY-3: Backend read contracts cannot report a selected delete marker, so GET/HEAD of a marker are plain `Not_Found`

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2). This is the backend half of SERVER-2, which is the primary finding and carries the P1 severity assigned at the wire level; the backend-interface half is P2 on its own because both cases are wrong diagnostics rather than a wrong success or failure outcome.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:318-329` (`Selected_Object_Index` returns 0 when the selected generation `Is_Delete_Marker`, for every selector kind including `Exact_Version`, and every read goes through it); `Head` at `:1729-1746`, `Attributes` at `:1748-1760` and `Fetch` at `:1562-1566`, all setting an empty record and `Not_Found`; the public types with no channel for the fact at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-52` (`Status`) and `:554-562` (`Object_Information`); the read contracts at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2049-2117`; the information that does exist in the store, `Listed_Version.Is_Delete_Marker` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:98-104`, populated at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:2671`; the tag, legal-hold, retention and attribute surfaces sharing the same blind spot at `:2016-2240` and `:1748-1794`; `GetObjectOutput.DeleteMarker` and `HeadObjectOutput.DeleteMarker` in `review/baseline/service-2.json`.

**Repro:** duplicate of SERVER-2, which is primary; it nonetheless contributes `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_memory_3.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations, and the pinned MinIO oracle. SERVER-2's own evidence is the wire-level `repro_server_2`.

**What is wrong:** `Selected_Object_Index` folds a delete marker into "no object" before any read can see it, and neither `Status` nor `Object_Information` can carry the fact, so the backend interface has no way to tell an adapter that the generation it selected is a marker. A caller cannot distinguish "this key is hidden by a delete marker" from "this key never existed", nor "this exact version is a marker" from "this version ID is unknown", through `Head_Object`, `Get_Object` or `Get_Object_Attributes`. Over HTTP that surfaces as a 404 with no `x-amz-delete-marker: true` for the current read and a 404 instead of a 405 with `Last-Modified` for an exact marker read. Anyone writing version-aware tooling hits it — bucket replication, backup and sync reconciliation, and anything that must reproduce delete markers keys on that header — and it is deterministic. The pinned model states both behaviours verbatim, and the pinned MinIO container answers exactly that way: HEAD of a marker-hidden key returns 404 with `x-amz-delete-marker: true` and `x-amz-version-id: <marker>`, and HEAD and GET of the exact marker version return 405.

**Notes:** This is an interface gap, not a memory-backend coding slip: the listing surface reports `Is_Delete_Marker` correctly (the repro's passing control), so the information exists in the store and only the read contracts cannot carry it. Neither the `.ads` contracts nor `docs/compatibility/s3.md` nor `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:139-140` promises or excludes the S3 behaviour, so this is contract drift against the pinned model rather than an `.ads` violation. An existing test asserts the current behaviour: `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb` requires a simple delete to make marker reads `Not_Found`, so a fix must update that expectation. A fix is a public-interface decision — either a new `Status` value whose `Info` still carries `Modified` and `Version`, or an `Is_Delete_Marker` field on `Object_Information` — and either way it is a visible addition to `src/flyology-object_storage.ads`, must be reflected in the read contracts at `backends.ads:2049-2117`, and must be implemented identically in the files and SQLite backends. See SERVER-2 for the wire-level disposition.

---

## BACKEND-FILES: pure-files backend, durability bridge and secure erase (BACKEND-FILES-1 .. BACKEND-FILES-6)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-durability.ad?`, `src/flyology_object_storage_durability.c`, `src/flyology_object_storage_secure_erase.c` and `docs/architecture/files-layout.md`, with `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ad?` for the contracts, `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb` as the reference oracle, and the admission bounds, status mapping and annotation routing in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` read to establish reachability.

### BACKEND-FILES-1: CompleteMultipartUpload in an unversioned bucket inherits the replaced object's annotations and orphans the previous annotation sidecar

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:11810-11811` and `:11854`, where `Complete_Multipart_Upload` targets the fixed `Object_Path` for `Versioning_Unconfigured` and calls `Write_Header` with no `Annotation_Generation_ID`; `Write_Header`'s trailing field at `:2340-2350` and `:2493-2494`; the read-back into `Info.Version` at `:2811-2827`; the path-derived fallback `Stored_Annotation_Generation_ID` at `:2873-2907`, specifically `:2892-2896`; sidecar resolution at `:891-909`; the ordinary PUT path that mints a fresh identity and removes the replaced sidecar at `:7093-7099`, `:7128-7132`, `:7247-7249` and `:7388-7392`; the versioned generation file name at `:11812-11843`; `Delete_Bucket`'s sweep at `:3575-3875`; the reference backend's `Annotations.Clear` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:3345`; contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2288-2290`; annotation routing at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15622`, `:15653`, `:15683` and `:15736`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_1.adb` with helper `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_support.ad?`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** Multipart completion writes its record without an annotation generation identity, so the persisted field is empty and the sidecar identity falls back to `SHA256(object path)`; in an unversioned bucket every generation of a key lives at the same fixed path, so two completions in a row on one key share one annotation sidecar. Annotations acknowledged against the first completed object are then served, byte for byte and with their original ETag, as annotations of the second completed object's bytes, and completion never removes the replaced generation's sidecar the way ordinary PUT does, so a completion that replaces a PUT-created generation leaves a `generation-*` directory no later request can read or delete. `Delete_Bucket` inspects only `objects/`, `versions/`, `multipart/` and `configuration/`, so the leak is permanent for the life of the root. Both effects are deterministic and need no concurrency; any client that combines annotations with multipart uploads on an unversioned bucket hits them. The reference memory backend clears the annotation map on completion (`memory.adb:3345`), so the intended contract is an empty annotation set on the newly published generation and the two backends disagree.

**Notes:** The verifier corrected one sub-claim of the discovery scenario: an ordinary PUT, annotate, then one completion does **not** expose the PUT generation's annotations through the API, because the PUT minted its own generation identity; that sequence produces only the orphan, and inheritance needs two completions in a row. The defect is confined to `Versioning_Unconfigured`, because a versioned publication's file name carries the publication number and so yields a distinct path-derived identity. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour and none covers the combination: `tests/src/multipart_part_conformance.adb` has no annotation case and `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` has no files-backend annotation-plus-multipart sequence. A fix needs one maintainer decision: whether completion should mint a fresh annotation generation identity the way PUT does and remove the previous sidecar, or whether the path-derived legacy fallback should be retired for records written by current code, which changes the on-disk contract for FOSOBJ01-06 records. BACKEND-FILES-4 is the other half of the annotation-lifecycle problem (removal ordering), and BACKEND-FILES-6 shows the versioned path's own identity reuse.

---

### BACKEND-FILES-2: Valid object keys beyond 464 bytes are unusable on the files backend and are reported as a retryable 503

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:835-859` (`Encoded_Object_Path`, two hex characters per key byte, a new component every 100 characters); `:217-239` (`Ensure_Directory` through `Ada.Directories.Create_Directory` on absolute paths) and `:7443-7457` (the `when others` handler that maps the resulting `Use_Error` to `Backend_Unavailable`); the depth guard `Maximum_Object_Path_Depth = 32` at `:113`; key contract at `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-489` and `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:667-670`; status mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`; documentation at `docs/architecture/files-layout.md:4-6`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_2.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation; the program also binary-searches and prints the measured ceiling.

**What is wrong:** The files backend expands every key byte to two hexadecimal characters and inserts a path separator every 100 characters, so a key of N bytes costs roughly 2.02N path bytes below `buckets/<name>/objects/`, while `Valid_Object_Key` admits 1,024 bytes. macOS enforces `PATH_MAX` = 1024 on every path-taking syscall, so `mkdir` on an intermediate component fails `ENAMETOOLONG`, `Create_Directory` raises `Use_Error`, and the blanket handler reports `Backend_Unavailable`, which the S3 adapter renders as 503 `SlowDown` — a status aws-cli and boto3 retry with backoff, so the request never completes and never reports a permanent error. Measured on this host (`getconf PATH_MAX /` = 1024, 49-byte root): 464 bytes is the largest accepted key and 465 already fails, matching the arithmetic exactly, so the ceiling scales with the root path length and is deployment-dependent rather than documented. HEAD, GET and DELETE of such a key fail identically, so an object written on Linux (limit 4096) becomes unreadable if the same root is opened on macOS, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs as a qualified platform. The reference memory backend accepts the same 1,024-byte key (repro control), and the pinned model's `ObjectKey` shape expresses no maximum, so the crate's own documented 1,024-byte bound is what the repro asserts against.

**Notes:** No files-specific key-length limit exists in `docs/architecture/files-layout.md`, `docs/compatibility/s3.md` or the backend's own `.ads`; `files-layout.md:4-6` bounds each component, not the total path. The depth guard never fires, because a 1,024-byte key needs 21 components. No existing test puts a key longer than a few dozen bytes into a `Files.Store`. A fix needs a maintainer decision on externally visible policy: either a documented files-backend key-length limit reported as `Invalid_Request` or `Capacity_Exceeded` rather than the retryable `Backend_Unavailable`, or a path encoding that bounds total path length (for example digest-prefixed sharding), which changes the on-disk layout. AWS S3's own 1,024-byte key limit was recorded by the verifier as from memory and unverified; it is not load-bearing, since the crate's own contract supplies the bound.

---

### BACKEND-FILES-3: Singleton bucket-configuration readers size a stack local from the stored document, so a 3 MiB acknowledged policy kills the reading process

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1; the verifier raised it because the reader allocates a frame past the end of a 2 MiB task stack — an unchecked stack overflow — and the observed effect is abrupt process termination, SIGKILL with shell status 137, which on a server takes down every concurrent request rather than failing the one that asked).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1579-1583` (`Read_String` declares `Result : String (1 .. Length)`, a dynamically sized local; only the 64 KiB `Data` buffer is chunked); the seven readers with that shape — bucket policy `:1780`/`:1795`, CORS `:1809`/`:1824`, encryption `:1839`/`:1857`, ownership controls `:1872`/`:1890`, lifecycle `:1908`/`:1930`, logging `:1947`/`:1965`, notification `:1980`/`:1998`; the already-chunked writer and readers at `:1478-1504` and `:1610-1631`; `Get_Bucket_Policy`'s `when others` handler at `:6258-6270`; admission bound `Maximum_Bucket_Policy_Bytes` = 16 MiB at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2792` and `Valid_Bucket_Policy` at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.adb:199-200`; request-body sizing at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:176-187`; toolchain `s-parame.adb:55-66` (2 MiB `Default_Stack_Size`); flyology 0.1.1 `src/flyology-io-structured_servers.adb:386-391` (the HTTP `Worker` task declares no `Storage_Size`).

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_3.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation; the child process reading the 3 MiB policy from a default-`Storage_Size` task is reported by `/bin/sh` as status 137 (SIGKILL), while the 1 MiB child control succeeds with status 0.

**What is wrong:** Reading a bucket policy, CORS, encryption, ownership-controls, lifecycle, logging or notification document needs as many contiguous bytes of the calling task's primary stack as the document has, while the writer streams the same document in 64 KiB chunks and admission allows 16 MiB. Any document larger than the reading task's stack is therefore writable, acknowledged with 200, durable on disk and unreadable afterwards. Because Flyology's HTTP worker task declares no `Storage_Size`, every server worker reads with GNAT's 2 MiB default, so the practical ceiling is roughly 2 MiB. The failure is not graceful: the oversized frame skips past the task's guard page and the process is killed with no Ada exception and no diagnostic, so a single `GetBucketPolicy` for an already-accepted configuration terminates the server for every client. It is fully deterministic, and the repro's environment-task control shows the bytes on disk are intact, so this is a read-path defect rather than a corruption.

**Notes:** The failure mode a caller sees depends on which task issues the read: from the environment task with headroom the same read succeeds, and with a 3 MiB local already on that stack it returns `Backend_Unavailable` instead — the same defect, not a separate one. That the 16 MiB documented bound also exceeds this host's 8 MiB process stack is an inference from the measured stack sizes, not a separate measurement. Existing coverage does not reach it: `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:1524-1579` round-trips 16 MiB only through replication, which uses the chunked `Read_Unbounded_String`, and the policy and CORS cases at `:583-657` and `:702-766` use short strings. The fix is mechanical — route these seven readers through `Read_Unbounded_String`, as the metadata, replication, website and named-configuration readers already do — and needs no new maintainer decision unless the maintainer also wants a smaller documented bound for these documents. If a maintainer's rubric treats an unchecked stack overflow as availability-only, this is still at minimum P1, because an acknowledged configuration can never be read back.

---

### BACKEND-FILES-4: A failed DeleteObject destroys the object's acknowledged annotations while leaving the object in place

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1; the verifier raised it because acknowledged payload data is destroyed by an operation that reports failure and did not delete its target, with no request having asked for that deletion — data loss under the rubric's first clause rather than a wrong result).
