**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:8430-8435` (unversioned current delete) and `:8419-8424` (exact-version delete), both calling `Remove_Annotation_Generation` before `Remove_Path`; `Remove_Annotation_Generation` through `Remove_Tree` at `:1034-1046` and `:338-346`, where `Validate_Tree_Symlink_Free`, `Delete_Tree` and `Sync_Directory` make the removal durable before the unlink is attempted; `Remove_Path`'s `Data_Error` at `:8320-8331`; the batch handler that reports the failure at `:8517-8541`; the same ordering on the PUT path at `:7388-7395`; annotation payload identity at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:571-577`; status mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`; documentation at `docs/qualification/delete-object.md:46-51`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_4.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; the injection makes `buckets/<b>/objects/` mode `0500` so the sidecar removal succeeds and the unlink fails, and the uninjected control key deletes cleanly.

**What is wrong:** `Delete_One` destroys the object's annotation sidecar, including an fsync of the sidecar's parent directory, before it issues the unlink that is the delete's publication point. If the unlink fails — EACCES on a directory that lost write permission, EPERM after a read-only remount, EIO, or `Data_Error` because a concurrent external change made the path something other than an ordinary file — the batch is reported as `Backend_Unavailable` (503 `SlowDown` at the S3 boundary), the object is still there and still readable, and the annotations the client stored and the server acknowledged are gone, with no request having asked for their deletion, no recovery path, and no diagnostic distinguishing this from an ordinary transient failure. Annotations are independent payloads with their own size, ETag and checksum, so nothing can re-derive them. The reproduction is fully deterministic.

**Notes:** The verifier confirmed the deterministic no-crash variant, which is the strongest one. The crash variant follows from the same ordering plus the fsync in `Remove_Tree` — a power loss between the two steps leaves exactly this state on any filesystem — and is verified by reading only, because the crash corpus lives in `tests/src/files_crash_probe.adb` and `tests/scripts/test.sh`, which this review may not modify; the deterministic crash matrix there contains no annotation scenario at all, so it is not gated. The btrfs-specific PUT-path window from the discovery report remains unverifiable on this APFS host. `docs/qualification/delete-object.md:46-51` documents that a status after publication is not proof the object deletion did not occur; it says nothing about destroying a different acknowledged payload when the deletion demonstrably did not occur. The fix should be a plain reordering — unlink first, then sweep the sidecar, with an orphan sweep for the crash case — and needs no new public constant or API, but the maintainer must decide whether the orphan sweep belongs in `Open`, in `Delete_Bucket`, or both. The orphan half is shared with BACKEND-FILES-1.

---

### BACKEND-FILES-5: Multipart operations with a valid upload ID but a different key answer `Backend_Unavailable` instead of `Not_Found`

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3412-3446` (`Read_Manifest` locates the manifest by bucket and upload ID only, then calls `Read_Header` with the requested key); `:3070-3087` (`Read_Header` raises `Ada.IO_Exceptions.Data_Error` on a stored-key mismatch); the key-blind presence tests at `:10829-10837` (UploadPart), `:11114-11122` (ListParts), `:11611-11618` (Complete) and `:11980-11986` (Abort); the blanket handlers at `:11061-11078` and their three twins; the opaque upload-ID derivation at `:10690-10694`; contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:2657-2659` and `:2771-2773`; the reference behaviour at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:332-346`; status mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_5.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations, one per entry point; the memory backend runs the identical sequence first as the control and answers `Not_Found` each time.

**What is wrong:** The files backend resolves an active multipart upload by upload ID alone — the upload directory is named `SHA256 (Upload_ID)`, independent of the key — and discovers the key mismatch only when `Read_Header` validates the manifest's stored key, which it signals with `Data_Error`; every multipart entry point's blanket `when others` handler turns that into `Backend_Unavailable`, rendered as 503 `SlowDown`. A client that mistypes or mangles a key on a retry therefore gets a retryable server-overload response where AWS and the reference memory backend return a definitive 404 `NoSuchUpload`; aws-cli and boto3 retry 503 with exponential backoff, so the request loop terminates only when the retry budget is exhausted, and an `AbortMultipartUpload` sent with the wrong key can never retire the upload nor tell the client why. It is deterministic. The backend contract states that upload identifiers are scoped to the exact bucket and key and that a missing upload is `Not_Found`, so this is a documented-contract violation as well as a cross-backend divergence.

**Notes:** It needs a client mistake to trigger, which is why it stays an edge case, but the consequence is a wrong diagnostic that turns a permanent error into an indefinitely retried one. No existing test asserts either behaviour; `tests/src/multipart_part_conformance.adb` never uses a mismatched key and makes no `Not_Found` assertion at all. A fix is a key comparison before `Read_Manifest`, or mapping the manifest key check's `Data_Error` to `Not_Found`, and needs no new maintainer decision.

---

### BACKEND-FILES-6: Deleting the newest generation lets the next PUT reuse the deleted generation's opaque version ID

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2).

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:1254-1270` (`Next_Generation_Publication` returns `max(publication) + 1` from a scan of the retained generations, so the counter is not monotonic across deletions); the version-ID derivation at `:7288-7297`, repeated for delete markers at `:8246-8250` and for multipart completion at `:11821-11829`; the exact delete that unlinks the generation file outright at `:8419-8424`; the null-tombstone path that does not apply at `:8400-8418`; the annotation identity that does not collide at `:7093-7099`; contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads:50-52` and the backend note at `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:47-49`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_files_6.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; the memory backend runs the identical sequence first as the control and mints a different identifier.

**What is wrong:** The opaque version ID is a pure function of the publication number, and an exact delete removes the generation file with no residue that keeps the counter high, so deleting the newest generation (or every generation above some N) and republishing mints the deleted generation's exact version ID again. `GET key?versionId=<that id>` then returns the new bytes with status 200 instead of 404, so a client, replicator or audit consumer keyed on (key, version ID) silently reads different content under an identifier it believes it has already seen or already deleted. The memory backend uses a monotonic publication counter and never repeats, so the two backends disagree on an externally visible identity. The effect is deterministic, and the repro confirms the second-order consequence empirically, not only the identifier collision.

**Notes:** It stays P2 because it needs an exact delete of the newest generation followed by a republish, but the consequence is stronger than "two versions share an ID". Existing coverage only asserts that three consecutive publications differ (`tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:544-549`); no test deletes the newest generation and republishes. S3's own uniqueness of version identifiers per object version was recorded by the verifier as from memory and unverified; the cross-backend divergence does not depend on it. A fix needs a maintainer decision because it changes the on-disk layout or record format: persist a per-key monotonic publication high-water mark, leave a tombstone for exact deletes the way null deletes already do, or mix an entropy source into the version-ID digest, which would end the current property that the identifier is reproducible from the generation file name.

---

## BACKEND-SQLITE: the SQLite backend crate, its catalog, and its durability shim (BACKEND-SQLITE-1 .. BACKEND-SQLITE-5)

The area covers `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.ad?`, `-sqlite-catalogs.ad?`, `-sqlite-databases.ad?`, `-sqlite-c.ads` and `sqlite/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_durability.c`, against the backend contract in `src/flyology-object_storage-backends.ads`, with `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb` and `-backends-files.adb` read as cross-backend controls, `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` read for the status mapping, and `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md`, `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md`, the SQLite suite and `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh` read for the claimed coverage. The reproduction programs live in the second harness, `sqlite/tests/repros/src/`, whose smoke program `repro_sqlite_smoke` passes with exit status 0. One defect confirmed in another area is also present here and is not counted twice: the same-key copy predicate of BACKEND-MEMORY-2, which rejects a versioned self-copy as `Invalid_Request` without consulting `Options.Source_Selector`, is duplicated verbatim at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2436-2445`, so a fix has to change this backend as well as the memory one.

### BACKEND-SQLITE-1: `Get_Object` opens the payload after the catalog gate is released, so a concurrent overwrite turns a valid GET into 503 SlowDown

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery said P2 with Medium confidence, assuming the window is a few instructions wide and that stock clients retry the 503; the verifier raised it because the measured rate under ordinary reader concurrency is about twelve failed GETs per overwrite rather than a rare edge, and because the code breaks an invariant `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23` states).

**Location:** `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2666-2683`, where `Get_Object` calls `Catalogs.Find_Selected_Object` with no `Check` argument, then evaluates read conditions, then calls `SIO.Open`; the overload that releases the gate before returning at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:5688-5716`; the hook's documented purpose at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.ads:707-712`; the two sibling readers that do pass it, `Head_Object` at `backends-sqlite.adb:2581-2583` and `Copy_Object` at `:2448-2451`; the immediate unlink of the replaced payload at `:2232-2235`; the `when others` that produces the status at `:2736-2745`; the same unprotected window in the part readers at `:4067-4072` and `:4295-4301`; the S3 mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1951-1954`; the invariant the code does not keep at `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23`.

**Repro:** `sqlite/tests/repros/src/repro_sqlite_1.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Get_Object` resolves the payload name under the catalog operation gate and opens the file after the gate has been released, while a concurrent `Put_Object` on the same key unlinks the superseded payload as soon as it leaves the gate. The reader's `SIO.Open` then raises `Name_Error`, the `when others` handler turns it into `Backend_Unavailable`, and the S3 boundary answers 503 `SlowDown` for a key that exists before and after the request. The program opens a SQLite store on a fresh scratch root, seeds one key, and runs two 20 s phases of 32 reader tasks against 2 writer tasks that all publish the same bytes, so the entity tag never changes and every GET is required to succeed. Phase A reads with no conditions: 316,407 GETs, 294,126 `SUCCESS` and 22,281 `BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE`, that is 7.0 %, against 1,822 PUTs that all returned `Success`. Phase B repeats it with a 14,284-byte matching `If-Match` list, the only work `Get_Object` performs inside the unprotected window: 336,671 GETs, 317,508 `SUCCESS` and 19,163 `BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE` against 1,792 PUTs, which shows the dominant factor is reader scheduling rather than the window's instruction count. Zero `NOT_FOUND` was recorded in either phase, so the catalog lookup always succeeded and only the file open failed, and the single-task control GET with the same arguments succeeds.

**Notes:** Not P0 because nothing is lost, no request is served with wrong bytes (the size check at `backends-sqlite.adb:2679-2681` and the immutable payload names prevent that), and 503 is retriable. The fix needs no interface change: `Head_Object` and `Copy_Object` already pass the `Check` hook for exactly this purpose and an open POSIX descriptor survives unlink, so giving `Get_Object` and the two part readers the same hook closes the window. The behaviour also contradicts `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23`, "Superseded and deleted bodies are intentionally retained until that recovery pass so an already-open reader is never invalidated by replacement", so either the code or that sentence has to change; this is discovery observation O5, confirmed and promoted into the finding rather than left as free-standing drift. No existing test asserts the wrong behaviour, because `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:1365-1518` races only writes against writes.

---

### BACKEND-SQLITE-2: the annotation copy lease is released below its own `CHECK(refs>0)`, so the first CopyObject of an annotated source raises after the destination has been committed

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery also said P1).

**Location:** `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:2842-2846`, where the TEMP table is created as `annotation_copy_leases(payload TEXT PRIMARY KEY, refs INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(refs>0)) WITHOUT ROWID`; the insert at `:846-856` (`VALUES(?1,1)` with `ON CONFLICT(payload) DO UPDATE SET refs=refs+1`); the release at `:874-892` (`UPDATE ... SET refs=refs-1 WHERE payload=?1 AND refs>0` followed by an unreachable `DELETE ... WHERE payload=?1 AND refs=0`); the unconditional annotation read at `:7331-7347`; the reclamation that counts the table at `:9150-9166`; the only caller of that overload and its lease handling at `backends-sqlite.adb:2448-2451`, `:2459`, `:2520-2521`, `:2532-2541`, with `Release_Annotations` at `:2378-2392`; the raise at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-databases.adb:398-400`; the build flags that contain no CHECK suppression at `sqlite/flyology_object_storage_sqlite.gpr:19-36`; the S3 dispatch at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:14555` and the catch-all that renders 500 at `:18056-18065`.

**Repro:** `sqlite/tests/repros/src/repro_sqlite_2.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** the temporary lease table that keeps a source annotation payload alive across a copy declares `CHECK(refs>0)` but releases with `SET refs=refs-1`, so the very first release of a lease taken at 1 violates the constraint and `Step` raises `SQLite_Error` with "CHECK constraint failed: refs>0". `Release_Annotations` clears `Annotation_Leases_Held` only after the fallible call returns, so the raise leaves the flag set, `Copy_Object`'s own `when others` handler re-enters the release and raises inside itself, and the `SQLite_Error` escapes `Copy_Object` entirely; the S3 boundary answers 500 `InternalError`. Every CopyObject of an annotated source is affected, deterministically, on the first copy of that source in a process, and in both copy directives: the program reproduces it with the default directive and again with `Exclude_Annotations`, which the discovery predicted but did not demonstrate. The destination has already been committed at the point of the raise, which the program shows by reading the copied annotation and heading the copied object successfully after the exception, so a client sees 500 for a copy that actually succeeded, and a retry against a versioning-enabled bucket publishes a second destination generation. A second copy of the same source succeeds, because the lease then goes 1 to 2 to 1, which is what masks the defect; an unannotated control copy in the same program succeeds, which is why the maintained copy tests pass.

**Notes:** Not P0: nothing is lost or corrupted and the client's retry succeeds. Two side effects matter for the fix. The `DELETE ... WHERE refs=0` immediately after the update is dead code, because the CHECK makes `refs=0` unreachable, so the intended reference-count lifecycle cannot complete even once; and the stranded `refs=1` row keeps `Delete_Payload_If_Unreferenced` at `catalogs.adb:9150-9166` from ever reclaiming that annotation payload until the process restarts. There is no SQLite annotation coverage at all, which is BACKEND-SQLITE-5, so no existing test asserts the wrong behaviour; the memory and pure-files backends are covered by `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19555-19696`, driven at `:19734` and `:19743`. A fix needs a maintainer decision on the intended lease lifecycle, either dropping the `CHECK` and keeping the delete-at-zero, or making the release a `DELETE ... WHERE refs=1` and `UPDATE ... WHERE refs>1` pair, and should separately clear `Annotation_Leases_Held` before the fallible call so the handler cannot re-enter it. This defect is also what makes BACKEND-SQLITE-3's release path fail today, though BACKEND-SQLITE-3 stands independently of it.

---

### BACKEND-SQLITE-3: a failed lease release inside `Find_Selected_Object`'s exception handler skips `Gate.Release` and leaks the catalog gate forever

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery also said P0). The store stops answering every operation, on every bucket, permanently, until the process is killed.

**Location:** `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:7349-7361`, where the handler runs `Release_Annotation_Copy_Leases_Internal (Item, Annotations)` before `Item.Gate.Release`; the fallible release at `:864-893`, which raises whenever the update does not change exactly one row and always for a lease at 1; `Operation_Gate` at `:918-928`, an `entry Acquire when not Held` with a plain `procedure Release`, no owner tracking, no timeout and no controlled unlock; the `Item.Gate.Acquire` that begins every catalog operation, for example `:3348`, `:5701`, `:5734`, `:7331` and `:9148`; the gate-safe sibling `Release_Annotation_Copy_Snapshot` at `:7363-7379`; the non-leasing overload that `Get_Object` and `Head_Object` use at `:5688-5716`; `Open_Source` at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2394-2416` and `Check_Context` at `:164-177`, which raise `Operation_Cancelled`, `Timeout_Error` or `Data_Error`; the only caller of the lease-taking overload at `:2448-2451`.

**Repro:** two programs. `sqlite/tests/repros/src/repro_sqlite_3.adb` is the deterministic mechanism: it seeds a key, attaches one annotation, confirms `Head_Bucket` works, removes the source body file so `Open_Source` raises `Data_Error` under the gate, calls `Copy_Object`, and then probes `Head_Bucket` from a second task with a bounded 5 s wait; exit status 1, one failed expectation. `sqlite/tests/repros/src/repro_sqlite_3b.adb` is the caller-reachable trigger with no tampering: the same setup with 200 annotations attached purely to widen the interval between `Copy_Object`'s entry `Check_Context` and the `Open_Source` callback, and the `Deadline` argument alone as the trigger, scanned upward in 3 µs steps (attempts rejected at the entry check raise `Timeout_Error`, take no leases and are harmless, so the scan is safe); it stops at a 6 µs deadline after 1 attempt rejected at the entry check, and the probe again does not return within 5 s; exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** the release that runs before the unlock is fallible, so a raise there skips `Item.Gate.Release` and `Held` stays `True` for the life of the process. Every subsequent catalog call, on any bucket, then blocks in the protected entry with no deadline and no cancellation, including `Close`, so an S3 server on this backend stops answering every request and cannot even close the store; re-opening cannot recover it from the same process. The caller-reachable sequence is `Copy_Object`, the only caller of the lease-taking overload, on a source generation carrying at least one annotation, where the `Open_Source` callback raises. `Open_Source` raises when the caller's token is cancelled, when the caller's deadline has passed, or when the source body file is missing, symlinked or mis-sized. Cancellation and deadline are plain arguments of the public `Copy_Object` profile, so an ordinary S3 CopyObject whose client aborts, or whose request deadline expires while the request waits for the gate, wedges the whole backend; `repro_sqlite_3b` demonstrates exactly that through the public `Backend` interface with nothing but a caller-supplied deadline. `Get_Object` and `Head_Object` cannot reach the leaking handler: they use the overload at `catalogs.adb:5688-5716`, which takes no leases and whose handler releases the gate unconditionally, so a cancelled or timed-out GET of an annotated object is not enough.

**Notes:** the amplifier is the unconditional gate entry the discovery lists as observation O9 (`catalogs.adb:918-928`): `Gate.Acquire` ignores the caller's `Token` and `Deadline` while waiting, so under contention a request's deadline can expire inside the gate-held window, which is what makes the timed-out-copy trigger easy to hit; on its own O9 is only latency, but a fix for this P0 should consider it. Discovery observation O3, the full-bucket listing scans that hold the gate, compounds it for the same reason. Two secondary effects are visible in the runs: the timed-out `Copy_Object` reports `Backend_Unavailable` instead of propagating `Timeout_Error`, because the SQLite exception replaced the original one before `Copy_Object`'s `when Operation_Cancelled | Timeout_Error => raise` handler could see it, and the store is unrecoverable in-process. Independently of BACKEND-SQLITE-2's constraint violation, any SQLite failure in the release has the same effect, because the handler puts fallible work before the unlock. No existing test covers it: the SQLite suite performs no annotation operation and no test asserts that the store survives a failed `Copy_Object`. A fix needs the unlock to run first, or under a controlled object, plus the separate BACKEND-SQLITE-2 lease repair; whether `Gate.Acquire` should honour the caller's deadline is a maintainer decision.

---

### BACKEND-SQLITE-4: `Delete_Bucket` reports `Backend_Unavailable` for a bucket whose keys are all delete markers

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery also said P1).

**Location:** `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-sqlite-catalogs.adb:3352-3374`, where the emptiness test is `EXISTS(... FROM buckets ...)`, `EXISTS(... FROM objects ...)` and `EXISTS(... FROM multipart_uploads ...)` and `object_versions` is never consulted; the restricting foreign key at `:387` (`FOREIGN KEY(bucket_name) REFERENCES buckets(name) ON DELETE RESTRICT`); the marker publication that removes the `objects` mirror row and returns early at `:6271-6279` and `:6317-6319`; the transaction and `Safe_Rollback` at `:3385-3393`; the wrapper that re-raises only `Operation_Cancelled` and `Timeout_Error` and turns everything else into `Backend_Unavailable` at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:386-406`; the documented status at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:21,45` ("Bucket still contains objects"); the two backends that get it right, `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3846-3852` and `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-memory.adb:578-600` with markers retained at `backends-memory.adb:1957`; the S3 mappings at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1947-1950` (409 `BucketNotEmpty`) and `:1951-1954` (503 `SlowDown`); the existing assertions at `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:7660-7663` and `:1503-1518`.

**Repro:** `sqlite/tests/repros/src/repro_sqlite_4.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** `Catalogs.Delete_Bucket` decides emptiness from the current-object mirror and the multipart table only, but `object_versions` holds retained generations and restricts the bucket row. Any versioning-enabled or suspended bucket whose keys have all been deleted through DeleteObject therefore has no mirror rows, passes the emptiness test, and then trips the foreign key inside the transaction; the SQLite error is rewrapped as `Backend_Unavailable` and the server answers 503 `SlowDown` rather than the 409 `BucketNotEmpty` that the defined status exists to produce. Every client that enables versioning, deletes its objects the ordinary way, and then tries to delete the bucket sees an indefinite retry loop instead of a definitive answer telling it to remove the versions first. The failure is deterministic. The program runs one class-wide procedure over the identical sequence, `Create_Bucket`, `Put_Bucket_Versioning (Versioning_Enabled)`, `Put_Object`, `Delete_Object`, `Head_Object`, `Delete_Bucket`, first against the memory store as a control and then against a SQLite store on a fresh root: the memory control returns `BUCKET_NOT_EMPTY` and SQLite returns `BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE`.

**Notes:** Not P0: nothing is deleted, because the transaction rolls back, so the defect is the reported status rather than durability. The memory and pure-files backends both count retained generations, so the cross-backend expectation is unambiguous and no maintainer decision is needed beyond adding `EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM object_versions WHERE bucket_name=?1)` to the emptiness query; the discovery's supporting claim that AWS answers 409 `BucketNotEmpty` here was recorded as from memory and unverified, and the finding does not rest on it. The existing SQLite `Bucket_Not_Empty` assertion at `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb:7660-7663` asserts the status only with a current data object, and the versioning race test at `:1503-1518` permanently removes the generation before its `Delete_Bucket` cleanup, so the marker-only path is untested and no test asserts the wrong behaviour.

---

### BACKEND-SQLITE-5: the annotation qualification record claims SQLite corpus evidence that does not exist

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery also said P2). Contract and qualification drift: the document overstates the evidence, and the gap it hides is precisely where BACKEND-SQLITE-2 and BACKEND-SQLITE-3 live.

**Location:** `docs/qualification/object-annotations-backend-server.md:26-27` ("Memory, pure-files, and SQLite implement the same interface.") and `:45-47` ("The maintained backend and server corpora exercise payload, checksum, version, list, missing, condition, and copy-directive behavior across the implementations."); `sqlite/tests/src/flyology_object_storage_sqlite_tests.adb`, which has zero matches for `Put_Object_Annotation`, `Get_Object_Annotation`, `Delete_Object_Annotation` or `List_Object_Annotations`, its only `annotation` matches being migration DDL fixtures at `:551-963` and `:1861-1872` and `Empty_Vector` arguments at `:3397-3398` and `:3439-3440`; `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb`, which has zero matches for `annotation` and which `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh:39-41,111-122` shows to be the only corpus run against `s3_sqlite_server`; the class-wide `Exercise_Object_Annotations` at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:19555-19696` with its copy-directive assertions at `:19661-19675`, driven at `:19734` and `:19743`; `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md`, which does not mention annotations at all.

**Repro:** none. This is a documentation finding with no program; it was verified with `rg` and by reading the cited sources, as the area brief allows.

**What is wrong:** the qualification record presents annotation behaviour as corpus-verified "across the implementations" while naming SQLite as one of them, but no SQLite-backed test performs any annotation operation, and the only black-box corpus run against `s3_sqlite_server` has no annotation coverage. A reader auditing the record would conclude that SQLite annotation copy is exercised, while BACKEND-SQLITE-2 and BACKEND-SQLITE-3 show that the first CopyObject of any annotated SQLite object fails and can wedge the store.

**Notes:** one correction to the discovery, which said annotation behaviour is asserted only on the memory backend: it is exercised against two, because `Exercise_Object_Annotations` is defined over `Backends.Backend'Class` and driven against both the memory store and the pure-files store. The substantive claim survives, because that suite lives in the root crate and the SQLite suite is a separate crate that does not call it, so the exerciser is never driven against a SQLite store. The cheapest repair is to run the existing class-wide `Exercise_Object_Annotations` against a SQLite store from the SQLite suite, which would have caught both of the annotation defects above; if that is not wanted, the sentence at `:45-47` should name the implementations it actually covers.

---

## CLIENT-LOW: the low-level prepared S3 operations and the generic REST/XML read and mutation families (CLIENT-LOW-1 .. CLIENT-LOW-7)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads` and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb`, including an enumeration of all 110 `Execute_*` bodies and the 116 modeled operations they serve, read against the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json` (`HeadObjectOutput`, `GetObjectAttributesOutput`, `PutObjectTaggingOutput`, `GetObjectTaggingOutput`, `DeleteObjectTaggingOutput`, `CreateSessionRequest`, `MaxKeys` and the tagging `responseCode`s), `docs/compatibility/s3.md` (300-380, 440-460, 720-730) and `docs/architecture/composable-client-operations.md`. The supporting units read were `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-errors.adb:100-130`, `-s3-tagging.ads`, `-s3-deletions.ads:53-67`, `-s3-object_reads.adb:75-100`, `-client-bounded_rest_xml_reads.adb:95-125`, `-client.ads:37-51` and `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:590-630`, with the in-repo server's version-header emission sites at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` (4188-4194, 15145-15165, 15790-15860, 16330-16360, 17420-17440). Coverage was inspected in `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1120-1185` and `:1617-1861`, and `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb` (2527-2540, 4325-4400, 15080-15260). The eight programs live in `tests/repros/src/` and reuse the read-only loopback helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb` written by the CLIENT-OBJECTS verifier, unmodified; nothing tracked was changed and no Docker server was started, so the live-server evidence for CLIENT-LOW-1 and CLIENT-LOW-3 is the pre-existing capture in `review/baseline-rustfs.md` and `review/baseline/{minio,seaweedfs}.log`. Two of the seven findings are the same predicates one layer up and are counted in CLIENT-OBJECTS as well as here: CLIENT-LOW-1 is the twin of CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-155`) and CLIENT-LOW-3 is the twin of CLIENT-OBJECTS-2 (`Tagging_Version_ID`, `client-objects.adb:7177-7211`); the composable `Client.Objects` GetObjectAttributes operation calls the same decoder CLIENT-LOW-2 lives in, so that one needs no twin.

### CLIENT-LOW-1: `Execute_Head_Object` raises `Invalid_Response` when a 200 omits `x-amz-version-id` after a `versionId=` request

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1). The verifier raised it on the rubric reading it states in full for all three version-echo findings: the P0 interop clause is about interoperability failure rather than about which side does the refusing, and here the library cannot complete a version-selected HEAD against any conforming S3 server on the default bucket state, the pinned model marks the demanded header optional, and the failure escapes as an exception rather than as a variant of `Head_Object_Outcome`, so a caller cannot even degrade gracefully. The strict alternative the verifier recorded and rejected: read literally, "an interop-breaking protocol violation that a stock client or server rejects" describes the library emitting bytes a stock peer refuses, and here the direction is reversed, so the finding would fall back to P1 ("unexpected exception on valid input via a reachable path").

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658` (`Head_Object_Response_Bound`), returning `(Requested_Version'Length = 0 or else US.To_String (Value.Version_ID) = Requested_Version) and then (Returned_Payer /= "requester" or else Requested_Payer = "requester")` — plain equality with no absence tolerance; the decoder that leaves the value empty when the header is absent at `:5813` (`Version_ID => H ("x-amz-version-id")`, where `H` is `Flyology.HTTP.Client.Header`); the raise site at `:5877-5887` in `Execute_Head_Object` (`Invalid_Response with "HeadObject response does not match prepared request"`); `Prepare_Head_Object` at `:5153`, which copies `Parameters.Version_ID` into `Result.Requested_Head_Object_Version_ID` unconditionally and never rejects `"null"`; the private-part comment that states the intent at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:10533-10535`; the contract `Invalid_Response` claims to enforce at `.ads:300-301`; the in-repo server's fallback at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4188-4194`; the conformance corpus case at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1144-1172`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_1.adb` with helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** `Head_Object_Response_Bound` compares the decoded `x-amz-version-id` against the retained request selector with plain string equality, and an absent response header decodes to the empty string. Any request that carried a `versionId` — including the AWS-documented `null` selector, which is how an object in a bucket where versioning was never enabled is addressed — therefore makes the predicate False and turns an otherwise valid 200 into `Invalid_Response`. The pinned model settles the response side: `HeadObjectOutput.required` is `[]` and `VersionId` is `{"location": "header", "locationName": "x-amz-version-id"}` with no presence requirement, so an omitting 200 does not violate the modeled contract `.ads:300-301` says the exception guards. Because the failure is an exception rather than an outcome variant, the caller never sees the metadata and cannot classify the result as a per-server deviation — which is exactly why no `*_ORACLE_MODE` in the implementation corpus can express it. Anyone doing a version-selected HEAD against MinIO, RustFS, SeaweedFS or AWS on a never-versioned bucket hits it, deterministically. The program scripts two exchanges on a loopback origin: the first replies to `HEAD /never-versioned/native-object?versionId=null` with the verbatim RustFS 1.0.0-rc.3 header set from `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 1 probe (b) — `200 OK`, `accept-ranges`, `content-length: 64`, `content-type`, quoted `etag`, `last-modified`, the two request-id headers, `date`, and no `x-amz-version-id` — and the second is identical except that `x-amz-version-id: null` is added. It drives `Prepare_Head_Object` with exactly the corpus parameters (`Version_ID = "null"`, `Request_Payer = "requester"`, `Checksum_Mode = True`) and `Execute_Head_Object`. The first leg raises; all three control assertions pass, which isolates the version clause.

**Notes:** This predicate is what fails the repository's own conformance corpus against RustFS, MinIO and SeaweedFS: `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 4 records 7/7 deterministic RustFS reproductions and section 5 records the byte-identical failure messages in `review/baseline/minio.log:96` and `review/baseline/seaweedfs.log:99`, and the corpus case is `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1144-1172`, which sets `Parameters.Version_ID := "null"` and asserts `Object_Found`. Its high-level twin is CLIENT-OBJECTS-1, the identical predicate shape at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-155`, and any fix must cover both layers. SERVER-3 is why neither shows up in the in-tree lanes: the in-repo server always supplies the header the client demands — HeadObject writes `x-amz-version-id: null` unconditionally at `s3_applications.adb:16619-16622` and GetObject writes it at `:4189-4194` only when the request named a version — so the memory-server CI gate is green while all three third-party servers are red, and the three findings must be sequenced together with the client predicates fixed before the server spelling. One attribution difference to reconcile when filing: this area's verification report anchors the `null` fallback at `s3_applications.adb:4188-4194` and describes it as the GET/HEAD route, which is the reverse of the operations SERVER-3 names; the masking effect is the same either way. This is not a documented limitation — `docs/compatibility/s3.md:449` documents the opposite direction ("The server omits the header for unversioned publication"), so the repository already treats omission as the correct shape, and the only statement of the client demand is the private-part comment at `low_level.ads:10533-10535`, which is the design decision under review rather than a maintained limitation. No existing test pins the defective behaviour at this layer: the socket-corpus cases added by commit 2703ed6 (`head-cancel-version`, `wrong-head-version`) always emit the header and none omits it, so the correction contradicts no assertion here. Two maintainer decisions a fix needs: whether the contract becomes "the response must not contradict the selector" — accept an absent header, reject a present-and-different one — or whether the mismatch becomes a typed outcome instead of an exception, noting that RustFS's versioning-suspended sentinel `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` (`review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 1(c)) is present-and-different, so a strict-echo contract is not viable across real servers even after the relaxation; and whether the server's `null` fallback should remain once the client stops requiring it.

---

### CLIENT-LOW-2: `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes` raises `Invalid_Response` when a 200 omits `x-amz-version-id` after a `versionId=` request

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1). The verifier raised it on the same rubric reading as CLIENT-LOW-1 and judged it sharper on one count: no server can satisfy the demand, because the repository's own in-process S3 application omits the header on this route, so the operation cannot complete against the library's own server either. The same strict alternative applies and was again recorded as immaterial: read literally, the P0 interop clause describes the library emitting bytes a stock peer refuses rather than refusing bytes a stock peer legitimately emits, which would put the finding at P1.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5383-5396` (`Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Complete_Response`), which raises `Invalid_Response with "GetObjectAttributes response does not match request"` when the outcome is `Object_Attributes_Found` and `US.Length (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID) > 0 and then Version_ID /= US.To_String (Prepared.Requested_Get_Attributes_Version_ID)`; the binding at `:5371-5372` (`Version_ID : constant String := Singleton_Header ("x-amz-version-id")`) and `Singleton_Header`'s `""` for `Count = 0` at `:5348-5350`; the executor at `:5488-5516`; the in-repo GetObjectAttributes handler at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16347-16352`, which emits the header only when `US.Length (Snapshot.Info.Version) > 0` and has no `null` fallback; the pinned model's `GetObjectAttributesOutput` (`required = []`, `VersionId` an optional `x-amz-version-id` header); the unexercised coverage at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1675-1861`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_2.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** The same mechanism as CLIENT-LOW-1 in a different decoder: the optional `x-amz-version-id` response header is required to equal the requested selector, and an absent header compares as the empty string, so `"" /= "null"` trips the check. A `GetObjectAttributes` request that selects `versionId=null` against an object in a never-versioned bucket turns a valid 200 carrying the requested ETag and ObjectSize into `Invalid_Response`. It is deterministic and reachable both from `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes` and from the composable `Client.Objects` GetObjectAttributes operation, which calls the same decoder. What makes it sharper than CLIENT-LOW-1 is that the demand is unsatisfiable even against the repository's own server: the attributes handler at `s3_applications.adb:16347-16352` has no `null` fallback, unlike the GET/HEAD route. The program proves that on the wire without Docker: it PUTs one object into a never-versioned bucket through `Repro_S3_Server.Run`, shows with a raw signed `GET /bucket/key?attributes&versionId=null` that the server answers 200 with zero `x-amz-version-id` headers, then starts the same application on the loopback listener and drives `Prepare_Get_Object_Attributes` (`Version_ID = "null"`, `Attributes.Entity_Tag` and `Attributes.Object_Size` selected) plus `Execute_Get_Object_Attributes`. The control leg, the same request with no version selector, returns `Object_Attributes_Found` with the ETag and `ObjectSize = 16`.

**Notes:** Unlike CLIENT-LOW-1 and CLIENT-LOW-3, this one is not masked by the in-repo server; it reproduces against it, because the attributes handler at `s3_applications.adb:16347-16352` has no fallback where SERVER-3's HeadObject (`:16619-16622`) and GetObject (`:4189-4194`) sites do. Nothing is currently red only because no maintained test reaches the branch: `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1675-1861` (`Require_Get_Object_Attributes`) never sets `Parameters.Version_ID`, and the socket-corpus attributes cases all echo a version. The defect is therefore unexercised rather than pinned, so no assertion has to be inverted with the fix. `docs/qualification/get-object-attributes.md:41-42` carries the same strict-echo requirement that CLIENT-LOW-1 shows is not satisfiable against real servers, and belongs with this finding rather than with CLIENT-OBJECTS, since the code it describes lives here. A fix needs the same maintainer decision as CLIENT-LOW-1 — tolerate an absent header versus expose a typed mismatch outcome — and the verifier's recommendation is that it be made consistently across all four sites (`low_level.adb:5641-5658`, `:5383-5396`, `:12463-12489` and `client-objects.adb:121-155` with `:7177-7211`).

---

### CLIENT-LOW-3: `Execute_{Put,Get,Delete}_Object_Tagging` require exactly one `x-amz-version-id` on every success, even with no version requested

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1). The verifier raised it on the same rubric reading and called it the broadest of the three, because the demand is unconditional on the request: no caller input avoids it, so the plain `Put_Tags` / `Get_Tags` / `Delete_Tags` path is affected on every server whose bucket never had versioning enabled, and for Put and Delete the mutation has already been applied when the exception is raised. The same strict alternative was recorded and rejected: under a literal reading of the P0 clause, which is about the library emitting bytes a stock peer refuses, all three version-echo findings fall back to P1.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12463-12489` (`Response_Version_ID`, nested in `Execute_Object_Tagging`), whose gate is `if Count > 1 or else (Success and then Count /= 1) then raise Invalid_Response with "invalid object tagging version header multiplicity";`; the supply of `Success` as `Status = Model.Response_Code (Expected)` at `:12527-12529`, which the pinned model makes 200 for `PutObjectTagging` and `GetObjectTagging` and 204 for `DeleteObjectTagging` (`service-2.json` gives `DeleteObjectTagging` an explicit `"responseCode": 204`; the other two default to 200); the separate and correct present-and-different check at `:12531-12538`, which is the one that binds an explicit selector; the shared executor's operation guard at `:12452`; the spec comment the code contradicts at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:10538-10543` ("Empty means the request selected the current version, so any returned version remains an observation rather than proof"); the model's `PutObjectTaggingOutput.required = []`, `DeleteObjectTaggingOutput.required = []` and `GetObjectTaggingOutput.required = ["TagSet"]`, with `VersionId` an optional header in all three; the in-repo server's `Set_Version_Header` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15846-15854`; the existing assertions of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15091-15253`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_3.adb` with helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Response_Version_ID` conflates two different rules. Header multiplicity (`Count > 1`) is a genuine protocol violation; header presence on a success is not, because the model marks `VersionId` optional in all three tagging output shapes. Since `Success` is only "the status matched the modeled response code", every 200 PutObjectTagging, every 200 GetObjectTagging and every 204 DeleteObjectTagging against a bucket that never had versioning enabled raises `Invalid_Response "invalid object tagging version header multiplicity"` — a misleading message, since the count is zero, not two. It needs no request parameter at all, which is what makes it the broadest of the three: the plain `Put_Tags` / `Get_Tags` / `Delete_Tags` call path is affected. For PutObjectTagging and DeleteObjectTagging the mutation has already been applied when the exception is raised, so the caller cannot distinguish an applied mutation from a corrupt response. `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 8 records RustFS answering `PUT /bucket/native-object?tagging` with `200 OK`, `content-length: 0` and the two request-id headers only. The program scripts six exchanges on a loopback origin: `PUT`, `GET` (with a one-tag `<Tagging>` document) and `DELETE` on `/never-versioned/native-object?tagging` carrying no `x-amz-version-id`, then the same three with the header present, none of the six prepared requests carrying a `versionId` selector. All three no-header legs raise; all six control assertions pass, including the decoded tag set reaching the caller.

**Notes:** Its high-level twin is CLIENT-OBJECTS-2, `Tagging_Version_ID` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7177-7211`, which is the site that actually failed against RustFS in `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 8 because the corpus calls `Client_Objects.Put_Tags`; the two are one defect at two layers and share a fix direction. As with CLIENT-LOW-1, the in-repo server is what keeps CI green — `Set_Version_Header` at `s3_applications.adb:15846-15854` defaults to the literal `"null"` when the identity has no version — and the server-side half of that masking is SERVER-3. **An existing test asserts the wrong behaviour and must be inverted with the fix:** `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15091-15253` (`Require_Invalid_Version` with `Invalid_Version_Kind = Missing`, fixtures `tag-put-missing` / `tag-get-missing` / `tag-delete-missing` served at `:4332-4334`, `:4361-4363` and `:4386-4389`) requires `Invalid_Response` synchronously or `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` with `Object_Tag_Mutation_Outcome_Unknown` in the composable path, and raises `"… object tagging accepted missing version response"` otherwise; the `Missing` case leaves `Selected` empty, so it pins exactly the no-selector case. It directly contradicts `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1617-1673` (`Require_Object_Tagging`), which expects success from real servers. It is not backed by any document: `docs/compatibility/s3.md:364-372` describes the tagging clients as validating "bounded responses and version IDs" without stating that a version header is mandatory, and the crate's own spec comment at `low_level.ads:10538-10543` says the opposite of the code. One line-span difference to reconcile when filing: this area's verification report gives `Response_Version_ID` as `:12463-12489`, while the CLIENT-OBJECTS consolidation cites `:12465-12475` for the same predicate from its own discovery report; the code is the same gate either way. The maintainer decision is whether `Version_ID` stays a plain `Unbounded_String` that is empty when the server omitted the header — the shape `Head_Object_Result` and `Get_Object_Attributes_Result` already use — or becomes an explicit optional, which is a public API change.

### CLIENT-LOW-4: a bodyless or non-XML 4xx/5xx becomes `Invalid_Response` instead of the typed rejection carrying the wire status

**Severity:** P2 (unchanged from the discovery report, whose confidence the verifier raised from Medium to High now that it has a run). Wrong diagnostics on a path a conforming S3 origin does not produce: the trigger is a non-S3 intermediary rather than ordinary valid input, and the client fails closed rather than accepting something wrong.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-errors.adb:109-127` (`Parse`), where an empty or non-`<Error>` document raises `Malformed_Error` from either the `Seen_Code`/`Seen_Message` check or `when XML.XML_Error =>`; the roughly twenty decoders that convert it to `Invalid_Response`, among them `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1956-1958` (ListObjectsV2), `:11297-11299` (DeleteObject) and `:7731-7733` (PutObject), with the same pattern at `:1694`, `:2228`, `:3340`, `:3884`, `:4369`, `:5331`, `:8240`, `:10849`, `:12412`, `:12732`, `:12925`, `:13193`, `:13427`, `:14044`, `:14426`, `:14779` and every bucket-control GET; the contrasting deliberate treatment for GetObject at `:7137-7166`, whose comment at `:7159-7163` reads "S3 peers and intermediaries may return bodyless or non-XML HTTP errors. Preserve the typed rejection and wire status instead of turning a valid rejection into a client protocol failure."; `Head_Error` at `:4856-4872`, which synthesises `Code => "HTTP" & <status>` and is used unconditionally by HEAD at `:5670-5674`; the composable mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-bounded_rest_xml_reads.adb:105-111`; the distinct `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` value in `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:37-51`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_4.adb` with helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `S3.Errors.Malformed_Error` is raised for any non-2xx whose body is absent, whitespace, HTML or otherwise not an S3 `<Error>` document, and about twenty decoders convert it to `Invalid_Response`, discarding the HTTP status the caller needs. The failure mode appears when a reverse proxy, load balancer or CDN answers on the origin's behalf with a 503 and an HTML page, or when a peer answers 403 with no body. A synchronous caller loses the status entirely; a composable caller receives `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` where `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` is the correct classification, so a transient outage is treated as a protocol violation and is not retried. The repository has already made the opposite decision twice — unconditionally for HEAD through `Head_Error`, and with an explicit explanatory comment for GetObject — so this is an internal inconsistency rather than a missing design decision, and every affected outcome type already carries a `Rejected` variant with the wire `Status`, so no new public type is needed. The program scripts four exchanges: a signed `ListObjectsV2` answered `503 Service Unavailable` with `content-type: text/html` and an HTML body, and a signed `DeleteObject` answered `403 Forbidden` with `content-length: 0`, both asserted to be typed rejections with `Error.Code = "HTTP503"` / `"HTTP403"` and both failing; then two controls that pass — a bodyless 503 on `HeadObject`, which already yields `Head_Object_Rejected` with `Error.Code = HTTP503`, and a 503 carrying a well-formed `<Error>` document on `ListObjectsV2`, which yields `Rejected` with status 503 and `Error.Code = SlowDown`.

**Notes:** No existing test asserts either behaviour — every 4xx/5xx fixture in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb` carries a well-formed `<Error>` body, and the only bodyless or non-XML error tests are the GetObject ones behind the tolerant path at `:7137-7166` — so the correction contradicts nothing. It stays P2 rather than P1 because a conforming S3 origin always emits an `<Error>` document: the model describes per-operation `errors` shapes but says nothing about intermediaries. The verifier's refutation attempts all failed: there is no wrapper that catches `Malformed_Error` earlier, since the composable generics catch only `Invalid_Response`; and the strictness is not uniform, so it is not deliberate. The fix is mechanical — mirror the GetObject `when S3.Errors.Malformed_Error` handler at each site — and the one maintainer decision is whether the synthesised `Code = "HTTP<status>"` convention that HEAD and GetObject already use should become the documented contract for the rest.

---

### CLIENT-LOW-5: `Prepare_Create_Session` rejects the explicit `Bucket_Key_Enabled = False` its specification says is preserved, and would serialise any set value as `true`

**Severity:** P2 (unchanged from the discovery report). Contract drift plus a latent value inversion rather than a live wire defect: no wrong bytes can reach the wire today, because the policy check fires before the serialiser.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:6401-6416`, the published contract ("Empty strings preserve omission; Bucket_Key_Enabled preserves absent versus explicit false", with `@field Bucket_Key_Enabled Presence-preserving bucket-key request flag`); `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2429-2432` (`Validate_Create_Session_Policy`), whose raising disjunction includes `or else (Bucket_Key_Enabled.Is_Set and then not Bucket_Key_Enabled.Value)`; the call from `Prepare_Create_Session` at `:2472-2482`, which remaps the raise to `Invalid_Request with "invalid CreateSession parameters"`; the serialiser at `:2494-2502`, which emits `Value => US.To_Unbounded_String ("true")` and never reads the caller's boolean; the pinned model's `CreateSessionRequest` (`required = ["Bucket"]`, `BucketKeyEnabled` a boolean bound to the header `x-amz-server-side-encryption-bucket-key-enabled`).

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_5.adb`, a pure preparation test with no server, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** The specification says the flag preserves absent versus explicit `false`; `Prepare_Create_Session` raises `Invalid_Request` on exactly the explicit `false` the comment promises, with a message that does not say which member was wrong, and the serialiser that would have emitted it hard-codes the string `"true"`, so the caller's boolean is never read. A caller who reads the specification and sets `(Is_Set => True, Value => False)` gets an exception; had the policy check not fired, the request would have carried `true`, silently inverting the value. The program prepares a request against an HTTPS virtual-hosted S3 Express origin with `SessionMode = ReadWrite`, `ServerSideEncryption = aws:kms`, a KMS key ARN and `Bucket_Key_Enabled = (Is_Set => True, Value => False)`, and asserts that preparation succeeds and that the canonical request carries `x-amz-server-side-encryption-bucket-key-enabled:false`; both fail. The two control legs pass and show the code path is otherwise live: with the flag absent the header is absent from the signed headers, and with it explicitly true the canonical request carries `…bucket-key-enabled:true`.

**Notes:** One refutation attempt partially succeeded and relocates rather than removes the finding: the pinned model's own documentation string for `CreateSessionRequest.BucketKeyEnabled` states that S3 Bucket Keys are always enabled for directory-bucket GET and PUT and cannot be disabled, so refusing `false` may well be the right wire behaviour — but then the `.ads` contract at `:6401-6404` is what is wrong, and the two cannot both stand. The `"true"` literal is harmless only today; it is dead-but-wrong code that inverts the caller's value the moment the policy is relaxed, and nothing in the file ties the check and the literal together. There is no existing coverage for an explicit `false`. The maintainer decision is a genuine either/or and should be recorded next to the value per the repository's convention for externally mandated policy: if S3 Express really forbids disabling bucket keys, keep the check, replace the `"true"` literal with a serialiser that cannot express `false`, and correct the `.ads` comment to say that only `true` is accepted; if the model's boolean is to be honoured, drop the check at `:2429-2432` and serialise `"true"`/`"false"` from the caller's value at `:2494-2502`. Either way the `"true"` literal must not survive unchanged.

---

### CLIENT-LOW-6: `Execute_Create_Multipart_Upload` sends a mismatched prepared request to the origin before rejecting it

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier raised it from the discovery report's uncounted observation that this executor lacks the up-front `Prepared.Operation` check every other one has, which the discovery did not test). It needs an erroneous caller input, which is the P2 "needs unusual input" clause, but the consequence is a real unintended request executed against the origin rather than a wrong diagnostic, so the verifier placed it at the top of P2.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:3505-3526` (`Execute_Create_Multipart_Upload`), where `Response` and `Payload` are elaborated in the declarative part so `Flyology.HTTP.Client.Execute` runs before the body, and no `Prepared.Operation` test exists anywhere in the subprogram; the check that does exist, after the exchange, at `:3345-3355` (`Decode_Create_Multipart_Complete_Response`); the guarded siblings for contrast, `Execute_List_Parts` `:13268-13279`, `Execute_Head_Object` `:5864-5867` and `Execute_Delete_Objects` `:12820-12823`, and the four thin wrappers delegating to guarded shared executors at `:8245`, `:9798`, `:10892` and `:12452`; the specification block at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:6777-6790`, which carries no `@exception Invalid_Request`, against `:6763-6769`, which documents exactly that exception for the decoder.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_6.adb` with helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** `Prepared_Request` is one opaque private type used for all 116 modeled operations, so "did the caller hand me the right kind of prepared request?" is checked as the first statement of every executor in the package. The verifier enumerated all 110 `Execute_*` bodies: each either performs that test first or is a thin wrapper delegating to a guarded shared executor, and `Execute_Create_Multipart_Upload` is the single standalone body with no guard. Because its `Response` and `Payload` objects are elaborated in the declarative part, the whole HTTP exchange happens before the decoder's `Invalid_Request` is raised, so the fail-safe invariant the rest of the API establishes — a mismatched prepared request never touches the network — is broken at exactly one site. The mismatched request is not harmless: `Prepared.Message` is a fully signed, complete request for whatever operation it was prepared for, so the worst case is that a mutation the caller did not intend to send at that moment (a `DeleteObject`, an `AbortMultipartUpload`, a `PutObject` with a retained payload) is really executed against the origin, with its effect reported only as "prepared request operation mismatch". It is deterministic and needs no server cooperation. The program prepares a genuine `DeleteObject` for `/example-bucket/irreplaceable-object`, hands it to `Execute_Create_Multipart_Upload`, and asserts both that `Invalid_Request` is raised and that the origin exchange counter did not move: the exception assertion passes, the counter assertion fails, and the origin log shows it received `DELETE /example-bucket/irreplaceable-object HTTP/1.1`. Two controls pass and prove the counter works: the same mismatched request through the guarded `Execute_List_Parts` raises and sends nothing, and a genuine `Execute_Delete_Object` succeeds with exactly one request.

**Notes:** No existing test covers a mismatched prepared request for this operation, so nothing has to change with the fix. The fix needs no maintainer decision and no new public entity: one guard copied from `Execute_List_Parts` (`:13276-13279`) placed before the declarative part, plus an `@exception Invalid_Request` line in the `.ads` block at `:6777-6784` to match every sibling. The verifier's refutation attempts all failed — a caller never legitimately reuses a prepared request across executors, which is precisely why every other executor checks, and the decoder catches the mismatch only after the side effect has happened.

---

### CLIENT-LOW-7: `Valid_Model_Scalar` accepts Ada literal integer forms and forwards them verbatim into the signed request

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier raised it from the discovery report's uncounted observation that integer members are validated with `Long_Long_Integer'Value`, which the discovery did not carry to the wire). It needs unusual caller text through the public `Prepare_Model_Request`, which is the P2 clause, but the request is signed and sent with no client-side complaint, so the validator silently fails at the one job it exists for.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:949-965`, where `Model.Integer_Shape | Model.Long_Shape` members are validated by computing `Number := Long_Long_Integer'Value (Value);` (the `'Value` call is at `:955`) and range-checking `Number`, while the raw `Value` text — not `Number` — is what the projector percent-encodes into the request target; the public projector documented at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:193-220` and declared in the visible part at `:210`; the pinned model's `MaxKeys` (`{"type": "integer"}` with no `min`/`max`, so bounds reject nothing here and the defect is purely lexical).

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_7.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Valid_Model_Scalar` exists so that a modeled member value is checked before it is signed and sent, but for integer and long shapes it borrows Ada's own numeric-literal grammar through `Long_Long_Integer'Value` instead of the S3 wire grammar, and then hands the projector the caller's original text rather than the parsed number. Ada's `'Value` accepts underscores, based literals, exponents and a leading `+`, so a caller of the public `Prepare_Model_Request` who supplies an Ada-shaped literal gets a fully signed request carrying `max-keys=1_000`, `max-keys=16%233E8%23`, `max-keys=1E3` or `max-keys=%2B1000` — bytes no S3 server accepts — with no client-side error at all. The failure surfaces only as a remote `400 InvalidArgument` whose cause is not visible locally, and because the malformed text is inside the signed canonical request it cannot be repaired by an intermediary. It is deterministic. The program calls `Prepare_Model_Request (Model.List_Objects_V2_Operation, …)` with the `MaxKeys` member set to `"1_000"`, `"16#3E8#"`, `"1E3"` and `"+1000"` and asserts each is rejected at preparation; all four are accepted and the report prints the resulting target for each. Two controls pass and show the validator is reached and does reject genuine garbage: `"500"` prepares and reaches the target verbatim, and `"not-a-number"` is rejected as `invalid modeled request scalar`.

**Notes:** The typed preparers are unaffected because they format with `'Image`, which is why this is P2 rather than P1 — but that confines the defect to the generic public entry point rather than making it unreachable, and `Prepare_Model_Request` is declared in the visible part at `low_level.ads:210`, not an internal helper. Nothing downstream catches it: the repro shows the value reaching the signed request target, and the first thing that notices is the S3 server. No existing test exercises a non-decimal integer member through `Prepare_Model_Request`. The fix is local and needs no maintainer policy decision: reject any character outside an optional `-` followed by decimal digits before calling `'Value`, or serialise the trimmed `Number'Image` instead of the raw text so that the validated value is the value sent. The MODEL area independently confirmed that no model-side defect contributes — the descriptor supplies `Minimum` and `Maximum` correctly, compared as 64-bit values for both `integer` and `long` kinds — and routed the observation here as O-7.

---

## CLIENT-OBJECTS: the object convenience client, its response bounds and its composable outcomes (CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 .. CLIENT-OBJECTS-3)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ads` (all 5,433 lines) and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb` (all 8,884 lines), read against `docs/architecture/client-objects.md`, `docs/qualification/{get-object,head-object,object-reads,conditional-put,object-tagging,get-object-attributes,delete-object}.md`, the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json`, the real-server capture `review/baseline-rustfs.md`, the low-level result contracts and decoders in `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ad?`, the status mapping in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` and the pinned `flyology_http_0.1.3_eb09a80a` `Read_All` and `Finish` contracts. Coverage was inspected in `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb`, `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb` and `tests/corpora/composable-client/{put-certainty,object-tagging-certainty}.tsv`. The programs live in `tests/repros/src/` and drive a scripted loopback HTTP/1.1 origin (`repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`), because the in-process S3 application cannot show the first two findings: it always emits the header they demand. No Docker script was run in this area; the real-server evidence is the verbatim capture already recorded in `review/baseline-rustfs.md`, replayed through that scripted origin. CopyObject is not part of this package — it lives in `Client.Transfers` and `Low_Level` — so the copy-source and metadata-directive items of the area brief belong to those areas. Two defects confirmed in another area are the same predicates one layer down and are not counted twice: CLIENT-LOW-1 (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658`) is the twin of CLIENT-OBJECTS-1, and CLIENT-LOW-3 (`Execute_Object_Tagging`, `client-low_level.adb:12465-12475`) is the twin of CLIENT-OBJECTS-2, so each fix has to change both layers.

### CLIENT-OBJECTS-1: Whole/range GetObject and HeadObject reject a valid 200/206 that omits `x-amz-version-id` after a `versionId=` request

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1). The verifier raised it because the effect is a total interop break rather than a corner of one operation: no stock server can satisfy the demand for an object in a bucket that never had versioning enabled, which is the documented meaning of `versionId=null`, and the only server that passes is the in-repo one, whose fallback exists to satisfy exactly this check. The counter-reading the verifier recorded and rejected: the rubric's P0 clause names "an interop-breaking protocol violation that a stock client or server rejects", and here the crate emits conforming requests and mis-validates conforming responses, so a strict textual reading lands on P1 ("wrong result or unexpected exception on valid input"), a direction of rejection the verifier judged immaterial to the harm the clause exists to capture.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:121-137` (`Get_Object_Response_Bound`) and `:139-155` (`Head_Object_Response_Bound`), both returning `(Requested_Version'Length = 0 or else US.To_String (Result.Version_ID) = Requested_Version) and then (Returned_Payer /= "requester" or else Requested_Payer = "requester")`; the four callers reached from public entry points, `:951-956` (synchronous `Get_Whole`, `raise Low_Level.Invalid_Response`), `:2429-2434` (composable whole read), `:2551-2556` (composable range read) and `:2964-2973` (composable HeadObject); the verbatim copy of the caller's selector into the bound at `:2683`, `:2804` and `:3063`, with no `"null"` special case anywhere in the file; the decoders that leave `Version_ID` empty when the header is absent at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5813` (HeadObject) and `:7220` (GetObject); the in-repo server's fallbacks at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4189-4194` and `:16620-16622`; the existing assertions of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:9333` (definition `:9308-9331`) and `:9741` (definition `:9711-9739`); the qualification prose at `docs/qualification/get-object.md:11-12` and `head-object.md:10-11`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_objects_1.adb` with helper `repro_client_objects_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Get_Object_Response_Bound` and `Head_Object_Response_Bound` require a successful response to echo the requested `versionId` byte-for-byte. The pinned model marks `VersionId` optional on both output shapes — `GetObjectOutput` and `HeadObjectOutput` have `required: None` and carry `VersionId` as `{"shape":"ObjectVersionId","location":"header","locationName":"x-amz-version-id"}` — and a bucket that never had versioning enabled answers `?versionId=null` with a `200`/`206` carrying no `x-amz-version-id` at all: `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 1 records that RustFS response verbatim, and section 5 records byte-identical failures against MinIO (`review/baseline/minio.log:96`) and SeaweedFS (`review/baseline/seaweedfs.log:99`). The absent header decodes to `Version_ID = ""`, `"" = "null"` is False, and a complete valid read is destroyed: the synchronous `Get_Whole` raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` after the head is decoded, the composable whole and range reads clear the destination and return `*_Exchange_Failed` with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, and `Head_Object` returns `Head_Exchange_Failed`. Anyone addressing an object by `versionId=null` — the documented way to name an object in a never-versioned bucket, and what the repository's own conformance corpus does at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1147` — hits it on every call. It is fully deterministic, because the predicate is a pure function of two strings. The program scripts six exchanges on a loopback origin: three carrying the verbatim RustFS never-versioned header set (`200 OK`, `accept-ranges`, `content-type`, strong `etag`, `last-modified`, `x-request-id`, `x-amz-request-id`, `date`, `content-length: 64`, no `x-amz-version-id`) and three controls identical except for an added `x-amz-version-id: null`, calling `Head_Object`, `Get_Whole` (`Whole_Get_Outcome` overload) and `Get_Whole` (bounded-destination overload) against each set with `Version_ID => "null"`. All three control legs pass, which isolates the version clause: with no `x-amz-request-charged` the payer clause is True, so it cannot be what fires.

**Notes:** This finding and CLIENT-LOW-1, the identical predicate in the low-level layer at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5641-5658`, are the two client-side defects behind the failed conformance corpus against RustFS, MinIO and SeaweedFS recorded in `review/baseline-rustfs.md`; SERVER-3, the in-repo server's unconditional `x-amz-version-id: null`, is the fallback that masks both, which is why the memory-server CI gate is green, so the three must be sequenced together and the client predicates fixed before the server spelling. One attribution difference to reconcile when filing: this area's reports place the unconditional emission in GetObject's `Set_Common_Headers` (`s3_applications.adb:16620-16622`) and the conditional one, emitted only when the request carried a version selector, on HeadObject (`:4189-4194`), which is the reverse of the operations named in SERVER-3; the masking effect is the same either way, because between them the in-repo server always supplies the header the client demands. Two existing tests assert the defective behaviour and must change with the fix, `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:9333` (`Require_Invalid_Whole_Get ("scoped-get-version-missing")`) and `:9741` (`Require_Invalid_Head ("scoped-head-version-missing")`); the `-mismatch`, `-duplicate`, `-control` and `-del` cases in the same loops are correct rejections and must keep failing. `docs/qualification/get-object.md:11-12` and `head-object.md:10-11` ("a successful response must echo it byte-for-byte") state the requirement the pinned model does not impose and no stock server satisfies, so they are part of the defect rather than a defence and must be reworded; the verifier explicitly did not treat them as a documented limitation for that reason. The maintainer decision a fix needs is the contract itself: relaxing the predicate to "present and different is a mismatch; absent is accepted" (`review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 7) is the minimal change, but it will still reject RustFS's suspended-versioning sentinel `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000` (section 1(c)), so a strict-echo contract is not viable across real servers even after the relaxation, and whether the residual mismatch stays an exception with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` or becomes a typed outcome is a public-API decision, because today the exception escapes before any outcome exists and no oracle mode can classify it. `git log -S 'Get_Object_Response_Bound'` gives `f4cabde` and `-S 'Head_Object_Response_Bound'` gives `2703ed6`, both 2026-08-30; neither diffstat touches `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` or any `tests/scripts/test-*.sh`, and both touch only `object_storage_test_cases.adb` and `s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, whose fakes always echo the requested version.

---

### CLIENT-OBJECTS-2: Put/Get/DeleteObjectTagging reject every successful response that omits `x-amz-version-id`, even with no version requested

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1). The verifier raised it for the same interop reason as CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 and judged it stronger on three counts: the demand is unconditional, so no caller input avoids it; it was observed directly against a real server, `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 8 recording RustFS's verbatim `200 OK` to the repository's own `PUT …?tagging` and the resulting `INVALID_RESPONSE`; and the mutation is actually applied while being reported as `Object_Tag_Mutation_Outcome_Unknown`, with the documented reconciliation read failing identically, so a caller can neither complete nor reconcile through this API. The same counter-reading applies and was again judged immaterial: the crate is the rejector rather than the emitter, so a strict textual reading of the rubric's P0 interop clause lands on P1.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:7177-7211` (`Tagging_Version_ID`), whose gate is `if Count > 1 or else (Success and then Count /= 1) then raise Low_Level.Invalid_Response with "invalid object tagging version header multiplicity";` and which consults `Requested` only later, at `:7203-7204`, for a present-and-different value; the three callers `:7421-7423` (Put, `Success := HTTP_Client.Status (Response) = 200`), `:7669-7671` (Get, `= 200`) and `:7960-7962` (Delete, `= 204`), each wrapping the call in `exception when Low_Level.Invalid_Response =>` and producing `Normalize_*_Failure (HTTP_Client.Response_Invalid, …)`; the `Start_*` procedures every public tagging entry point funnels through at `:7511`, `:7758` and `:8049`; the low-level decoder that already accepts an empty version at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12379-12383`; the in-repo server's `Set_Version_Header` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15846-15854`; the maintained documents `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:99-101` and `docs/qualification/object-tagging.md:72-74`; the maintained certainty rows 3, 5 and 7 of `tests/corpora/composable-client/object-tagging-certainty.tsv`; the existing assertions of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15091-15254` (`Require_Invalid_Version`), invoked at `:15256-15260`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_objects_2.adb`, exit status 1, six failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Tagging_Version_ID` raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` whenever a successful tagging response carries anything other than exactly one `x-amz-version-id` header. Rejecting a duplicate is right; rejecting an absent one is not, because the pinned model marks the member optional on all three output shapes — `PutObjectTaggingOutput.required` is `None`, `GetObjectTaggingOutput.required` is `['TagSet']` and `DeleteObjectTaggingOutput.required` is `None` — and a never-versioned bucket has no version to report. Because the multiplicity test never reads `Requested`, it fires on ordinary tagging calls that mention no version at all, which is exactly what the repository's own real-server call `Client_Objects.Put_Tags (…)` at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1629` does. The consequence is worse than a failed read: the server applied the tag replacement or deletion and answered `200`/`204`, the client reports `Object_Tag_Mutation_Outcome_Unknown` with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, the `Tagging_Outcome` convenience overload re-raises the exception, and the documented way out of an unknown mutation, a reconciling `Get_Tags`, fails the same way. It is deterministic and affects every caller using object tagging against MinIO, RustFS, SeaweedFS or AWS on a never-versioned bucket. The program scripts eight exchanges: four carrying the verbatim RustFS tagging reply shape from `review/baseline-rustfs.md` section 8 (`x-request-id`, `x-amz-request-id`, `content-length`, no `x-amz-version-id`; `200` for Put, `200` plus a one-tag `<Tagging>` document for Get, `204` for Delete), then four controls identical except for `x-amz-version-id: null`, calling `Put_Tags`, `Get_Tags`, `Delete_Tags` and the `Tagging_Outcome` convenience `Put_Tags`, with every `Parameters.Version_ID` left empty. All four control legs pass.
