**Notes:** CLIENT-OBJECTS-2 and CLIENT-LOW-3 (`Execute_Object_Tagging`'s twin predicate at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:12465-12475`) are the two tagging sites of one defect and share a fix direction; the corpus reaches this layer, not the low-level twin, because it calls `Client_Objects.Put_Tags`. As with CLIENT-OBJECTS-1, the in-repo server's always-emitted header is what keeps CI green, the server-side half being SERVER-3. The balance of maintained documents is against the code, not for it: `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:99-101` says "All three preserve the optional response version ID and structured S3 errors", and rows 3, 5 and 7 of `tests/corpora/composable-client/object-tagging-certainty.tsv` (`selection = omitted-version`) require a `200`/`204` with no S3 code to map to `Object_Tag_Mutation_Completed` with `No_Failure` and no reconciliation, which is precisely the case the code refuses; `docs/qualification/object-tagging.md:72-74` is ambiguous rather than permissive, so it is not a documented limitation, and a fix must reconcile it with `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:100-101`. `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:15091-15254` asserts the defective behaviour for all three operations and must change, but only its `Missing` variant is wrong; the `Duplicate`, `Mismatch`, `Control` and `Delete_Control` variants driven from the same loop are correct rejections. One maintainer decision is needed: whether an absent header on a success yields `Version_ID = ""`, matching the low-level decoder that already accepts an empty value at `client-low_level.adb:12379-12383`, or a new explicit "no version reported" discriminant on the tagging result records, which is a public API change. `git log -S "invalid object tagging version header multiplicity"` gives `51c4375` (2026-08-30) and `144d63d`; `51c4375`'s diffstat touches the socket corpus, the certainty TSV and the fixture verifier, not `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb` and no live-server script.

---

### CLIENT-OBJECTS-3: GetObjectTorrent labels every rejection `No_Failure`, including transient 500/503 responses that every sibling maps to `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier raised it from the discovery report's second uncounted observation, which named only the 404 and 500 inconsistency and did not test it). Wrong diagnostics on a reachable path: the value is a label, callers can still read `Response.Kind`, `Response.Status` and `Response.Error.Code`, and no bytes or state are lost; it is not P1 because the response payload itself is correct and complete.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:5200-5210` (`Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response`), which returns a fixed `Failure => No_Failure` for every decoded outcome with no reference to `Value.Kind`, `Value.Status`, the error `Code` or `Admission`; the classifying sibling in the same file, `Normalize_Get_Object_ACL_Response` at `:4899-4940`; the torrent path that reaches the normalizer only for a decoded complete response at `:5405-5422`; the shared domain's definition at `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:22-23` and the identical field wording for this operation's result at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ads:1527`; the existing coverage at `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:703`, with that corpus's only `500` scenarios at `:341-345` (`malformed-error`) and `:346-348` (`error-limit`).

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_objects_3.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Normalize_Get_Object_Torrent_Response` hardcodes `Failure => No_Failure` instead of classifying the decoded status and error code the way every other normalizer in the package does. The ACL sibling maps `404` with `NoSuchBucket|NoSuchKey|NoSuchVersion` to `Not_Found`, maps `409 OperationAborted`, `429 SlowDown`, `500 InternalError`, `502 BadGateway`, `503 SlowDown` and `504 RequestTimeout` to `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`, and maps anything unmodeled to `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`; the tagging, legal-hold and retention normalizers carry the same table, and `tests/corpora/composable-client/put-certainty.tsv` documents it as the maintained policy. A caller that routes retry policy off the shared `Failure_Reason` domain — which `client.ads:22-23` presents as exactly that, the "stable reason domain shared by composable provider results", with `No_Failure` defined as "Successful or conclusively failed condition" — sees a `503 SlowDown` from GetObjectTorrent as carrying no failure, while the identical response to GetObjectAcl, GetObjectTagging, GetObjectLegalHold or GetObjectRetention on the same client yields `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`. It is deterministic and needs no unusual input; it needs only a server that answers `?torrent` with a transient error. The program scripts five exchanges and demonstrates both sides in one run: a well-formed `503 SlowDown` and a `500 InternalError` answered to `Get_Torrent` (asserted `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`, both failing), a `404 NoSuchKey` answered to `Get_Torrent` (reported only), the identical `503 SlowDown` answered to `Get_ACL` (control, passes with `Unavailable_Or_Retryable`) and a `200 OK` torrent body (control, passes).

**Notes:** The `404` label is a deliberate, tested choice — `tests/src/s3_get_object_torrent_socket_corpus.adb:703` asserts `No_Failure` for a `404` rejection, and for a `404 NoSuchKey` the enum's own text ("conclusively failed") arguably admits it — which is why the program asserts only the `500` and `503` cases and merely reports the `404` one. No test covers a well-formed retryable rejection on this path: that corpus's only `500` scenarios are a malformed error body and an over-limit error body, both of which take the `Response_Invalid` path instead, so the retryable statuses can be corrected without contradicting a test. There is no torrent certainty corpus and no torrent-specific policy in the `.ads`, so nothing documents a different domain for this operation. The maintainer decision is how far to align: the minimal fix maps only the six retryable status and code pairs, and the consistent fix reuses the sibling table whole, which would also change `404` to `Not_Found` and force the `:703` assertion to be updated.

---

## CLIENT-BUCKETS: the bucket convenience client, its authentication classification tables and its ListBuckets binding (CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 .. CLIENT-BUCKETS-3)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads` and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb` (the convenience raise mappers `:60-145`, `List_Page` `:145-222`, `Normalize_Create_Bucket_Response` `:4096-4151`, `Normalize_Head_Bucket_Response` `:4796-4822`, `Normalize_Get_Bucket_Location_Response` `:23460-23499`, the bucket-tagging classifiers `:24417-24450`, the shared generated-mutation classifiers `:26443-26476` and the replication exchange-failure sentinel `:8747-8772`), read against the parts of `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ad?` this file delegates to (`Head_Bucket_Result` and `Head_Bucket_Outcome` `.ads:1370-1440`, `Head_Error` `.adb:4856-4872`, `Decode_Head_Bucket_Response` `.adb:4874-4913`, `Decode_Head_Bucket_Complete_Response` `.adb:4915-4967`, `Decode_Get_Bucket_Location_Response` `.adb:4483-4510`, the ListBuckets prefix binding `.adb:4130-4176` and the PutBucketTagging preparation `.adb:200-245`), the shared reason domain `src/flyology-object_storage-client.ads:22-52`, the in-repo server's credential path (`src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:180-226` and `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1860-1912`), the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json` (`ListBucketsOutput`, `HeadBucketOutput`), `docs/compatibility/s3.md:8-20` and `:487-506`, `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:1-40`, `docs/qualification/head-bucket-composable.md`, `docs/architecture/client-buckets.md:68-75`, and the pinned `flyology_0.1.1` `Flyology.Bytes.Clear` contract (`flyology-bytes.ads:91-93`). Coverage was inspected in `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb` (the per-family certainty corpora and their 401/403 rows) and `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb`. The programs live in `tests/repros/src/` and drive a scripted loopback HTTP/1.1 origin (`repro_client_buckets_fake.ads/.adb`, a private copy of the CLIENT-OBJECTS pattern; the CLIENT-OBJECTS unit was read, not modified), because the in-process S3 application cannot show CLIENT-BUCKETS-2 or CLIENT-BUCKETS-3 and shows only half of CLIENT-BUCKETS-1. One MinIO container was started by hand from the digest pinned in `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` and stopped afterwards; no repository test script, no `alr` and no `gprbuild` was invoked directly, and every build and run went through `tests/repros/run.sh`. CLIENT-BUCKETS-2's discard happens in `Client.Low_Level`, so CLIENT-LOW is its sibling area, but it is counted once, here, because the only public contract that promises a region is this file's convenience `Head`; the fall-through that CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 describes is the structured-error case of the same classification gap whose bodyless and non-XML case is CLIENT-LOW-4, and the two are complementary rather than duplicates.

### CLIENT-BUCKETS-1: authentication rejections are recognised only at HTTP 401, so every stock server's `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` / `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` becomes `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` and, for mutations, `..._Outcome_Unknown`

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report said P2 because the misclassification is "conservative, therefore safe"). The verifier raised it after measuring the one fact the discovery report held as unverified memory: the pinned MinIO release `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` answers an unknown access key with `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` (`Content-Length: 378`) and a wrong secret with `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` (`Content-Length: 433`), and this crate's own S3 application answers both with `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`; neither ever emits 401. With that captured, the rubric's P1 clause is met on all three counts: the path is the most common operator error, the input is a well-formed stock rejection, and two documented public enum meanings are contradicted — `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response  Response was not conclusive or valid` (`client.ads:36`) is reported for a response that is both conclusive and valid, and `Bucket_Creation_Outcome_Unknown  Creation must be reconciled` (`client-buckets.ads:150`) instructs the caller to reconcile a request the server refused before touching any state. The "conservative" reading would hold if the value were advisory; it is the documented decision input for whether a mutation must be reconciled before retry. It is not P0 because `Result.Response.Error.Code` still carries the true code, so a caller who ignores the classification is unaffected.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4109` — the only 401 pairing in `Normalize_Create_Bucket_Response`'s `Conclusive_Rejection`, `or else (Value.Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId")` — and `:4110`, the only 403 pairing, `Code = "AccessDenied"`; `:4123-4131`, where `Failure` is `Authentication_Failed` only for `(401, InvalidAccessKeyId)` and `Authorization_Failed` only for `(403, AccessDenied)`, everything else falling through to `Invalid_Request`, `Unavailable_Or_Retryable` or `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`; `:4136-4143`, where `Disposition` is `Bucket_Definitely_Not_Created` only when `Conclusive_Rejection` and `Bucket_Creation_Outcome_Unknown` otherwise; the same shape in the shared classifier for the generated bucket mutations at `:26443-26453` (`Conclusive_Generated_Mutation_Rejection`) and `:26464-26476` (`Generated_Mutation_Response_Failure`), in bucket tagging at `:24417-24426` (`Conclusive_Bucket_Tag_Rejection`) and `:24437-24450` (`Bucket_Tag_Response_Failure`), and on the read path at `:23474-23477` (`Normalize_Get_Bucket_Location_Response`); the single classifier that switches on status alone, `Normalize_Head_Bucket_Response` (`:4796-4822`), whose `elsif Value.Status = 403 then Authorization_Failed` at `:4810-4811` turns a bodyless credential rejection into an authorisation label instead; the in-repo server's own answer, `Verify_Request` mapping an unknown key and a bad signature alike to `Credential_Rejected` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-authentication.adb:213-216` and `Send_Authentication_Error` emitting `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1907-1910`; the existing assertions of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:130`, `:271`, `:437`, `:711`, `:846`, `:982`, `:1120`, `:1257`, `:1391`, `:1527` and `:1658`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_buckets_1.adb` with helper `repro_client_buckets_fake.ads/.adb`, exit status 1, eight failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** every `Normalize_*_Response`, `Conclusive_*_Rejection` and `*_Response_Failure` body in `client-buckets.adb` enumerates exact `(status, code)` pairs, and the only authentication pair enumerated is `(401, "InvalidAccessKeyId")`. No S3 server in evidence returns 401 for a credential problem: MinIO returns `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` for an unknown key and `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` for a bad signature, and this crate's own server returns `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` for both. The pair therefore never matches, the response falls through to `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, and because the same predicate also decides `Conclusive_Rejection`, every bucket mutation additionally reports `..._Outcome_Unknown`. The caller is told that a well-formed conclusive refusal was "not conclusive or valid" and that the mutation must be reconciled by a read before any retry, when nothing was mutated and the correct action is to fix the credentials. It is deterministic and hits anyone who mistypes a key or lets a secret rotate, including anyone running this client against this repository's own server. `grep -c 'Status = 401 and then Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"'` gives 88 occurrences in `client-buckets.adb`, and `rg InvalidAccessKeyId src/ server/src/` matches only the client packages, so no in-repo server ever emits the pair the client waits for. The program first runs two in-process exchanges against the crate's own S3 application, one with the access key in the credential replaced and one with a single signature hex digit flipped, and prints `HTTP 403 <Code> = "SignatureDoesNotMatch"` for both; it then serves the verbatim MinIO 403 documents from a scripted loopback origin and drives `Get_Location` (read), `Put_Tags` (mutation) and `Create` (mutation) through them, each reporting `CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE` and, for the two mutations, `BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` and `BUCKET_CREATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. Six control legs replay the two enumerated pairs (`401 InvalidAccessKeyId`, `403 AccessDenied`) through the same code and all pass, which isolates the missing pairs as the cause; the origin served all eleven exchanges exactly once.

**Notes:** The live MinIO run reaches the same classification through the same public calls with no scripted bytes (`repro_client_buckets_probe.adb`: `location/wrong-key` and `location/wrong-secret` both `CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE`, `put-tags/wrong-key` and `put-tags/wrong-secret` both `CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE` with `BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, and a `put-tags/control` that reaches `BUCKET_TAGS_REPLACED` with `NO_FAILURE`), so the finding does not depend on the scripted origin. This is not a documented limitation: `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and `docs/qualification/*` say nothing about the accepted status and code pairs for authentication, and the closest text, "every accepted service status/code pair" at `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:21-22`, describes the corpus rather than a limitation. `review/baseline/service-2.json` has no error shape for `InvalidAccessKeyId` or `SignatureDoesNotMatch`, so the pinned model cannot justify the 401 pairing either way. The unit suite pins the wrong behaviour: `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb` carries 32 `401, "InvalidAccessKeyId"` rows and no row anywhere presents `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` or `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`, and `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb` has 401 rows of the same shape; those rows may stay if the 401 pairing is retained, but a fix must add the 403 rows. The same fall-through catches any structured rejection whose exact pair is unlisted, which is the structured-error half of the gap whose bodyless and non-XML half is CLIENT-LOW-4 (`src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1956-1958`); CLIENT-LOW-4 reaches callers of this file through the `Raise_*_Exchange_Failure` mappers at `client-buckets.adb:108-112` and `:137-141`, so the two are complementary and neither is counted twice. `Normalize_Head_Bucket_Response` (`:4796-4822`) is the one classifier that would not fall through, because it switches on status alone; it labels the same 403 `Authorization_Failed`, which is wrong in a different way and must be settled by the same decision. A fix is a maintainer decision about the public error surface: which `(status, code)` pairs count as conclusive authentication rejections (at minimum `403 InvalidAccessKeyId` and `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch`, with `403 RequestTimeTooSkewed` and `400 AuthorizationHeaderMalformed` in the same class), and whether the 401 pairing is kept for compatibility. The identical pattern appears at 18 sites in `client-objects.adb` and 11 in `client-transfers.adb`, so the decision should be made once and applied uniformly.

---

### CLIENT-BUCKETS-2: a bodyless HeadBucket rejection discards `x-amz-bucket-region`, so `Client.Buckets.Head` cannot report where a redirected bucket lives

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2; unchanged). Confirmed for the mechanism and its consequence at every layer of this repository, with one boundary recorded: the discovery report's external premise, that AWS attaches `x-amz-bucket-region` to the 301/403/404 HeadBucket responses used for region discovery, was not checked against a live AWS endpoint and remains unverified. MinIO was checked and does not redirect at all: a HeadBucket signed with an `eu-west-1` credential scope against a bucket MinIO reports as `us-east-1` answers `200 OK` with no `x-amz-bucket-region` header. No server in the six-server matrix can produce the shape, so the repro supplies the header itself. The discarded datum and the `.ads` sentence it contradicts are both real, but reaching it needs a cross-region bucket plus a wrong signing region, which is the rubric's "contract drift needing unusual input".

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4952-4964`, where `Decode_Head_Bucket_Complete_Response` builds `Headers.Bucket_Region => H ("x-amz-bucket-region")`, and `:4965-4966`, where it passes `Headers` into `Decode_Head_Bucket_Response` for every status; `:4886-4906`, where `Headers` is consulted only inside the `Status = 200` branch and returned as `Result => Headers`; `:4907-4913`, the `else` branch returning `(Kind => Head_Bucket_Rejected, Status => Status, Error => Head_Error (...))` with no mention of `Headers`; `:4865-4871`, where `Head_Error` synthesises `Code => "HTTP" & Status_Text`, `Message => Operation & " returned HTTP status " & Status_Text` and `Resource => US.Null_Unbounded_String`, carrying through only `Request_ID` and `Host_ID`, so there is no field a region could hide in; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ads:1396-1404`, where the `Head_Bucket_Rejected` variant has exactly `Status` and `Error`; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads:10129-10141`, where the convenience `Head_Outcome` likewise carries `Region` only on `Bucket_Available`, against the documented purpose at `:10179` (`@param Bucket Bucket whose availability and region are requested`); and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:4806-4807` (`elsif Value.Status in 301 | 307 | 400 | 501 then Invalid_Request`), which additionally labels the redirect a request rejection — discovery observation 2, folded in here.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_buckets_2.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Decode_Head_Bucket_Complete_Response` parses `x-amz-bucket-region` out of the response, hands it to `Decode_Head_Bucket_Response`, and that function throws it away on every non-200 status, returning a synthesised `HTTP301` error whose message repeats the status and nothing else. The `Head_Bucket_Rejected` variant has no field that could hold a region, so the loss is structural rather than an oversight in one branch: no caller of the typed low level, the composable operation or the convenience can recover the one datum a HeadBucket redirect exists to convey, and the convenience's documented purpose is met only on 200. The redirect is additionally reported as `Invalid_Request`, which `client.ads:26` defines as a "Local or modeled service request rejection" — a redirect is not one. It is deterministic. The program works at two layers. It first calls the public `Low_Level.Decode_Head_Bucket_Response` with `Status => 301` and a `Head_Bucket_Result` whose `Bucket_Region` is `eu-west-1`, prints every readable field of the returned rejection (`HTTP301|HeadBucket returned HTTP status 301||AMZREQ1|AMZHOST1`) and finds no region; it then serves `HEAD /flyology-elsewhere` from a scripted loopback origin as `301 Moved Permanently` with `Content-Length: 0` and `x-amz-bucket-region: eu-west-1`, and drives both the convenience `Head` (`Head_Outcome`) and the parameter-record `Head` (`Head_Bucket_Result`), each losing the region and the composable leg reporting `Failure = INVALID_REQUEST`. The four control legs repeat every leg with `200 OK` and the same header, all reporting `eu-west-1`, which isolates the non-200 branch; the origin served all four exchanges.

**Notes:** The discard is in `Client.Low_Level`, so CLIENT-LOW is the sibling area for any fix, but the finding is counted once, here, because the public contract that promises a region is this file's convenience `Head` and CLIENT-LOW did not report it. Refutation attempts that failed: no other public path recovers the header (the composable `Head` exposes only `Response : Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Outcome`, the same two fields); `Get_Location` cannot substitute, because a 301 to GetBucketLocation takes the identical bodyless-rejection route through `Decode_Get_Bucket_Location_Response`'s `else` branch at `client-low_level.adb:4499-4505`; `docs/qualification/head-bucket-composable.md` excludes cross-region redirect *following*, not exposing the region a redirect carries, so it is not a documented limitation; and the header is not rejected as invalid on a 301, since the singleton and transfer-coding checks accept it and the repro shows the value present in `Headers` at the moment it is dropped. `review/baseline/service-2.json` models `BucketRegion` as the `x-amz-bucket-region` header of `HeadBucketOutput` and declares only `NoSuchBucket`, so the model neither requires nor forbids the header on a non-200. Existing tests pin the discard: `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets-testing.adb:583-584` assert `(301, Invalid_Request)` and `(307, Invalid_Request)`, and the helper `Check_Head_Bucket_Response` at `:503-522` builds the outcome from the status alone, so no test ever supplies the header on a non-200. A fix needs a maintainer decision on the public surface: either add a region, or a full modelled-header component, to the rejected variants of `Low_Level.Head_Bucket_Outcome` and `Buckets.Head_Outcome`, or fold the value into `Error.Resource` or `Error.Message`. The first changes two public variant records; the second changes the meaning of a documented field. Both are `.ads` decisions. The measured MinIO behaviour also sharpens the uncounted observation that `Head` returns the signing region when the header is absent (`client-buckets.adb:2846-2849`, documented at `client-buckets.ads:10174-10176`): a bucket MinIO reports as `us-east-1` is reported by `Head` as `Region = "eu-west-1"` purely because the request was signed for `eu-west-1`, so `Head`'s `Region` is not a trustworthy region source on any server that omits the header, which is every server in the matrix.

---

### CLIENT-BUCKETS-3: `Client.Buckets.List_Page` with a nonempty `Prefix` is unusable against the pinned MinIO release

**Severity:** P2 (new; the discovery report recorded it as an uncounted observation with no severity, noting that a MinIO run "would settle interop"). The verifier ran it and MinIO fails the rule. Verdict: DOCUMENTED LIMITATION — the behaviour reproduces exactly as described and the strictness is written down at `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:13-16` ("An explicitly supplied prefix must be echoed with the same presence and bytes, every returned bucket name must remain in that prefix") — but it is counted as a confirmed finding because the documented rule has an interop consequence: the public convenience parameter `Prefix : Optional bucket-name prefix filter` (`client-buckets.ads:961`) raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` against MinIO, a server the compatibility matrix lists for ListBuckets at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:12` ("native/lightweight clients and s5cmd across the six-server matrix"), and no maintained document says so. Not raised higher because the rule is documented and the strict direction is deliberate: the client refuses to present an unfiltered page as a filtered one.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:4141-4148` — the echo rule, `(if Prepared.Requested_List_Buckets_Has_Prefix then not Page.Has_Prefix or else US.To_String (Page.Prefix) /= Requested_Prefix else Page.Has_Prefix and then US.Length (Page.Prefix) > 0)` followed by `raise Invalid_Response with "ListBuckets response does not match prepared request"` — and `:4150-4172`, the per-bucket prefix and region checks that follow; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.adb:191-197`, where the convenience overload sets `Has_Prefix => Prefix'Length > 0` at `:196`, so any nonempty prefix arms the rule; `:137-141`, where the convenience turns the resulting `Response_Invalid` into `raise Low_Level.Invalid_Response with "ListBuckets response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit"`, a message that names neither the prefix nor the server; the documented rule at `docs/qualification/list-buckets.md:13-16`; the matrix claim at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:12` and `:487-506`; and the real-server corpus call that would not notice, `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3182-3184`, which invokes `List_Page` with `Maximum => 1_000` and no prefix.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_client_buckets_3.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** the ListBuckets decoder requires a server that was sent `prefix=` to echo `<Prefix>` byte-for-byte. MinIO does not: `GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology` answers `200 OK` with a `ListAllMyBucketsResult` carrying `Owner`, one correctly filtered `Bucket`, and no `<Prefix>` element. The convenience overload therefore raises `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` and the composable overload reports `Response_Invalid` with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`, for a response that is a valid, correctly filtered page. Anyone calling the public `Prefix` parameter against MinIO hits it — and, by the same rule, against any server that filters without echoing. It is deterministic. The program replays MinIO's exact document from a scripted loopback origin and drives both the convenience `List_Page` (which raises) and the parameter-record `List_Page` (`LIST_BUCKETS_EXCHANGE_FAILED`, `CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE`); the control legs replay the identical document with `<Prefix>flyology</Prefix>` added and pass with `PAGE_AVAILABLE, status 200` and one bucket in the page, which isolates the echo rule from any parse error. The failure also occurs live against the container with no scripted bytes (`repro_client_buckets_probe.adb`): `List_Page (Prefix => flyology)` raises `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE`, the composable form reports `HTTP_Result = RESPONSE_INVALID`, and the no-prefix control on the same server returns `PAGE_AVAILABLE, status 200`.

**Notes:** The pinned botocore documentation embedded in `review/baseline/service-2.json` supports the rule for AWS ("If `Prefix` was sent with the request, it is included in the response"), but `Prefix` is in no `required` list, so the shape itself does not mandate the echo; the rule is a repository policy, not a model obligation. It is recorded as a limitation rather than a bug because the strictness is deliberate and documented; what is missing is that neither the compatibility matrix nor the `.ads` parameter documentation warns that the parameter is AWS-only in practice, and the diagnostic the caller receives ("response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit") names neither the prefix nor the server. No maintained gate would notice, because the real-server corpus calls `List_Page` only without a prefix (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3182-3184`). The fix is a maintainer decision between the two directions, and neither is an implementation detail: keep the strict echo rule and document the limitation — stating in `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and at `client-buckets.ads:961` that MinIO, and any server that filters without echoing, is unsupported for a prefixed `List_Page` — or relax the rule, accepting a missing `<Prefix>` while still enforcing that every returned name matches the requested prefix, which is a new public policy choice about what a filtered page is allowed to mean. Either way the diagnostic should name the real cause. It is counted as confirmed under the DOCUMENTED LIMITATION verdict because the choice is still open and a public parameter is unusable against a matrix-listed server until it is made.

---

## TRANSFERS: multi-subject transfer client and multipart planning and codecs (TRANSFERS-1)

The area covers `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ad?`, `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart.ad?`, `docs/architecture/client-transfers.md`, `tests/src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers-testing.ad?` and `tests/src/s3_transfer_many_corpus.adb`, with `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb`'s GetObject head validation, the loopback server and download lanes of `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb`, `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` and the pinned botocore model read as the reference.

### TRANSFERS-1: `Download_File` misdiagnoses a 200 answer to a `Range` request as an "unsolicited representation interval"

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2). The refusal itself is documented policy and only the operator-facing text is wrong, which is the rubric's "wrong diagnostics" band; it is not P1, because no valid input produces a wrong result and the documented contract that a successful 206 atomically publishes the selected interval is honoured.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb:1711-1716`, one `elsif` covering both `(Byte_Range_Header'Length = 0 and then Head.Status /= 200)` and `(Byte_Range_Header'Length > 0 and then Head.Status /= 206)` and raising `Low_Level.Invalid_Response with "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval"` for both — the literal appears at exactly one site in the repository; the head validator that cannot produce a better message at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5981-5987`; the contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1688-1689` and the public parameter with its empty default at `:1710`; the `Transfer_Many` call site at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.adb:663-669` and the message capture at `:710-716`; `Subject` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-transfers.ads:1731-1737`; existing lanes at `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:16935-16957` and `:16959-16978`, with the ranged request at `:16965`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_transfers_1.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; a loopback HTTP/1.1 server answers two GETs with 200 and the whole 27-byte body regardless of any `Range`, and a third with an unsolicited 206, and the program shows both events producing byte-identical messages.

**What is wrong:** `Download_File` uses one status gate for two opposite protocol events. When the caller passed no `Range` and the server answered 206, "GetObject returned an unsolicited representation interval" is exactly right; when the caller did pass a `Range` and the server answered 200 with the whole representation and no `Content-Range`, the same text is emitted and it is false in every particular — no interval was returned and nothing was unsolicited. RFC 9110 section 14.2 permits a server to ignore `Range` (recorded by the verifier as from memory and unverified), and a `Range`-stripping proxy or a cache produces exactly this answer in practice, so it is a legitimate server behaviour rather than a protocol violation. The consequence is diagnostic only: the destination file is left untouched, no temporary is left behind, and the refusal matches the documented range-mode contract. It is fully deterministic, being a pure function of `Byte_Range_Header'Length` and `Head.Status`.

**Notes:** One sub-claim of the discovery entry is refuted. It said the wrong text also lands in `Transfer_Result.Message` for a subject in `Transfer_Many`; it does not, because `Execute_Transfer` calls `Download_File` with an argument list ending at `Token` and `Subject` has no range field, so `Transfer_Many` can never pass a non-empty `Byte_Range_Header` and only the accurate first disjunct is reachable from it. The misleading text is reachable only through the direct `Download_File` entry point, which is public and is exercised by the maintained suite. No maintained lane sends a `Range` and answers 200, so the suite never observes the text, and no existing test asserts the wording. The fix needs no new public constant or parameter: split the `elsif` into two branches with two messages, keeping the existing text for the unrequested-206 case.

---

## S3-DOMAIN-A: the bucket-level typed XML codecs, their wire roots, their enumerations and their listing invariants (S3-DOMAIN-A-1 .. S3-DOMAIN-A-7)

The area covers the bucket-level codecs `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-{versions,listings,lifecycle,notifications,replication,versioning,buckets,inventory,metrics,analytics,intelligent_tiering}.ad?`, `-acl.ad?`, `-bucket_controls.ad?`, `-encryption.ad?`, `-website.ad?`, `-logging.ad?`, `-metadata_configurations.ad?` and `-metadata_tables.ad?`, the five generated units `-generated_{list_directory_buckets,put_bucket_acl,put_bucket_inventory_configuration,put_bucket_logging,put_bucket_website}_xml.ad?`, and the shared infrastructure they depend on: `-core.ads`, `-xml.adb`, `-xml_writers.adb`, `-paginated_rest_xml_reads.adb` and `-strict_xml_codecs.adb`, with the generator regions `tools/s3_codegen.py:805-830` and `:1922-1990`. They were read against the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json` (every shape named below dumped with python3), the callers `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` (`:6218-6250`, `:7233-7240`, `:8801-9100`, `:9595-9610`, `:10395-10440`, `:10758-10772`), `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ad?` (`:395-430`, `:2672-2775`, `:2855-2946`) and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1690-1700,2220-2230,9277,9319,9577,16793`, and the maintained documents `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and `docs/qualification/{list-objects-v1,list-object-versions,list-bucket-configurations,list-directory-buckets}.md`. Coverage was inspected in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb` (listing and versioning regions), `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb`, `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/*.tsv`, `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb`, `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb`, `tests/src/s3_put_bucket_lifecycle_configuration_corpus.adb`, `tests/src/s3_bucket_notification_configuration_corpus.adb` and `tests/scripts/test-{minio,rustfs,seaweedfs}.sh`. Where the pinned model is silent about a wire fact, the external authority is the AWS S3 API Reference page named per finding, fetched 2026-09-06.

The programs live in `tests/repros/src/` as `repro_s3_domain_a_1.adb` .. `_7.adb`, with `repro_s3_domain_a_5_live.adb` (a loopback holder that asserts nothing) and `repro_s3_domain_a_minio_probe.adb`; every one exits 1 on the current sources and carries at least one passing control. Live evidence came from one MinIO container started by hand from the digest pinned at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:5`, `quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, whose `--version` prints `minio version RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z (commit-id=07c3a429bfed433e49018cb0f78a52145d4bedeb)` — the provenance string that script checks at `:21-24` — published on `127.0.0.1::9000` as container `flyology-review-domain-a-8289` and removed at the end; and from three stock clients driven against this crate's own in-process S3 application. No repository test script was run, `alr` and `gprbuild` were never invoked directly, and nothing tracked was modified.

Two cross-area relations matter when filing. S3-DOMAIN-A-5 is the third instance of one class: the S3-DOMAIN-B findings S3-DOMAIN-B-1 (namespace-free `<Delete>` for DeleteObjects) and S3-DOMAIN-B-2 (namespace-free `<Tagging>` for PutBucketTagging) are the same defect in different request codecs, both P0, and the fix is the same class of change — let the request parser accept an unqualified root the way its lenient siblings already do — so the three should be decided together even if they are three commits. S3-DOMAIN-A-4's server-side half belongs to the SERVER area: the in-house server emits the same four wrong roots (`server-s3_applications.adb:9599-9608`, `:7237`) and `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:14` documents one of them as the server's contract, so client, generator, server and document must change together. The blind spot behind S3-DOMAIN-A-1 and S3-DOMAIN-A-7 is a single gate: the only caller of `Client_Objects.List_Versions_Page` in the implementation corpus sits inside `Require_Durable_Version_Routing`, which returns immediately unless `FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION` is `flyology-files` or `flyology-sqlite` (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3310-3333`, the gate at `:3318-3333`; the calls at `:3708`, `:3780`, `:3805`, `:3993`), so no MinIO, RustFS or SeaweedFS lane runs ListObjectVersions at all, which is why a Docker matrix that exercises MinIO on every change has never seen either finding. One MinIO `?versions` call added to that corpus would have caught both.

### S3-DOMAIN-A-1: ListObjectVersions decoder rejects every `Version` whose `StorageClass` is not `STANDARD`

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1, at Medium confidence, because the claim that real servers emit non-`STANDARD` classes there was memory). The verifier settled that part against a live server and kept the severity: it is a wrong result — an exception — on valid input through a reachable path, and it is not P0 only because `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67` ends the ListObjectVersions row with "no external interoperability claim", so no documented contract is broken. Were that disclaimer absent it would be P0 by the interop clause, since a stock server's valid 200 response is refused outright.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:798-805` (`Storage_Class_Field` calls `Valid_Enumeration (Value, Version_Member_Shape (5))` and raises `Malformed_Version_Listing` with "invalid ObjectVersion storage class"); `:354-361` (`Version_Member_Shape` resolves output member 6, `Versions`, then that list's member shape, then member N of `ObjectVersion`); `:364-380` (`Valid_Enumeration` returns False for anything outside the list and False when the shape carries no enumeration, with no permissive fallback); `:893-918` (`Finish_Version` additionally makes `Has_Storage_Class` mandatory at `:900`); `:1202-1216` (the server-side `Validate_Version` applies the same one-value enumeration, so the in-house server can never emit a page that would trip the decoder); the sibling that accepts all fifteen classes for the same object, `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:648-653`; the only decode path, `Parse_List_Object_Versions` at `versions.adb:1126-1150`; the client mapping to `Invalid_Response "malformed ListObjectVersions response"` at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2226-2229`; the existing assertion of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb:335-341` (`Expect_Bad (Page ("...<StorageClass>GLACIER</StorageClass>..."), "accepted unmodeled storage class")`) with the vector table that pins it, `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/members.tsv:30` ("Require exactly one pinned ObjectVersion storage class value."; the discovery report names `vectors.tsv:12`, LOV-RS-006, as the matching row); the documented contract at `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:90-91` ("exact pinned storage class") and the matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_1.adb`, exit status 1, five failed expectations; the live half is `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_minio_probe.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Finish_Field` validates every `<Version>/<StorageClass>` against the pinned one-value `ObjectVersionStorageClass` enumeration, so a page in which any single version reports a different class raises `Malformed_Version_Listing` and the client turns it into `Invalid_Response`: the entire page is lost, not just the offending entry, and there is no cursor with which to skip it. The pinned model is unambiguous — `ObjectVersion` member 5 is `StorageClass` with shape `ObjectVersionStorageClass`, which is `{"type": "string", "enum": ["STANDARD"]}`, while `ObjectStorageClass`, used by `Object` member 7, has fifteen values — and so is the AWS S3 API Reference, whose `API_ObjectVersion.html` StorageClass entry reads "Type: String / Valid Values: `STANDARD`", so the one-value enumeration is not a botocore artifact and the discovery report's "documentation artifact" argument does not hold. The finding rests on the pinned MinIO instead. That server accepts `x-amz-storage-class: REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` on PutObject with 200 (and rejects `STANDARD_IA`, `GLACIER`, `ONEZONE_IA` and `INTELLIGENT_TIERING` with `InvalidStorageClass`), and its `GET /bucket?versions` returns 200 with `<StorageClass>REDUCED_REDUNDANCY</StorageClass>` inside `<Version>`. Anyone calling `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` against MinIO therefore loses every listing of a bucket holding such an object; on AWS the same happens to lifecycle-transitioned versions. It is fully deterministic. The program decodes a minimal `ListVersionsResult` with one version once per storage class through `S3.Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions`, runs the same five classes through `S3.Listings.Parse_List_Objects` to show the crate accepts them there, then replays the exact `ListVersionsResult` body MinIO returned (1157 bytes, verbatim) and asserts both versions decode: `STANDARD` passes as the control, `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY`, `STANDARD_IA`, `GLACIER` and `INTELLIGENT_TIERING` are each `REJECTED, malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document`, all five decode through the ListObjects v1 path (`decoded, objects=1`), and the MinIO body is rejected the same way. Live, the probe reports `List_Versions_Page flyology-review-domain-a-versions: raised Invalid_Response: malformed ListObjectVersions response`.

**Notes:** This finding and S3-DOMAIN-A-7 both break ListObjectVersions against MinIO and must be fixed together: fixing S3-DOMAIN-A-1 alone leaves the operation just as unusable, because `Versions.Validate` rejects every untruncated MinIO page before any storage class is reached. The probe isolates them by listing a second MinIO bucket holding only a default-class object, which still raises. Neither has ever been exercised by CI, for the reason given in the area introduction: `Require_Durable_Version_Routing` (`tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3310-3333`) is the only implementation-corpus caller of `List_Versions_Page` and returns unless the implementation is a Flyology backend. The strictness is contract drift the maintainer chose, not an accident — `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:90-91` and `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/members.tsv:30` state it as intended — but the only place it is disclosed to a user is the matrix row's "no external interoperability claim", which does not name storage classes. A fix needs a maintainer decision: either the decoder accepts any non-empty `StorageClass` text, or it validates against the fifteen-value `ObjectStorageClass` used by the sibling codec; either choice widens a publicly observable acceptance domain, and the second also changes `Validate_Version` on the server side. `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb:335-341` and `tests/corpora/list-object-versions/members.tsv:30` assert the current behaviour and must change with it. `Finish_Version`'s requirement that `StorageClass` be present at all (`versions.adb:900`) is a second, smaller drift from a model that marks the member optional, and AWS documents `ObjectVersion.StorageClass` as `Required: No`.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-A-2: ListObjects v1 decoder rejects a truncated page that carries `NextMarker` without a delimiter

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1, at High confidence). The verifier corrected it upward. Unlike ListObjectVersions, `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65` makes an explicit external interoperability claim for ListObjects v1 — a "native/lightweight six-server oracle" — while the crate cannot read the first page of an ordinary undelimited listing from any of the three pinned third-party servers. That is an interop-breaking protocol violation of a claimed contract, and the CI lane that would have caught it is switched off rather than fixed.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:1093-1094` (`Effective_Delimiter : constant Boolean := Value.Has_Delimiter and then US.Length (Value.Delimiter) > 0;`, so an echoed empty `<Delimiter/>` cannot rescue the case); `:1108-1116`, the raise, `Value.Has_Next_Marker /= (Value.Is_Truncated and then Effective_Delimiter)` … `raise Malformed_Listing with "inconsistent S3 next marker";`; `:1144-1163` (`Parse_List_Objects` always calls `Validate`, with no bypass); the client mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1694-1695` (`Decode_List_Objects_Response` converts it to `Invalid_Response`); the client's existing marker fallback at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.adb:403-415`, which already derives the marker from the last key when `NextMarker` is absent; the oracle-mode switch-off at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:258-274`, where `Check_List_Objects_V1_Pagination` returns `False` for the three `*-next-marker-without-delimiter` modes (the skip at `:262-270`) and `True` when the variable is unset, driven from `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:66-67` (`FLYOLOGY_LIST_OBJECTS_V1_ORACLE_MODE=minio-2025-next-marker-without-delimiter`), `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:71` and `tests/scripts/test-seaweedfs.sh:78`; the qualification paragraph `docs/qualification/list-objects-v1.md:54-58`; the matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65`; the existing assertion of the wrong behaviour for the final-page variant at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9597-9599`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_2.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations; the live half is `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_minio_probe.adb`.

**What is wrong:** `Listings.Validate` treats "AWS only sends `NextMarker` when a delimiter was requested" as a wire rule and raises when a truncated page carries the element without a non-empty `<Delimiter>`. The pinned model imposes no such constraint — `ListObjectsOutput` has no `required` list and `NextMarker` is a plain optional string — and the rule the model's own documentation carries is advice to a client, not a constraint on a server: "This element is returned only if you have the `delimiter` request parameter specified. If the response does not include the `NextMarker` element and it is truncated, you can use the value of the last `Key` element … as the `marker` parameter". The AWS API Reference for ListObjects says the same and lists no error for it. MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS all emit `NextMarker` unconditionally: against the pinned MinIO, three keys with `max-keys=1` and no delimiter return 200 with `<IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>` and `<NextMarker>k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]</NextMarker>` and no `<Delimiter>`, while the same request with `delimiter=/` returns the same `NextMarker` plus `<Delimiter>/</Delimiter>`. So a Flyology client paging any bucket with more than `max-keys` objects and no delimiter against those servers loses the very first page and pagination never starts; this is the common case, not an edge case, and it is deterministic. The program puts four documents through `S3.Listings.Parse_List_Objects` — the verbatim MinIO page with `delimiter=/` (control, `decoded, objects=1 truncated=TRUE has_next_marker=TRUE next_marker="k1[minio_cache:v2,return:]"`), a truncated no-delimiter page without `NextMarker` as the Flyology server emits it (control, `decoded`), the verbatim MinIO page without a delimiter, and a minimal synthetic equivalent, the last two both `REJECTED, inconsistent S3 next marker`; the two MinIO bodies differ by exactly one element. Live, the probe reports `List_V1_Page max-keys=1 delimiter=/: kind PAGE_AVAILABLE status 200` against `List_V1_Page max-keys=1 no delimiter: raised Invalid_Response: ListObjects response is invalid or exceeds the XML limit`.

**Notes:** The repository already knows about the behaviour and has switched the check off rather than fixed it, which is what makes this P0 rather than a documented limitation. The switch-off lives in two places that must be retired with the fix: the three Docker scripts set the oracle-mode variable (`tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:66-67`, `test-rustfs.sh:71`, `test-seaweedfs.sh:78`) and `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:258-274` reads it, `Check_List_Objects_V1_Pagination` returning `False` for the three `*-next-marker-without-delimiter` values at `:262-270` so the subcase is skipped, while `docs/compatibility/s3.md:65` still advertises the six-server oracle and `docs/qualification/list-objects-v1.md:54-58` records the exclusion in prose. The fix itself is small and needs no new public value: accept `NextMarker` whenever the page is truncated and keep rejecting it on a final page; the client's fallback at `client-objects.adb:403-415` already handles both shapes. `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:9597-9599` asserts the final-page rejection and stays correct, but the truncated no-delimiter case has no test and should gain one. Refutations that failed: an echoed empty `<Delimiter/>` does not satisfy `Effective_Delimiter`; the model does not impose the rule; and the failure is not confined to a later page, since the first page of a three-key bucket with `max-keys=1` is already rejected.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-A-3: Lifecycle and Notifications serializers reject U+007F that XML 1.0, their own parsers and the Replication serializer accept

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report also said P2). It needs an unusual byte in a configuration value and produces a clean exception before any request is sent; no data is lost and no server sees a bad document. The verifier found no reachable caller that would hit it accidentally.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:789-790` (`if First in 16#20# .. 16#7E# or else First in 16#09# | 16#0A# | 16#0D#`) and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-notifications.adb:503-504` (identical range), against `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:828-829`, which is one byte wider (`16#20# .. 16#7F#`), and the shared escape boundary at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-xml.adb:221-226`, which rejects only `Character'Pos (Item) < 32` other than TAB, LF and CR; the raise sites `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-lifecycle.adb:852-863` (`Malformed_Lifecycle "invalid lifecycle XML text"`, raised at `:854`) and `-notifications.adb:566-577` (`Malformed_Notification "invalid bucket-notification XML text"`); the comment that justifies the range at `lifecycle.adb:770-773`; the crate's own key domain, `Valid_Object_Key` at `src/flyology-object_storage.adb:479-490`, which rejects only NUL.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_3.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** Two of three otherwise identical UTF-8 validators stop the single-byte range one code point early, at 0x7E instead of 0x7F, so a lifecycle or notification configuration containing DEL in any text member (`ID`, `Prefix`, tag `Key`/`Value`, `Id`, filter values) can be read from a server but never written back. The stated rationale does not apply: the comment says the validator "keeps the serializer from emitting bytes the matching parser must reject", but U+007F is not a C0 control, XML 1.0's `Char` production includes it, the matching parser accepts it, and the same value survives `Replication.Serialize` and every `XML_Writers`-based codec. The program parses a minimal valid configuration for each of Lifecycle, Notifications and Replication and hands the parsed value straight back to `Serialize`, once with a plain ASCII `ID`/`Id` and once with U+007F: the three plain-ASCII cases round-trip, replication with U+007F round-trips (`Parse accepted (210 bytes)`, `Serialize produced 258 bytes`) and is the key control, while `lifecycle ID with U+007F` and `notifications Id with U+007F` both parse (`Parse accepted (139 bytes)`, `(195 bytes)`) and then fail at `Serialize` with `invalid lifecycle XML text` and `invalid bucket-notification XML text`. A final case shows the boundary is not about control characters at all: U+0080, an actual C1 control, serializes through Lifecycle without complaint.

**Notes:** Whoever hits it is a caller round-tripping a configuration whose prefix or tag came from an object key containing DEL, which the crate's own `Valid_Object_Key` permits. It is deterministic. No existing test covers U+007F: `tests/src/s3_put_bucket_lifecycle_configuration_corpus.adb` and `tests/src/s3_bucket_notification_configuration_corpus.adb` exercise C0 controls and invalid UTF-8 only. The fix is to change `16#7E#` to `16#7F#` in the two bodies, matching `replication.adb:828`; no public value changes and no maintainer decision is needed beyond accepting the widened byte range. Refutations that failed: `S3.XML.Escape_Text` does not reject DEL, so the validators are not a harmless front-run; the key domain does not exclude DEL; and all three validators carry the same "raw C0 controls" comment, so the divergence is not deliberate.

### S3-DOMAIN-A-4: four response decoders require the botocore output *shape* name as the XML root

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report also said P1, at Medium confidence because the AWS wire root names were memory). The verifier settled the names from the AWS S3 API Reference and kept the severity: the decoders cannot read a response from the only provider that implements the operations, which is a wrong result on valid input through a reachable path (`client-low_level.adb:9277`, `:9319`, `:9577`, `:16793`). It is not P0 because `docs/compatibility/s3.md:36-40` ends each of those rows with "external interoperability are not claimed", so nothing documented is violated on the client side — although `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:14` positively documents the non-AWS root `<ListDirectoryBucketsOutput/>` as the Flyology server's contract.

**Location:** the four `Root_Name` generic actuals at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-inventory.adb:607-613`, `-metrics.adb:468-472`, `-analytics.adb:572-576` and `-intelligent_tiering.adb:528-532`, each preceded by the comment "Both element names are fixed by the pinned Botocore operation/output model."; the enforcement at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-paginated_rest_xml_reads.adb:133-136` (open) and `:283-286` (close), exact comparison both times; the generated decoder `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:9-13` (`when Root => return "ListDirectoryBucketsOutput";`) with its message-less re-raise at `:272-274`; the strict runtime's check at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-strict_xml_codecs.adb:312-315`; the generator rule at `tools/s3_codegen.py:815-816` (`root_name = shapes[root_shape].get("locationName", root_shape)`), the same rule for request payloads at `:1932`; the places where the crate already hard-codes the true wire root, `buckets.adb:914` (`ListAllMyBucketsResult`), `listings.adb:690` (`ListBucketResult`), `versions.adb:528` (`ListVersionsResult`), `metadata_configurations.adb:211` and `metadata_tables.adb:295`; the server emitters `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:9599-9608` and `:7237`; the qualification sentence `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:14`; the matrix rows `docs/compatibility/s3.md:36-40`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_4.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** The generator's rule "root element = payload shape's `locationName`, else the shape name" is right for request payloads, whose members carry a `locationName`, and wrong for responses, whose output shapes carry nothing: botocore's rest-xml parser ignores the root element name, so the model has never needed to record it. Dumped with python3, none of `ListBucketInventoryConfigurationsOutput`, `ListBucketMetricsConfigurationsOutput`, `ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationsOutput`, `ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput`, `ListDirectoryBucketsOutput`, `ListBucketsOutput`, `ListObjectVersionsOutput` or `ListObjectsOutput` carries a `locationName`, a `resultWrapper`, an `xmlNamespace` or a `payload` — the model is silent for every one of them, including the three whose wire roots the crate already hard-codes correctly, so the model cannot be the authority the comments claim. The AWS S3 API Reference, read from both the Response Syntax block and the Response Elements root-level-tag entry (three of the four also confirmed by a worked sample response on the same page), documents `<ListInventoryConfigurationsResult>` for ListBucketInventoryConfigurations, `<ListMetricsConfigurationsResult>` for ListBucketMetricsConfigurations, `<ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurationResult>` (singular "Configuration") for ListBucketAnalyticsConfigurations and `<ListAllMyDirectoryBucketsResult>` for ListDirectoryBuckets, while `API_ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurations.html` really does document the shape name `<ListBucketIntelligentTieringConfigurationsOutput>`, which is presumably why the pattern looked safe. Anyone calling the four affected operations against AWS gets an invalid-response failure on every 200; it is deterministic and total. The program feeds each of `Inventory.Parse_List`, `Metrics.Parse_List`, `Analytics.Parse_List`, `Intelligent_Tiering.Parse_List` and `Generated_List_Directory_Buckets_XML.Parse` two documents differing only in the root name: the shape-name roots are accepted (recorded, not asserted), Intelligent-Tiering passes as the control, and the four documented roots are `REJECTED, malformed inventory-list XML`, `malformed metrics-list XML`, `malformed analytics-list XML` and, for ListDirectoryBuckets, a bare source location `flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:274`.

**Notes:** The pinned MinIO cannot settle this: `GET /bucket?inventory`, `?metrics`, `?analytics` and `?intelligent-tiering` all answer 501 `NotImplemented` with an `<Error>` root, so AWS is the only provider that implements the operations and the API Reference is the only authority. No in-repository test can catch it either, because the in-house server emits the same wrong names and the qualification document blesses one of them, so a fix must change the generator's response-root rule, the four decoders, the server emitters (SERVER area) and `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:14` together. It needs a maintainer decision on where the four wire root names live: they are externally mandated protocol values with no model source, so under the "Decision authority" rule their authority must be recorded at the declaration — most plausibly an explicit response-root table in `tools/s3_codegen.py` citing the AWS API Reference, rather than four more hand-written string literals. Separately, `Generated_List_Directory_Buckets_XML.Parse` re-raises its inner exception with no message (`generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:272-274`, `raise Malformed_Document;`), which is why that leg prints a source location where the other three print a diagnostic.

### S3-DOMAIN-A-5: the server-side PutBucketVersioning body parser is the only request codec that rejects an unqualified root

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P2 by repository evidence, and P0 if a stock client were confirmed to send the body without `xmlns`). The condition it named is met. `mc version enable` — MinIO's own CLI, the default way to turn versioning on against any S3 endpoint — sends `<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>` with a correct `Content-MD5` and is answered 400 `MalformedXML`, against a row (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:57`) that claims an "authenticated path-style server" and a "configuration-only six-server oracle". There is no client-side workaround: `mc` does not offer to add a namespace.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.adb:41-55` (`Start_Element_Details` raises `Malformed_Configuration` with "bucket versioning namespace or attributes are invalid" when the namespace is not the S3 one and `Allow_Empty_Namespace` is False; the test itself at `:47-54`); `:171-186` (`Parse` passes `Allow_Empty_Namespace => False`, `Parse_Response` passes `True`); the server call at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10421-10425`, which invokes the strict `Versioning.Parse (Document)` after the Content-MD5 and checksum gates at `:10395-10420`; the lenient siblings `lifecycle.adb:63-85` (`Unqualified` is an accepted `Namespace_Style`), `notifications.adb:161-181` (identical) and `buckets.adb:531-545` (`Parse_Create_Configuration` has no namespace check at all; `grep -n Namespace_URI src/flyology-object_storage-s3-buckets.adb` returns nothing); the crate's own documentation at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versioning.ads:29-32` ("AWS responses use the S3 namespace, while a few otherwise compatible implementations omit it"); the existing assertions at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:21868-21871` (`Expect_Malformed ("<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>", "namespace-free versioning request accepted")`) and `:21879-21886` (`Parse_Response` accepts the same bytes); the matrix row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:57`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_5.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations, with `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_5_live.adb` as the loopback holder used for the stock-client capture (it asserts nothing).

**What is wrong:** `Versioning.Parse` is the only request-body codec in the crate that treats the S3 namespace as a wire precondition, and the server calls exactly that one, so a `PUT /bucket?versioning` whose body omits `xmlns` gets 400 `MalformedXML` while the same client's lifecycle, notification and CreateBucket bodies succeed. The pinned model does not support the rule: `PutBucketVersioningRequest`'s `VersioningConfiguration` payload member carries `xmlNamespace`, which is what a serializer emits, and nothing marks it a parsing precondition. Three stock clients were driven at the in-process application with `PutBucketVersioning` and `Status=Enabled`. boto3 / botocore 1.43.89 and aws-cli v2 2.36.34 (Python 3.14.7) both send `<VersioningConfiguration xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>` and are rejected 400 `InvalidRequest` for the checksum reason (`requires one valid Content-MD5 header`), not the namespace, so both are unaffected by this finding. MinIO `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z (minio-go/v7.0.90, image `minio/mc@sha256:a7fe349ef4bd8521fb8497f55c6042871b2ae640607cf99d9bede5e9bdf11727`) sends `<VersioningConfiguration><Status>Enabled</Status></VersioningConfiguration>`, 75 bytes, with no `xmlns`, and is answered 400 `MalformedXML`. `mc` runs inside Docker, so it reached the loopback listener through a plain logging TCP relay on `0.0.0.0`; the capture is of the real socket: `PUT /stock-versioning-3/?versioning= HTTP/1.1`, `Host: host.docker.internal:58080`, `User-Agent: MinIO (linux; arm64) minio-go/v7.0.90 mc/RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z`, `Content-Length: 75`, `Content-Md5: 8qj8HSeDu3APPMQZVG06WQ==`, `X-Amz-Date: 20260907T063040Z`, that body, and the reply `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request`, `Content-Length: 235`, `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?><Error><Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message><Resource>/stock-versioning-3/?versioning=</Resource></Error>`. `mc` reports `mc: <ERROR> Unable to enable versioning. The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema`. Since `mc` supplied a correct `Content-MD5` it cleared every checksum gate, so the absent namespace is the only reason for the rejection, and `mc version enable alias/bucket` cannot enable versioning on a Flyology server at all. It is deterministic and affects every minio-go-based tool, which is the largest non-AWS S3 client family. The program's first part runs the four namespace-free request bodies through their own parsers side by side — `Lifecycle.Parse`, `Notifications.Parse`, `Buckets.Parse_Create_Configuration` and `Versioning.Parse_Response` all accept, `Versioning.Parse` is `REJECTED, malformed bucket versioning XML` — and its second part sends signed requests through the application: the namespaced `?versioning` body is accepted (200, control), a second `CreateBucket` succeeds (200, control), the exact 75-byte `mc` body returns 400 with the `MalformedXML` document above, and the follow-up `GET /domain-a-5-other?versioning` returns 200 with `<VersioningConfiguration xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"></VersioningConfiguration>`, showing the bucket never became versioned.

**Notes:** This sits beside the S3-DOMAIN-B namespace findings, S3-DOMAIN-B-1 (DeleteObjects refusing a namespace-free `<Delete>`) and S3-DOMAIN-B-2 (PutBucketTagging refusing a namespace-free `<Tagging>` that PutObjectTagging accepts), both also raised to P0 on stock-client captures; the fix is the same class of change in all three, namely letting the request parser accept an unqualified root the way the lenient sibling codecs already do, so the three should be settled as one decision even if they are filed and fixed separately. Here the leniency already exists and is simply not wired to the server path: `Parse_Document` already takes `Allow_Empty_Namespace`, and `Parse_Response` already passes `True`, so the minimal fix introduces no new public value; the only sense in which it needs a maintainer decision is that it widens the server's accepted request domain. `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:21868-21871` asserts the current rejection and must change with the fix, while `:21879-21886` stays correct. The strictness is not disclosed anywhere a client would look: the `.ads` documents "fail closed" for `Parse`, but neither `docs/compatibility/s3.md:57` nor `docs/qualification/put-bucket-versioning.md` names the namespace as a client requirement.
