### S3-DOMAIN-A-6 (new): the listing query parsers reject `max-keys` above 1000 instead of capping the page

**Severity:** P1 (new; the verifier promoted it from the discovery report's first uncounted observation, which called it a documented policy value and marked its interop consequence "from memory, unverified"). It is neither documented as a wire rule nor consistent with AWS or MinIO, and a stock client hits it: a valid request is refused. It is not P0 because no default client invocation reaches it — the user must explicitly ask for more than 1000 — the caller can work around it by asking for 1000 or fewer, and the refusal is an honest 400 with a correct error code, losing no data.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:111-118` (v1), `:223-230` (v2) and `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:134-141`, all three of the form `if Seen_Max_Keys or else not Number.Valid or else Number.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last then raise Malformed_List_Request with "invalid … max-keys";`; the bound itself at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:28`, `subtype Page_Size is Natural range 0 .. 1_000;`, a visible subtype; the server mapping of `Malformed_List_Request` to 400 `InvalidArgument` at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:10763-10767` and the two neighbouring handlers; the backend page contract at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:863` ("max-keys 0..1000"), which is not a request-validity rule; the client side, where `Low_Level.List_Objects_Parameters.Max_Keys` is `S3.Core.Page_Size` so a Flyology client cannot even express the value.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_6.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** The three listing query parsers reuse the response page bound `S3.Core.Page_Size'Last` as a request validity bound, so a request AWS documents as valid and MinIO answers with 200 is refused with 400 `InvalidArgument`. The AWS S3 API Reference is explicit that the parameter is a ceiling on the response, not a constraint on the request — `API_ListObjectsV2.html`, URI request parameters, `max-keys`: "Sets the maximum number of keys returned in the response. By default, the action returns up to 1,000 key names. The response might contain fewer keys but will never contain more." — the same wording appears for ListObjects and ListObjectVersions, and the `Errors` section of ListObjectsV2 lists only `NoSuchBucket`. The pinned MinIO answers `?max-keys=2000` and `?list-type=2&max-keys=2000` with 200, echoing `<MaxKeys>2000</MaxKeys>`. Against this crate's own application, aws-cli 2.36.34 gets `An error occurred (InvalidArgument) when calling the ListObjectsV2 operation: The ListObjectsV2 request is invalid` for `--max-keys 2000`, the same for `list-objects --max-keys 5000` and `list-object-versions --max-keys 2000`, while `list-objects-v2 --max-keys 1000` returns 200 with `"MaxKeys": 1000`; the same three commands against the pinned MinIO return 200. Anyone pointing a stock tool at a Flyology endpoint with an explicit large page hint gets a hard failure instead of a 1000-key page, and SDK paginators that pass a caller-supplied `MaxKeys` through do the same. The program sends six signed GETs through the in-process application: `?max-keys=1000`, `?list-type=2&max-keys=1000` and `?versions&max-keys=1000` all return 200 as controls, and the same three at 2000 each return 400 with an `InvalidArgument` body naming ListObjects, ListObjectsV2 and ListObjectVersions respectively. It is deterministic and affects the server only.

**Notes:** This needs a maintainer decision because it touches the visible subtype `S3.Core.Page_Size` (`core.ads:28`), which the "Ada interface values" rule puts under explicit approval: either the three query parsers clamp an oversized `max-keys` to `Page_Size'Last` and leave the subtype alone, which keeps every response bound and every backend contract intact and is exactly what AWS's own wording describes, or the request-side bound becomes a separate, wider type. The verification report recommends the clamp in the parsers, on the ground that it changes no public declaration. `docs/compatibility/s3.md:863` describes the backend page contract rather than the accepted request domain, and the two listing qualification documents mention 1000 only as the modeled default, so this is not a documented limitation. No existing test asserts the current rejection for values above 1000 in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`; the fix should gain one.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-A-7 (new): `Versions.Validate` rejects every untruncated MinIO ListObjectVersions page, whatever the storage classes

**Severity:** P1 (new; the verifier found it while checking S3-DOMAIN-A-1 against a real server, and promoted it from the discovery report's fourth uncounted observation, which raised the mirror direction — `NextKeyMarker` present with an empty `NextVersionIdMarker` — and left it uninvestigated). Valid input raises through a reachable path, and as with S3-DOMAIN-A-1 it is not P0 because `docs/compatibility/s3.md:67` disclaims external interoperability for ListObjectVersions. In reach it is strictly worse than S3-DOMAIN-A-1: 100 per cent of untruncated MinIO pages fail, not only buckets containing a non-`STANDARD` version.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-versions.adb:1031-1041`, whose first disjunct is `Value.Has_Next_Version_ID_Marker /= Value.Has_Next_Key_Marker`, followed by `or else Value.Is_Truncated /= Value.Has_Next_Key_Marker` and the non-emptiness pair, raising `Malformed_Version_Listing` with "inconsistent ListObjectVersions next markers"; the client mapping at `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:2226-2229`; the coverage gate at `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3318-3333`; the documented invariant at `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:87-89` ("truncated pages require both nonempty next markers, while final pages reject either one") and the corpus that enforces it, `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_7.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation; the live half is `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_a_minio_probe.adb`.

**What is wrong:** `Versions.Validate` requires the two next-page markers to be present together and to match `IsTruncated`, a pairing the pinned model does not express — `ListObjectVersionsOutput` has no `required` list and `NextKeyMarker` and `NextVersionIdMarker` are plain optional strings with no cross-member constraint — and the AWS S3 API Reference does not require either, documenting both only as "When the number of responses exceeds the value of `MaxKeys`, … specifies the first … not returned", with no rule against emitting one, or an empty one, on an untruncated page. The pinned MinIO emits an empty `<NextVersionIdMarker></NextVersionIdMarker>` on every `?versions` response, including untruncated ones, and omits `<NextKeyMarker>` there, so `Has_Next_Version_ID_Marker` is True while `Has_Next_Key_Marker` is False, the first disjunct fires, and every page is rejected; its truncated pages carry both markers non-empty and are accepted. The client turns the exception into `Invalid_Response` and `Client.Objects.List_Versions_Page` returns nothing, so ListObjectVersions is completely unusable against MinIO through the high-level client, independently of S3-DOMAIN-A-1. It is deterministic. The program puts three documents through `S3.Versions.Parse_List_Object_Versions`, every `StorageClass` in them `STANDARD` so the storage-class rule cannot interfere: MinIO's untruncated envelope with the empty `NextVersionIdMarker` removed (control, `decoded, versions=1 truncated=FALSE`), MinIO's truncated envelope with both markers non-empty (control, `decoded, versions=1 truncated=TRUE`) and MinIO's actual untruncated envelope (`REJECTED, inconsistent ListObjectVersions next markers`). Live, the probe lists a second MinIO bucket holding only a default-class object and `List_Versions_Page flyology-review-domain-a-standard` still raises `Invalid_Response: malformed ListObjectVersions response`, which isolates this finding from S3-DOMAIN-A-1.

**Notes:** This finding and S3-DOMAIN-A-1 both break ListObjectVersions against MinIO and must be fixed together; fixing S3-DOMAIN-A-7 alone still loses any page containing a `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` version, and fixing S3-DOMAIN-A-1 alone changes nothing at all against MinIO, since every page fails here first. Nothing in the repository catches either, because no third-party lane runs ListObjectVersions: `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:3318-3333` gates the corpus's only `List_Versions_Page` caller on `FLYOLOGY_S3_IMPLEMENTATION` being `flyology-files` or `flyology-sqlite`, and the in-house server never emits a lone marker. A fix needs a maintainer decision about how much presence structure the decoder should require of a foreign server: the narrowest change that admits MinIO is to key the rule on truncation and non-emptiness — require both markers non-empty when `IsTruncated` is true, and ignore a present-but-empty marker otherwise — but that relaxes the invariant documented at `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:87-89`, so the qualification text and `tests/src/s3_list_object_versions_corpus.adb` must change with it. The direction the discovery report named, a present `NextKeyMarker` with an empty `NextVersionIdMarker`, is not reproducible against MinIO, whose truncated pages always carry both markers non-empty.

---

## S3-DOMAIN-B: object-level typed codecs and the shared tag vocabulary (S3-DOMAIN-B-1 .. S3-DOMAIN-B-9)

The area covers the object-level S3 codecs `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-{errors,copies,object_reads,tagging,deletions,object_lock,multipart_uploads,attributes,annotations}.ad?` and `src/flyology-object_storage-tags.ad?`, read against their `.ads` contracts together with `-checksum_policy.ads`, `-sigv4_encoding.ad?`, `-core.ad?`, `-xml.ads`, `-model.ads` and the generated descriptor `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.adb:45580-45605`, the pinned botocore model `review/baseline/service-2.json` (dumped with python3), `docs/compatibility/s3.md` and `docs/qualification/{delete-objects,copy-object,object-tagging,bucket-tagging,get-object-attributes,object-annotations-backend-server}.md`. Call sites were confirmed in `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb` (DeleteObjects, PutBucketTagging, PutObjectTagging, `x-amz-tagging`, ListMultipartUploads, GetObjectAttributes, annotation routing and the authentication error map) and `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.ad?` (the annotation request builder, model query emission, and the GetObjectAttributes and GetBucketTagging decoders). Coverage was inspected in `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb`, `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:13400-13600`, `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:12640-12700`, `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:300-340` and `:1675-1812`, `tests/src/s3_list_object_annotations_qualification.adb:50-83`, `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb` and `tests/src/s3_memory_server.adb`. The programs are `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_1.adb` .. `repro_s3_domain_b_9.adb`; they drive the prebuilt, tracked `tests/bin/s3_memory_server`, started by hand on 127.0.0.1 with `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=AKIDEXAMPLE`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=wJalrXUtnFEMI/K7MDENG+bPxRfiCYEXAMPLEKEY` and `AWS_REGION=us-east-1`, and stopped afterwards; no `alr` or `gprbuild` was run. The stock-client bodies were read out of a logging TCP proxy on `0.0.0.0:62943` forwarding byte-for-byte to that server's `PORT 62942`, which also let a Docker `mc` container reach the loopback-only listener through `host.docker.internal`; the clients were boto3 1.43.89 with botocore 1.43.89 (uv, CPython 3.13.7), aws-cli/2.36.34 Python/3.14.7, s3cmd 2.4.0 (uv, CPython 3.13) and `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z with minio-go v7.0.90 (image `minio/mc@sha256:a7fe349ef4bd8521fb8497f55c6042871b2ae640607cf99d9bede5e9bdf11727`, not pinned by the repository, run with `--rm`). The oracle was one MinIO container started by hand from the image pinned in `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` (`quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`), stopped and removed at the end; `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` itself was not run, because it would have invoked `alr -n build`. Out of area and not re-reviewed here: the ListParts, CompleteMultipartUpload, InitiateMultipartUpload and `CopyPartResult` codecs live in `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart.ad?` (S3-DOMAIN-A), `Content-Range` and `Range` parsing live in `s3-core.adb` and `client-low_level.adb`, the S3 XML parser itself belongs to WIRE, and the mandatory `Content-MD5` on the `requestChecksumRequired` routes is SERVER-1.

### S3-DOMAIN-B-1: DeleteObjects rejects a namespace-free `<Delete>` body, so `mc` and `s3cmd` cannot batch-delete anything

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1, with High confidence in the code path and the model but only Medium confidence, from memory, about which stock clients omit `xmlns`). The verifier raised it after driving both named clients at the real application and capturing their bodies off the wire: `mc` RELEASE.2025-08-13T08-35-41Z (minio-go v7.0.90) and s3cmd 2.4.0 both send a namespace-free `<Delete>` **with a correct Content-MD5** by default and are answered `400 MalformedXML`, while MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` answers `200` for the identical bytes. That is the rubric's "interop-breaking protocol violation that a stock client or server rejects": batch delete is completely unavailable from these clients and nothing is deleted.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.adb:66-79` (`Delete_Handler.Start_Element_Details`, which raises `Malformed_Delete` unless `Namespace_URI = "http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"`; it is an override on the handler, so it runs for **every** element and not only the root) and `:380-393` (`Parse_Request` calls `XML.Parse` then `Validate`, and the handler exception surfaces through the `when XML.XML_Error` branch as `Malformed_Delete "malformed DeleteObjects XML"`); `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:17730-17741`, where the request body is passed verbatim to `Deletions.Parse_Request` with no namespace normalisation anywhere in between, and `:17959-17964`, `when Deletions.Malformed_Delete => Send_Error (X, 400, "MalformedXML", ...)`; the contract that promises no such rule, `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-deletions.ads:76-82` ("Parse one bounded DeleteObjects request document"); the pinned model, in which the `Delete` shape carries no `xmlNamespace` and only the `DeleteObjectsRequest.Delete` member does; the existing assertion of the wrong behaviour at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11450-11452`, `Must_Reject_Request ("<Delete><Object><Key>k</Key></Object></Delete>", …)`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_1.adb`, exit status 1, five failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Delete_Handler.Start_Element_Details` requires the exact S3 namespace URI on every element of a DeleteObjects request body. In the pinned model the namespace is a *serializer* trait on the `DeleteObjectsRequest.Delete` member, not a constraint on the `Delete` shape, so receivers must not demand it, and AWS and MinIO do not. minio-go marshals `<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>m1</Key></Object><Object><Key>m2</Key></Object><Object><Key>m3</Key></Object></Delete>` with `Content-Md5: 31pfUAHQbxJHn3iLLTGlGg==`, and s3cmd concatenates `<Delete><Object><Key>k1</Key></Object><Object><Key>k2</Key></Object></Delete>` with `Content-MD5` present; both were captured off the wire from `mc rm --recursive --force` and `s3cmd rm --recursive --force` against this server, and both are answered `400 MalformedXML`. `mc rm --recursive`, `mc rb --force` and `s3cmd rm --recursive` therefore cannot delete anything from a Flyology endpoint, while the same commands work against MinIO and AWS. The rejection was isolated to the namespace by replaying the same bytes over plain HTTP with a correct SigV4 header signature, a real payload hash and `Content-MD5` (botocore `SigV4Auth`), so that neither SERVER-1 nor the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD rule applies: `DeleteObjects mc body (no xmlns): 400 <Code>MalformedXML</Code>`, `same body + xmlns: 200 <DeleteResult ...><Deleted><Key>m1</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>`, `s3cmd DeleteObjects body (no xmlns): 400 <Code>MalformedXML</Code>`. The same namespace-free body sent to MinIO answers `200 <DeleteResult xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Deleted><Key>single-crc</Key></Deleted></DeleteResult>`. The program creates a bucket and `k1`/`k2`/`k3`, calls `Deletions.Parse_Request` on `mc`'s captured document (which raises `Malformed_Delete: malformed DeleteObjects XML`), then sends `mc`'s `<Delete><Quiet>false</Quiet><Object><Key>k1</Key></Object></Delete>` and s3cmd's `<Delete><Object><Key>k2</Key></Object></Delete>` as signed `POST /domain-b1?delete` requests with a correct `Content-MD5`, both answered `400` with `<Code>MalformedXML</Code><Message>The XML provided was not well-formed or did not validate against the published schema</Message>`; the boto3/aws-cli control `<Delete xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"><Object><Key>k3</Key></Object><Quiet>false</Quiet></Delete>` passes with `200` and a `<Deleted><Key>k3</Key></Deleted>` entry, which is what isolates the namespace inside the program as well. It is deterministic and fires on every batch.

**Notes:** This finding and S3-DOMAIN-B-2 are the same change class as S3-DOMAIN-A-5, the server-side PutBucketVersioning body parser that is the only request codec rejecting an unqualified root: three request codecs in two areas enforce a namespace the pinned model places on the member as a serializer instruction, and a single decision about receiver-side namespace tolerance settles all three, so they should be sequenced together. This finding also meets SERVER-1 on the same route, and the order of rejection matters for both fixes: current boto3 and aws-cli send `x-amz-checksum-crc32` instead of `Content-MD5`, so SERVER-1's integrity gate stops them one step *earlier* and they never reach the namespace check, while `mc` and `s3cmd` do send `Content-MD5`, so for them the namespace check is the first and only rejection. Today no client can batch-delete at all; fixing SERVER-1 alone would still leave `mc` and `s3cmd` broken here, and fixing this alone would still leave boto3 and aws-cli broken there. `docs/qualification/delete-objects.md` documents the strictness, so it is deliberate rather than accidental, but it is not an S3 protocol rule and MinIO accepts what this server refuses, so it is not a documented limitation. `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11450-11452` pins the wrong behaviour and would have to be inverted with the fix. The maintainer decision a fix needs is equivalent to the one already taken for bucket tagging: either accept an absent namespace on request parsing along the `Allow_Empty_Namespace` pattern of `tagging.adb:434-448`, which would be a new public parameter or a second entry point, or accept it unconditionally; foreign namespaces and attributes can remain invalid either way. Refutation attempts that failed: there is no tolerant request-side entry point in `Deletions` analogous to `Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response`; the XML layer does not supply the S3 namespace implicitly for an unqualified document, which the program's unit-level call shows; and the rejection is not some other property of those bodies (`<Quiet>` first, or no `<Quiet>` at all), because adding only `xmlns` turns each of them into a `200`.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-B-2: PutBucketTagging rejects a namespace-free `<Tagging>` body that PutObjectTagging accepts

**Severity:** P0 (the discovery report said P1 with Medium confidence, the stock-client claim being from memory). The verifier raised it for the same interop reason as S3-DOMAIN-B-1 and confirmed the client sub-claim directly: minio-go really does marshal `<Tagging>` with no namespace, captured off the wire from `mc tag set` for **both** the bucket and the object form, as `<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>team</Key><Value>storage</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>`. That `mc`'s default PutBucketTagging body is rejected *for the namespace* was proven by replaying its exact bytes with a valid signature and `Content-MD5` (`400 <Code>MalformedXML</Code>`) and then adding only `xmlns` (`200`); MinIO accepts the identical body with `200`.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:434-448` (`Bucket_Tagging_Handler.Start_Element_Details`, which rejects an empty `Namespace_URI` unless `Allow_Empty_Namespace` is set) and `:574-586` (`Parse_Bucket` uses `Allow_Empty_Namespace => False`, `Parse_Bucket_Response` uses `True`, both through `Parse_Bucket_Document` at `:550-586`); `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:8261-8271`, where the server calls the strict `Tagging.Parse_Bucket`, and `:8284-8294`, which maps `Malformed_Tagging` to `400 MalformedXML` and `Invalid_Tag` to `400 InvalidTag`; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:43-89`, where the object `Tagging_Handler` overrides no `Start_Element_Details` at all, so `PutObjectTagging` accepts any namespace including none; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-52`, which grants the tolerance to the *response* decoder only; the pinned model, in which the `Tagging` shape carries no `xmlNamespace`; the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD gate `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:1903-1906`; the existing assertions at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11268-11270` (the rejection) and `:11233-11243` (the response decoder accepting the same string).

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_2.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** the crate already has the tolerant behaviour — the `Allow_Empty_Namespace` flag threaded through `Parse_Bucket_Document` — and grants it only to the response decoder. The result is a server that accepts a document on `PUT /bucket/key?tagging` and rejects the identical document on `PUT /bucket?tagging`, deterministically, for any client that does not emit `xmlns`; minio-go is such a client, on both resources. The program compares `Parse_Bucket` with `Parse_Bucket_Response` on `mc`'s captured document — `Parse_Bucket_Response` accepts it with `tags=1`, `Parse_Bucket` raises `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.TAGGING.MALFORMED_TAGGING: malformed bucket tagging XML` — then sends that document as a signed `PUT …?tagging` with a correct `Content-MD5` to both resources: `PUT /domain-b2/k?tagging` is answered `200`, `PUT /domain-b2?tagging` is answered `400` with `<Code>MalformedXML</Code>`, and the boto3/aws-cli control carrying `xmlns="http://s3.amazonaws.com/doc/2006-03-01/"` on the bucket resource is answered `200`. The plain-HTTP caveat is stated plainly: over the plain-HTTP test endpoint `mc tag set` is stopped one step earlier, because minio-go signs this body as `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD`, which the crate refuses without TLS (`s3_applications.adb:1903-1906`, `400 InvalidRequest` "UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD requires a secure transport"), so the namespace check is not reached over that endpoint; on the HTTPS endpoint a real deployment uses, that gate passes and the namespace check is what remains, which is why the isolating replay was signed with a real payload hash rather than driven through `mc`.

**Notes:** With S3-DOMAIN-B-1 this is the same change class as S3-DOMAIN-A-5, the server-side PutBucketVersioning root check in another area; one decision on receiver-side namespace tolerance settles all three sites. Here the crate is already inconsistent with itself in two directions — the object request path and the bucket *response* path both accept what the bucket request path refuses — and the test suite currently asserts that inconsistency, `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:11268-11270` pinning the rejection and `:11233-11243` pinning the acceptance of the same string by the response decoder, so the first would have to be inverted with the fix. `docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md` documents "strict namespace-aware XML", but the model imposes no such rule and MinIO accepts the body, so the same reasoning as S3-DOMAIN-B-1 applies and it is not a documented limitation. The fix is one Boolean: flipping `Parse_Bucket`'s `Allow_Empty_Namespace` to `True` brings the request path in line with the response path and with the object path, and needs no new public declaration; the maintainer decision is only whether "foreign namespaces remain invalid" should stay, which it can, because only the *empty* namespace is at issue. Refutation attempts that failed: there is no other tolerant request path (`Parse_Bucket_Response` is called only from `client-low_level.adb:392`, `Decode_Get_Bucket_Tagging_Response`); the object path does not enforce a namespace either, which would at least have made the server self-consistent; and no other property of `mc`'s body is at fault, since adding only `xmlns` turns the request into `200`.

### S3-DOMAIN-B-3: The ListObjectAnnotations query codec binds camelCase names the model, the generated descriptor and this crate's own client do not use

**Severity:** P1 (unchanged). This is a self-interop failure: the crate's own client cannot use a prefix, a result limit, or pagination against the crate's own server. It is not P0 because `ListObjectAnnotations` is a Flyology extension operation, so no third-party SDK is involved; it is a wrong result on valid input via a reachable path.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-annotations.adb:137-172`, where `Parse_Query` matches `annotationPrefix`, `maxAnnotationResults` and `continuationToken` and falls through to `raise Malformed_Annotation_Request with "unsupported annotation query parameter"`; the pinned model's `ListObjectAnnotationsRequest` querystring members, `VersionId → versionId`, `MaxAnnotationResults → max-annotation-results`, `AnnotationPrefix → annotation-prefix`, `ContinuationToken → continuation-token`; the generated descriptor `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.adb:45588-45599`, which carries the same hyphenated names; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:11605-11624`, where `Prepare_List_Object_Annotations` adds `MaxAnnotationResults`, `AnnotationPrefix` and `ContinuationToken` by member name, and `:1216-1222`, which emits `SigV4.Pair (Model.Member_Location_Name (Input_Shape, Member), …)`, that is, the hyphenated spelling; `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:6505-6529`, where the route sniff uses the model-conformant `&annotationName=` but any `Malformed_Annotation_Request` sets `Annotation_Query_Invalid`, and `:7015-7019`, which answers `400 InvalidArgument` "The object annotation request query is invalid"; the in-tree gate asserting the non-model names at `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:13552` and `:13588`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_3.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Annotations.Parse_Query` hard-codes camelCase names for the three list-only query members, while every other part of the crate — the pinned model, the generated descriptor read through `Model.Member_Location_Name`, and `Client.Low_Level.Prepare_List_Object_Annotations` which reads that descriptor — uses the hyphenated names. A Flyology client that asks for a prefix, a page size, or the next page therefore gets `400 InvalidArgument` from a Flyology server, deterministically; only the degenerate "list everything, one page" call works. The program prints the compiled model's query location names for the operation (`MaxAnnotationResults -> "max-annotation-results"`, `AnnotationPrefix -> "annotation-prefix"`, `ContinuationToken -> "continuation-token"`, `VersionId -> "versionId"`, each annotated "what Client.Low_Level puts on the wire"), creates an object with the annotations `alpha` and `beta`, then issues signed `GET /domain-b3/obj?annotation&<name>=<value>` requests in both spellings. The camelCase controls pass: `maxAnnotationResults=1` is answered `200` with a `<NextContinuationToken>fos1.5073ca3abd4a8ffc65e51385fca7513b2c3a2c5015c9debcaa3ecaf577636496.616c706861</NextContinuationToken>`, and `annotationPrefix=al` is answered `200` with `<AnnotationPrefix>al</AnnotationPrefix><AnnotationCount>1</AnnotationCount>`. The model's spellings `max-annotation-results=1`, `annotation-prefix=al` and `continuation-token=fos1.5073ca3a…` are each answered `400 <Code>InvalidArgument</Code>`, the last of them driven by a real `NextContinuationToken` taken from the preceding page, so pagination is impossible in the model's spelling. The two singular annotation verbs are correct (`annotationName`, `versionId`), so only these three names are wrong.

**Notes:** This is a self-interop failure between the generated descriptor and the hand-written codec, and the direction of the fix follows from which side is generated. `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.adb:45588-45599` is generated from the pinned model and agrees with it — the program reads the location names out of the *compiled* model and prints the hyphenated forms, so descriptor and pinned JSON are not in conflict — and the "Ada source style" section of the repository rules forbids hand-editing generated sources, so the hand-written `Annotations.Parse_Query` is the side that must change: either compare against `Model.Member_Location_Name`, as `multipart_uploads.adb` already does for enumerations, or correct the three literals. No new public declaration is needed. `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:13552` and `:13588` send the camelCase names and would have to be updated with the fix; the qualification lane `tests/src/s3_list_object_annotations_qualification.adb:50-83` adds the optional members only when environment inputs are set, which is why the default lane never noticed. Refutation attempts that failed: the descriptor did not come from a different model revision; there is no second parser accepting the hyphenated spellings, since `continuation-token` appears only in the ListObjectsV2 and ListBuckets parsers; and the optional members are not unreachable in practice, because the program drives them end to end.

### S3-DOMAIN-B-4: URL-encoded ListMultipartUploads cursors are compared bytewise, so page 2 of a valid `encoding-type=url` listing is 500 InternalError

**Severity:** P1 (unchanged), and cheaper to hit than the discovery estimated: no non-ASCII key is needed, because `%` (16#25#) sorts below every unreserved byte, so the ASCII keys `a9` and `a:` suffice. A valid, backend-answered request ends as `500 InternalError`, and the crate's own client maps the same exception to `Invalid_Response`. Not P0: no data is lost and nothing hangs.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:655-667`, where the "next cursor does not advance" predicate compares `US.To_String (Value.Next_Key_Marker) < US.To_String (Value.Key_Marker)`, and `:773-795` and `:823-828`, where `Validate` runs inside both `Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads` (client) and `Serialize_List_Multipart_Uploads` (server); `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:11199-11204`, where the local `Encoded` function applies `Encoding.URI_Encode (Value, Encode_Slash => False)` when `Request.URL_Encoding`, and `:11218-11220` and `:11240-11244`, which fill `Key_Marker` and `Next_Key_Marker` with `Encoded (...)` while `Upload_ID_Marker` and `Next_Upload_ID_Marker` stay raw; `:11279-11286`, where the handler catches only `Multipart_Uploads.Malformed_List_Request`, so `Malformed_Upload_Listing` escapes to the final `when others` and becomes `500 InternalError`; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_encoding.adb:10-46`, where `Unreserved` is `A-Z a-z 0-9 - . _ ~` plus `/` when `Encode_Slash => False`, every one of those bytes being above `%`; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:13405` and `:13423-13426`, which map `Malformed_Upload_Listing` to `Invalid_Response`, with the supported URL-encoding parameter at `:13477-13484`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_4.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Multipart_Uploads.Validate` treats `Next_Key_Marker < Key_Marker` as proof that a page's cursor went backwards, but the server has already percent-encoded both markers when `encoding-type=url` was requested, and percent-encoding is not order-preserving: `a9 < a:` raw, while `a%3A < a9` encoded. Any truncated page whose next key first differs from the previous marker at a byte that encodes — `:` `[` `=` `&` `?` `#`, a space, or any non-ASCII byte — trips the check inside `Serialize_List_Multipart_Uploads`, and because the handler catches only `Malformed_List_Request` the exception escapes to the generic handler, so the caller sees `500 InternalError` for a request the backend answered correctly. Keys containing `:` are ordinary (timestamps, URNs, `a:b` namespacing), so this is deterministic and not exotic. The program creates multipart uploads for `a9`, `a:` and `az`, decodes a conforming truncated URL-encoded page through the client decoder — which raises `Malformed_Upload_Listing: ListMultipartUploads next cursor does not advance` — then pages the real server with `encoding-type=url&max-uploads=1`. Page 1 is `200` with `<NextKeyMarker>a9</NextKeyMarker>` and `<IsTruncated>true</IsTruncated>`; page 2, `GET /domain-b4?uploads&encoding-type=url&max-uploads=1&key-marker=a9&upload-id-marker=…`, is `500` with `<Code>InternalError</Code><Message>We encountered an internal error</Message>` instead of the expected encoded `a%3A` next marker. The control, the same paging without `encoding-type`, is `200` with `<KeyMarker>a9</KeyMarker><NextKeyMarker>a:</NextKeyMarker>`, which isolates the encoding as the cause. The client decoder is affected symmetrically, so any conforming server that URL-encodes `NextKeyMarker` — AWS included — makes `Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads` raise `Invalid_Response`.

**Notes:** No existing test produces the combination: `tests/src/s3_http_socket_corpus.adb:12666-12694` uses URL encoding only to check echo mismatches, and `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:10975` only rejects `encoding-type=xml`, so nothing has to be inverted. A fix must compare the *decoded* markers — the server has them in `Request.Key_Marker` and `Page.Next_After.Key` before encoding — which means either validating before encoding or carrying the decoded cursor alongside; that part is internal, but the codec's `Validate` is shared with the client decoder, so the maintainer must decide whether `Validate` should decode `Key_Marker` and `Next_Key_Marker` when `Encoding_Type = "url"`, which is the only choice that also fixes the client, or drop the ordering predicate for encoded pages. Refutation attempts that failed: the server does not compare decoded values, since the `Response` record is built from `Encoded (...)`; the client cannot simply avoid requesting URL encoding, because it is a supported parameter and a conforming non-Flyology server produces the same rejection in the decoder; the inversion needs no non-ASCII key; and no sibling listing codec shares the predicate, since `rg "does not advance" src/*.adb` matches only this file.

### S3-DOMAIN-B-5: GetObjectAttributes rejects the two AWS Backup storage classes the pinned model carries

**Severity:** P2 (unchanged). It needs an object in one of two uncommon storage classes and affects only the client decoder.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:756-760`, where `Valid_Storage_Class` lists thirteen literals, and `:775-779`, where `Validate` raises `Malformed_Attributes "invalid storage class"` whenever the member is present and unlisted; the pinned model's `StorageClass`, which has fifteen values; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:5324-5333`, which maps `Malformed_Attributes` to `Invalid_Response "malformed GetObjectAttributes response"`; the sibling codec `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:687-689`, which validates the same shape *dynamically* against the model with `Valid_Enumeration (…, Upload_Member_Shape (4))`; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-replication.adb:483-487`, which already decodes both missing spellings; the compatibility row `docs/compatibility/s3.md:60`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_5.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Attributes.Valid_Storage_Class` duplicates the model's `StorageClass` enumeration as thirteen string literals and has drifted two values behind it. The program prints the same fifteen values straight out of the compiled model — `STANDARD`, `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY`, `STANDARD_IA`, `ONEZONE_IA`, `INTELLIGENT_TIERING`, `GLACIER`, `DEEP_ARCHIVE`, `OUTPOSTS`, `GLACIER_IR`, `SNOW`, `EXPRESS_ONEZONE`, `FSX_OPENZFS`, `FSX_ONTAP`, `AWS_BACKUP_WARM`, `AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM` — then decodes a `GetObjectAttributes` response body for each class through `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response`. `STANDARD` and `GLACIER_IR` are accepted with `kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND`; `AWS_BACKUP_WARM` and `AWS_BACKUP_LOW_COST_WARM` raise `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.ATTRIBUTES.MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid storage class` and then `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response`. An AWS object materialised by AWS Backup answers `200` with `<StorageClass>AWS_BACKUP_WARM</StorageClass>`, and every typed and convenience GetObjectAttributes client then reports a malformed response for a valid, modelled answer.

**Notes:** No test pins the list — `rg AWS_BACKUP tests/src` matches only the replication corpus — and the crate's own server only ever emits `STANDARD`, which is why no lane sees it. The compatibility matrix row at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:60` claims a "complete typed" client for this operation, so the drift also contradicts a maintained document. The fix should replace the literal list with the model-driven check the sibling codec already uses, so it cannot drift again; no new public declaration is needed. Refutation attempts that failed: `StorageClass` is validated whenever present, not only when selected, and it is present exactly when the caller selects it, which the convenience clients expose; and the pinned model is not the limitation, since it carries both values and `s3-replication.adb:483-487` already decodes them.

### S3-DOMAIN-B-6: PutObjectTagging reports tag failures as `MalformedXML` while the two other tag paths report `InvalidTag`

**Severity:** P2 (unchanged) — wrong diagnostics, no wrong data. The internal inconsistency is exact, and MinIO confirms `InvalidTag` for two of the three cases.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:61-64`, where the eleventh `<Tag>` raises `Malformed_Tagging "invalid object tag entry"`, and `:161-166`, where `Parse` folds `not Valid_S3_Tags (Handler.Tags)` — repertoire and duplicate-key failures — into `Malformed_Tagging "invalid object tagging document"`, so the package's own `Invalid_Tag` exception (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:10`) is never raised on this path; `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:15956-15962`, `when Tagging.Malformed_Tagging => Send_Error (X, 400, "MalformedXML", "The XML provided did not validate against the object tagging schema", …)`; the two paths that answer `InvalidTag` for the same `Valid_S3_Tags` failure, `:15072-15080` (`x-amz-tagging` on PutObject) and `:11987-11999` (CreateMultipartUpload); `:8284-8294` (PutBucketTagging), which keeps `Invalid_Tag` and `Malformed_Tagging` apart and so gives `InvalidTag` and `MalformedXML` respectively; the storage path `:15940-15954`, which stores the parsed set directly and never re-validates.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_6.adb`, exit status 1, three failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** the object tagging XML parser has no way to signal "the XML was fine, the tags were not": `Tagging.Parse` raises `Malformed_Tagging` for a structural error, a repertoire violation, a duplicate key and an eleventh tag alike, and the server maps that one exception to `MalformedXML` with a message about the schema. The program sends three namespaced `PUT /domain-b6/k?tagging` documents with a correct `Content-MD5` — an out-of-repertoire key `a#b`, a duplicate key, and eleven tags — and each is answered `400` with `code=MalformedXML`, while the two controls on the same server, `PutObject` with `x-amz-tagging` carrying the key `a#b` and `PutBucketTagging` with the same bad key, are both answered `400 code=InvalidTag`. A caller whose tag key contains `#`, or who repeats a key, or who sends eleven tags, is told its XML is malformed; the identical tag set sent as `x-amz-tagging` or as a bucket tag set is told `InvalidTag`. Every document in the program carries `xmlns`, and the bucket control with the identical body reaches `InvalidTag`, so this is not S3-DOMAIN-B-2 in disguise.

**Notes:** The pinned model does not settle the code: it names **no error shape at all** for `PutObjectTagging`, `PutBucketTagging` or `DeleteObjects` — their `errors` lists are empty — and there is no `InvalidTag` exception shape anywhere in the model, so the authority for the two settled cases is the crate's own other two paths plus MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, which answers the same three documents `400 <Code>InvalidTag</Code>` "The TagKey you have provided is invalid" for the key `a#b`, `400 <Code>InvalidTag</Code>` "Cannot provide multiple Tags with the same key" for the duplicate key, and `400 <Code>BadRequest</Code>` "Tags cannot be more than 10" for eleven tags. The maintainer decision is therefore the code for the count case alone: the crate's own header path says `InvalidTag`, which is what the program asserts, MinIO says `BadRequest`, and the model names neither; the repertoire and duplicate cases are settled by both the crate's own other paths and MinIO. A fix means raising `Tagging.Invalid_Tag`, already declared at `tagging.ads:10`, from the `Valid_S3_Tags` and tag-count failures and adding one `when Tagging.Invalid_Tag` arm at `s3_applications.adb:15956`; no new public declaration is needed. `tests/src/s3_server_application_corpus.adb:6420-6422` already asserts `InvalidTag` on the header path, and `:14400-14495` covers PutObjectTagging admission without asserting any code for an invalid tag, so no test would have to be inverted for the two cases MinIO confirms.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-B-7: GetObjectAttributes infers "multipart" from the presence of `<ObjectParts>` and rejects valid full-object SHA/MD5/xxHash checksums

**Severity:** P2 (unchanged). It needs a non-Flyology server that reports the parts container for a single-part object; the crate's own server never does, which the program shows, so there is no self-interop failure and the severity is not raised. The "concrete stock-server trigger" the discovery could not verify is now captured.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:422-428`, where `Has_Object_Parts` is set on the `<ObjectParts>` start tag alone; `:771-774`, `Validate_Object_Checksum (Value.Checksum, Is_Multipart => Value.Has_Object_Parts)`; `:731-738`, whose `FULL_OBJECT` branch requires `Policy.Supported (Algorithm, Policy.Full_Object)` whenever `Is_Multipart`; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-checksum_policy.ads:68-75`, where `Supported` is documented as "whether AWS permits the algorithm/type pair **for multipart use**" and allows only `CRC32 | CRC32C | CRC64NVME`, and `:76-83`, where the package already exports the right predicate for the other case, `Ordinary_Object_Supported (Kind) = (Kind = Full_Object)`, documented as "PutObject and CopyObject accept every concrete algorithm in the pinned model, always as one full-object digest"; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:857-877`, where an absent `ChecksumType` with a plain Base64 digest is *inferred* as `FULL_OBJECT`, so servers that omit the type hit the same branch; the pinned model, in which `GetObjectAttributesParts` is an ordinary optional member of `GetObjectAttributesOutput` with nothing tying its presence to a multipart upload, the request enum letting a caller select `ObjectParts` for any object; `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:16314-16317`, where the Flyology server emits `ObjectParts` only when `Snapshot.Is_Multipart`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_7.adb`, exit status 1, four failed expectations.

**What is wrong:** `Attributes.Validate` uses the presence of the requested `<ObjectParts>` attribute group as evidence that the object was uploaded in parts, and then applies the narrow multipart policy (`Checksum_Policy.Supported`, CRC only) instead of the ordinary-object policy the same package documents. MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` really does return a full-object SHA-256 together with a one-part parts container for an object written with a single `PUT` carrying `x-amz-checksum-sha256`: `GET /probe-b7/single?attributes` with `x-amz-object-attributes: ETag,Checksum,ObjectParts,StorageClass,ObjectSize` is answered `200` with `<Checksum><ChecksumSHA256>uU0nuZNNPgilLlLX2n2r+sSE7+N6U4DukIj3rOLvzek=</ChecksumSHA256><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></Checksum><ObjectParts><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated><MaxParts>10000</MaxParts><NextPartNumberMarker>1</NextPartNumberMarker><PartNumberMarker>0</PartNumberMarker><PartsCount>1</PartsCount><Part><PartNumber>1</PartNumber><Size>11</Size></Part></ObjectParts><StorageClass>STANDARD</StorageClass><ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize>`. The program decodes that captured combination, plus MD5, xxHash64 and a type-omitted variant, through `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Object_Attributes_Response`: all four raise `MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: invalid full-object checksum` and then `INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response`, while the two controls — `FULL_OBJECT CRC32` with the parts container present, and `FULL_OBJECT SHA256` with no parts container — are accepted. The last block shows the crate's own server answering a single-PUT `GetObjectAttributes` with `<Checksum><ChecksumSHA256>…</ChecksumSHA256><ChecksumType>FULL_OBJECT</ChecksumType></Checksum><ObjectSize>11</ObjectSize>` and no `ObjectParts`, read back successfully by its own client.

**Notes:** MinIO's real bytes had to be re-rooted under the AWS root in the program, because MinIO uses the lowercase root `getObjectAttributesResponse`, which `attributes.adb:405-408` rejects; that deviation is already a documented exclusion (`tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:64,74` sets `FLYOLOGY_GET_OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_ORACLE_MODE=minio-2025-lowercase-root`, "a non-AWS lowercase root element"), so it is not a new finding, but it is why this checksum rule was never exercised in-tree. MinIO's `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` was lowered to `1000` in this program so that only the checksum rule is exercised; the 10000 echo is S3-DOMAIN-B-8, and against a live MinIO the two rejections stack. `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:1675-1700` pairs `ObjectParts` only with CRC-family checksums on completed multipart objects, so no test asserts the wrong behaviour and none would have to be inverted. A fix needs a maintainer decision on what "multipart" means to this decoder: the available signals are `TotalPartsCount > 1`, a composite `-N` ETag suffix, or `ChecksumType = COMPOSITE`, or, simplest, applying `Ordinary_Object_Supported` unless the checksum is explicitly `COMPOSITE`. No new public declaration is required, since `Checksum_Policy` already exports both predicates. Refutation attempts that failed: no stronger multipart signal is consulted anywhere in the decoder; `Has_Object_Parts` cannot be false for an empty container, because `:426-427` sets it on the start tag; and the Flyology server cannot produce the combination itself.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-B-8: The GetObjectAttributes decoder bounds the response's `MaxParts` echo by `Core.Page_Size'Last`, and MinIO echoes 10000

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier found it while verifying S3-DOMAIN-B-7, because MinIO's real response was rejected before the checksum rule was even reached). A valid, modelled response is rejected, but only from a server that echoes a `MaxParts` above 1000, and against MinIO specifically it is masked by the already-documented lowercase-root exclusion.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-attributes.adb:551-576`, where `Finish_Field` raises `Malformed_Attributes "invalid object part scalar"` when `Item.Field = Max_Parts_Field and then Parsed.Value > Core.Page_Size'Last`, and, because that runs inside the SAX handler, `Parse_Result`'s `when XML.XML_Error` arm rewrites it to the misleading "malformed GetObjectAttributes XML"; `:786-790`, where `Validate` applies the same `Core.Page_Size'Last` bound a second time; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:28`, `subtype Page_Size is Natural range 0 .. 1_000;`; the pinned model, in which `GetObjectAttributesParts.MaxParts` has shape `MaxParts`, which is `{'type': 'integer'}` with **no** `min` or `max`, the request-side member being the equally unbounded `x-amz-max-parts` header.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_8.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** the decoder constrains the *response's* `MaxParts` echo to `Core.Page_Size'Last` (1000), a bound that exists for Flyology's own pagination policy and that the pinned model does not place on this member. MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` answers `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` on **every** GetObjectAttributes request that selects `ObjectParts`, regardless of the requested value, so a Flyology client talking to a MinIO-compatible server gets `Invalid_Response` for a well-formed `200`. The program prints `Core.Page_Size'Last = 1000` and `Core.Part_Number'Last = 10000`, then decodes the same response body twice: with `<MaxParts>1000</MaxParts>` both `S3.Attributes.Parse_Result` and `Client.Low_Level` accept it (`MaxParts=1000`, `kind=OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_FOUND`), and with MinIO's real `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` they raise `MALFORMED_ATTRIBUTES: malformed GetObjectAttributes XML` and `INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetObjectAttributes response`. The body uses a `FULL_OBJECT CRC32` checksum, which the multipart policy accepts, so the only difference between pass and fail is the `MaxParts` value and S3-DOMAIN-B-7 does not explain it. The diagnostic is wrong as well as the rule: a range rejection is reported as a parser failure, pointing a maintainer at the XML rather than at a bound.

**Notes:** This finding relates to S3-DOMAIN-A-6, the listing query parsers that reject `max-keys` above 1000 instead of capping the page: both are the same 1,000 ceiling, the visible `Core.Page_Size` subtype at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.ads:28`, applied where the pinned model places no bound, and MinIO exceeds it in both directions — `<MaxKeys>2000</MaxKeys>` there, `<MaxParts>10000</MaxParts>` here. The difference is the side: S3-DOMAIN-A-6 is a request-side rejection, this one is a receive-side rejection of another server's echo, so the two can be fixed independently, but the maintainer answer to "where does Flyology's own pagination policy apply" should cover both, and under the "Ada interface values" section of the repository rules the bound is a specification-level value whose scope is a decision rather than an implementation detail. The maintainer decision here is what bound, if any, an echoed `MaxParts` should have on the receive side: `Core.Part_Number'Last` (10000) would accept MinIO, no bound at all would follow the model, and either choice is a change to a validation rule rather than to a public declaration. Nothing in-tree asserts the wrong behaviour: `tests/src/s3_implementation_corpus.adb:303-340` disables the GetObjectAttributes assertions entirely under `FLYOLOGY_GET_OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_ORACLE_MODE=minio-2025-lowercase-root`, so MinIO's `MaxParts` never reaches the decoder in CI. Refutation attempts that failed: the crate is not reusing a request-side bound the model does impose, because neither `MaxParts` shape carries one; and no in-tree oracle covers it.

---

### S3-DOMAIN-B-9: The GetBucketTagging response decoder rejects an empty `<TagSet/>` with the publication predicate

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier promoted it from the discovery report's "empty TagSet" observation, which said the tested servers answer `NoSuchTagSet` instead of an empty set). MinIO answers `NoSuchTagSet` only when no tag set was ever stored; if a client stores an *empty* tag set, which MinIO accepts with `200`, MinIO then serves `<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>` with `200`, so the state is reachable. It needs a bucket whose tag set was deliberately stored empty by some other client, and it produces a wrong error rather than wrong data.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:565-566`, where `Parse_Bucket_Document` raises `Invalid_Tag "invalid bucket tag set"` when `not Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set (Handler.Result)`, which runs for both `Parse_Bucket` and `Parse_Bucket_Response`; `src/flyology-object_storage-tags.ads:34-40`, where `Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` is documented as validating "a **nonempty** bucket tag set", that is, a *publication* predicate, applied here to a decoded response; `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:375-405`, where `Decode_Get_Bucket_Tagging_Response` calls `Parse_Bucket_Response` and maps `Invalid_Tag` to `Invalid_Response "malformed GetBucketTagging response"`; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:48-52`, whose contract for `Parse_Bucket_Response` promises namespace tolerance only and says nothing about emptiness; the pinned model, in which `Tagging.TagSet` is a plain list with no `min`, so an empty set is modelled; `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.adb:591-595`, where `Serialize_Bucket` refuses to serialize an empty set.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/src/repro_s3_domain_b_9.adb`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.

**What is wrong:** `Parse_Bucket_Document` runs the same `Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` predicate on the way in and on the way out, and that predicate's documented meaning is "a tag set that may be published", which requires at least one tag. The program decodes MinIO's exact `<Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>` bytes through `S3.Tagging.Parse_Bucket_Response` and `Client.Low_Level.Decode_Get_Bucket_Tagging_Response`, which raise `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.S3.TAGGING.INVALID_TAG: invalid bucket tag set` and `FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.CLIENT.LOW_LEVEL.INVALID_RESPONSE: malformed GetBucketTagging response`; the one-tag control `<Tagging><TagSet><Tag><Key>k</Key><Value>v</Value></Tag></TagSet></Tagging>` is accepted with `tags=1` and `kind=BUCKET_TAGS_FOUND`. The MinIO oracle establishes that the state is reachable and distinct from the absent-tag-set state: `DeleteBucketTagging: 204`, `GetBucketTagging after delete: 404 <Code>NoSuchTagSet</Code>`, `PutBucketTagging with an empty TagSet: 200`, `GetBucketTagging after PUT with an empty TagSet: 200 <Tagging><TagSet></TagSet></Tagging>`.

**Notes:** The failure is deterministic given that bucket state and unreachable through Flyology's own writer — `Serialize_Bucket` refuses to serialize an empty set — which is exactly why the decoder never sees it in-tree and why another client has to create the state; nothing in the suite asserts the wrong behaviour, so no test has to be inverted. A fix means splitting the receive-side check from the publication check, accepting an empty decoded set in `Parse_Bucket_Response` while `Serialize_Bucket` and `Parse_Bucket` keep the nonempty rule; that is a maintainer decision about the meaning of `Tags.Tag_Set` at the boundary (`tags.ads:34-40`) rather than a new public declaration. Refutation attempts that failed: the client does not leave the exception unmapped, which would be worse, since `Invalid_Tag` is caught and re-raised as `Invalid_Response`; the empty state is not reachable through Flyology's own client at all; and `Parse_Bucket_Response`'s contract does not permit the rejection.

## SERVER-EXE: the server executable, its two listeners, its credential bootstrap and its configuration surface (SERVER-EXE-1 .. SERVER-EXE-5)

The area covers the `flyology_object_storage_server` crate read in full — `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server.adb`, `..._configuration.ad?`, `..._credentials.ad?` with `..._credentials_native.c` and `..._credentials_corpus.adb`, `..._runtime.ad?`, `..._sessions.ad?`, `..._signals.ad?`, `..._assets.ad?`, plus `server/assets/app.js` and `server/assets/index.html` — against `server/README.md`, `server/alire.toml`, `server/alire/build_hash_inputs`, `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. The library contracts consulted are the pinned `flyology_0.1.1_7be049fc` sources (`src/platform/darwin/flyology.ads:21-26`, `src/flyology-execution_groups.ads:154-158`, `flyology-io-structured_servers.ads:100-105`, `flyology-supervision.ads:167-177,405-411`) and the pinned `flyology_http_0.1.3_537edbc5` sources (`src/flyology-http-server-routing.ads:81-100`, `src/flyology-http-server-middleware_rate_limits.ads`). The repository pieces the executable wires were read where they carry the defect: `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.ads` and `.adb:355-405,3514-3573`, `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:107-125,301-305`, `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:39-`, `docs/architecture/files-layout.md`. **Server build**, the one build this area ran, taken while holding `tests/repros/.build.lock` and released immediately afterwards: `cd server && alr -n build` → **exit 0, 47 s wall** (`alr` itself reports "Build finished successfully in 46.26 seconds"), log `review/baseline/server-build.log`, binary `server/bin/flyology_object_storage_server` (28.1 MB), build profile `DEVELOPMENT` with `-Og` from `server/alire/build_hash_inputs`, which is the crate's own default for `alr -n build` and the profile every timing number below was measured under. **Measurement conditions**, recorded per the repository's performance-testing rule: Apple M3 Max, macOS 25.6.0, on AC power, `pmset -g` reporting `powermode 0` (normal, not low-power) and `pmset -g therm` recording no thermal or performance warning; load average 12.3 because other review agents were building concurrently; every baseline and its comparison were taken back-to-back on that same host under the same profile, and the reported effects are 300–3400x, far outside load noise. The reproduction programs are mixed: four shell programs under `tests/repros/shell/` drive the real executable, because the in-process harness `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb:108-110` instantiates `Structured_Servers` with the default native handler model and has no supervisor, so it cannot show SERVER-EXE-1 or SERVER-EXE-4; one Ada program under `tests/repros/src/` shows SERVER-EXE-3 at the cheaper backend level. Every script sets `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_ASSET_ROOT` explicitly, because `assets.adb:16-27` looks only at `./assets/index.html` and `./server/assets/index.html`. Two findings are shared with the backend areas and must not be counted twice there: the destructive code of SERVER-EXE-3 belongs to the FILES and SQLITE backends' `Open` (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3548-3564` and `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:304-305`), and SERVER-EXE-5 is the asymmetry between those same two `Open` procedures (`files.adb:3514-3573` takes no lock; `sqlite.adb:107-125` `Acquire_Root_Lock`, called from `Open` at `:301-302`); what belongs to this area in both is the reachable path from one documented, unvalidated environment variable.

### SERVER-EXE-1: administrator login runs 600,000 PBKDF2 iterations on the shared cooperative event loop and stalls the S3 listener

**Severity:** P1 (the discovery report said P1, unchanged, and the verifier raised its confidence from Medium to measured). The single-login case is a wrong result on valid input via a reachable path with the default configuration, which is P1. It is not P0: the stall terminates, so it is starvation rather than a hang or deadlock; it falls into none of the P0 security categories, with no signature bypass, traversal, credential exposure or authorisation bypass; and the unbounded-starvation variant needs a local process that can reach the loopback-only management port.

**Location:** `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:432-441` (`Login` calls `Admin_Credentials.Verify` inline in the handler), `:187-190` (the S3 listener instantiates `Flyology.IO.Structured_Servers` with `Handler_Model => Flyology.Lightweight_Task`) and `:853-858` (the management listener does the same, `Server : aliased Structured.Server (Capacity => 32)`); `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_credentials.adb:16` (`Work_Factor : constant Positive := 600_000;`), `:134-145` (the derivation loop: no `delay`, no I/O, no `Cancellation.Requested` poll) and `:325-344` (`Verify` derives first and then compares in constant time, so a wrong password costs the same as a right one) with the length and user gate at `:329-333`; the handler-model contract at `/Users/yrashk/.local/share/alire/builds/flyology_0.1.1_7be049fc/5e4ec5d2d7fb582d5512843a434aaaa816073423e6241e0ce95898ea1d152ad2/src/platform/darwin/flyology.ads:21-26` and the pool-size contract at `.../src/flyology-execution_groups.ads:154-158`; the handler requirement at `flyology-io-structured_servers.ads:100-105`; the documented listener coupling at `server/README.md:8-12`; the existing coverage at `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:102-117` and the `env -i` launches at `:388-392,462-471`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/shell/repro_server_exe_1.sh`, exit status 1, one failed expectation. It starts the server itself on a fresh temporary root under `/private/tmp/claude-501/` with `env -i`, `FLYOLOGY_S3_PORT=0 FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_PORT=0` and the memory backend, reads the two `READY` ports and the `BOOTSTRAP ADMIN` password from the server's own output, then times three signed SigV4 `ListBuckets` requests with no other traffic, three logins on their own, and three signed S3 requests each issued 0.15 s into a valid login, with a plain TCP connect alongside. It fails when the worst concurrent S3 latency exceeds `max(10 x worst baseline, 0.050 s)`, the floor keeping sub-millisecond scheduling noise from tripping the ratio, and it always stops the server. The control re-issues the same signed request with no login in flight.

**What is wrong:** Both HTTP listeners run their handlers as Flyology lightweight tasks, and with the shipped default of one shared execution group every handler for both listeners is multiplexed onto a single event-loop thread. `Login` performs a 600,000-iteration PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 derivation inline in its handler, and that loop contains no I/O, no delay and no `Cancellation.Requested` poll, which is exactly the CPU-only case `Structured_Servers` requires handlers to break up. The event loop therefore cannot run any other handler for the roughly one second the derivation takes, so every in-flight and newly accepted S3 request waits behind it. Measured against the built binary: the signed S3 baseline with no other traffic is **0.002 s** (three runs, 0.002/0.002/0.002, status 200, 184 bytes); the same signed request issued 0.15 s into a single valid administrator login takes **0.771–0.876 s** (the recorded runs are 0.863, 0.849 and 0.771, worst 0.863 s), a factor of about 350; one login on its own costs **0.99–1.07 s** of CPU (runs 1.001, 1.067, 1.074, and a wrong-password login measured at 0.994 s, because `Verify` derives before it compares); ten simultaneous logins starve one S3 request for **10.2 s** (`t+1 s3=10.201 200 184`, flood wall 10.50 s, min 1.10 s, median 6.40 s, max 10.50 s, with zero 429 or 503); and fifty simultaneous logins hold the management listener for **51 s** and cause 1–3 of the 50 connections to be closed with no response at all. The plain TCP connect always completes in 0.001 s, because the kernel's accept queue absorbs the connection, so a health check that only opens a socket does not see the outage and only a request that needs a response does. Anyone running the server hits the one-second version on every workbench sign-in; the unbounded version needs only a local process that can open the loopback management port and post form bodies, with no session, no credential and no rate limit (SERVER-EXE-2), and it also drops connections outright once the queue exceeds the 30 s request timeout. It is fully deterministic.

**Notes:** Five refutation attempts failed. A wrong password does not short-circuit before the derivation: `credentials.adb:329-333` rejects a wrong `User` and a `Password'Length` outside `1 .. 64` first, but `Login` reaches `Verify` only with a 48-character password, so every attempt pays the full cost, measured at 0.994 s. The two listeners are not on different execution groups: both use the default `Handler_CPU`, so both are placed by the same automatic pool. The pool is not larger than one by default, and raising it does not remove the coupling — with `FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE=8` against the same binary a **single** concurrent login costs the S3 probe nothing (0.002 s, login 1.001 s), but **ten** concurrent logins still stall an S3 request for **0.774 s** and the flood's wall time only falls from 10.5 s to 2.13 s, so the pool size raises the number of simultaneous derivations needed to starve the loop from one to the pool size and nothing more; the executable never grows the pool (`rg 'Grow_Configured_Pool'` over `server/`, `src/`, `sqlite/` and `tests/` finds no call site), the variable is not mentioned in `server/README.md`, and `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:388-392,462-471` launch the server with `env -i`, so the shipped default is one. It is not documented: `server/README.md:8-12` couples the two listeners only through supervision order ("The management child depends on the S3 child, so an S3 generation failure restarts the workbench generation as well") and no document says a workbench sign-in pauses S3 service. And no existing test would catch it: `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` issues its three logins sequentially with `sleep 1` between them and never runs S3 traffic concurrently with a login, while nothing in CI runs the lightweight handler model at all (see the observations below). A fix needs a maintainer decision this review cannot make: either the derivation moves off the cooperative loop (a `Flyology.Native_Task` handler model, or an explicit worker for password verification), or the loop is broken up with a cooperative yield and a cancellation poll, or the executable grows the automatic pool at startup — the last of which introduces a new public policy value, a pool size, under the "Ada interface values" and "Decision authority" rules, and would only raise the threshold rather than remove it, as the `FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE=8` measurement shows. SERVER-EXE-2 is what leaves this path with no admission bound at all, so the two are best read together.

---

### SERVER-EXE-2: management route `Concurrency`/`Rate_Per_Second` policies are inert

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report said P2; unchanged). The library explicitly calls the consuming layers optional — `flyology-http-server-routing.ads:81`, "Route-local policy consumed incrementally by **optional** toolkit layers" — so this is not a violation of a library contract, and `server/README.md` promises no rate limit. What is wrong is that the server declares admission policy on five routes and never enforces it, and that `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` paces itself as though the limit existed.

**Location:** `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:882-887` (the `/api/login` registration with `Body_Handling => Apps.Buffer_Body, Max_Body => 256, Concurrency => 1, Rate_Per_Second => 2`), with the same shape on `/api/buckets` (GET and POST), `/api/objects`, `/api/buckets/delete` and `/api/logout` at `:888-918`; `:846-850`, where `Handle` calls `State.Routes.Serve` directly and `grep -rn -i 'middleware|bulkhead|rate_limit' server/src/` returns nothing (exit 1); the policy fields and "A zero concurrency or rate value means unlimited" at `flyology-http-server-routing.ads:81-89`; the unused consumer at `flyology-http-server-middleware_rate_limits.ads:24-25` ("Token-bucket middleware. Burst capacity equals the configured route requests-per-second rate. Rejection is 429 with Retry-After."); the maintained test's pacing at `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:102-117`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/shell/repro_server_exe_2.sh`, exit status 1, two failed expectations. It starts the server on a temporary root, fires 50 barrier-synchronised `POST /api/login` requests with a wrong 48-hex password from one peer, tallies the outcomes and `Retry-After` headers, and asserts that some are rejected with 429 or 503 and that at most the declared burst of 2 is admitted. Control: one wrong-password login on its own is answered 401, so the request shape is right and the flood measured real handler work.

**What is wrong:** Five management routes carry `Concurrency` and `Rate_Per_Second` values, but the router is served with no middleware, so the only readers of those fields, `Middleware_Rate_Limits` and `Middleware_Bulkheads`, are never instantiated and every management request from every peer is admitted up to the listener's 32 handler slots. Of 50 simultaneous `POST /api/login` attempts with a wrong password, **49 were run to completion** and answered 401, each paying a full 600,000-iteration derivation, 1 failed as a transport error (`RemoteDisconnected`), and **zero** received 429 or 503; the flood's wall time was **51.66 s**, min 1.05 s, median 27.08 s, max 51.66 s. The declared policy is `Concurrency => 1, Rate_Per_Second => 2`, whose token bucket has a burst capacity equal to the rate, so at most 2 should have been admitted. On its own this is a documentation-versus-code discrepancy; combined with SERVER-EXE-1 it is what removes any admission bound from the expensive login path, and it also means the `Concurrency => 1` intended to serialise bucket create and delete is absent, though the backends' own atomic primitives still make those operations safe.

**Notes:** Three refutation attempts failed. The router does not enforce the policy itself: no counter or bucket exists in `routing.adb` outside the middleware packages, and `Configure_Route` only stores the values. The burst did not merely happen to fit under the limit: the flood is barrier-synchronised, so all 50 requests are outstanding simultaneously, and 47–49 were nonetheless served. And the effective serialisation is not equivalent to a limiter: the requests are serialised by the single event loop of SERVER-EXE-1, so all of them run and the burst takes about 51 s of CPU instead of being rejected in milliseconds. An earlier run of the same script against the same server showed `47 x 401` and `3 x transport-error RemoteDisconnected`; the split between answered and dropped connections varies, the zero limiter rejections do not. Nothing in `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` asserts a 429 or 503, and its `sleep 1` between mutating requests passes with or without the limit, so no existing test changes with the fix. A fix needs a maintainer decision on the client key policy and the retained-key capacity that `Middleware_Rate_Limits` takes as generic formals; both are new externally visible resource choices under the "Decision authority" rule.

---

### SERVER-EXE-3: any `FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_ROOT` is handed to `Files.Open`/`SQLite.Open`, which delete `<root>/tmp`, `<root>/staging` and unreferenced `<root>/objects` files with no layout check

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report said P2; unchanged). The data destroyed is never the store's own acknowledged writes, and it needs a root that is not a dedicated directory, which the rubric puts at P2, "edge-case incorrectness … needing unusual input". It sits at the top of that band: the destruction is silent, irreversible, and reached through one documented variable whose documentation states no dedicated-directory precondition.

**Location:** `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_configuration.adb:42-45`, where the only check on the root is `Root'Length = 0` for non-memory backends; `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server.adb:64-68,78-82`, which pass `US.To_String (Settings.Storage_Root)` straight to `Files.Open` and `SQLite.Open` with no further validation; `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3514-3573` (`Open`) and specifically `:3548-3564`, which checks that the root is not a symlink and is a directory, creating it if absent, then for `<root>/tmp` checks not-a-symlink, is-a-directory, `Validate_Tree_Symlink_Free`, `Ada.Directories.Delete_Tree`, with nothing looking for a store marker or refusing a non-empty foreign directory; `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:364-365`, where `Temp_Path` is exactly `<root>/tmp`, so the swept directory is the store's staging area; `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:304-305`, the `Clean_Directory` calls over `<root>/staging` and `<root>/objects`; the documented precondition at `docs/architecture/files-layout.md:3` and `:66`; the variable's documentation at `server/README.md:64`; the existing coverage at `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:458-471`.

**Layout marker:** there is none. `docs/architecture/files-layout.md` was read in full and grepped for `marker|\.flyology|layout file|sentinel` with no match. The document states the precondition on the operator — "The files backend requires exclusive ownership of its configured root by one process" at line 3, and "opening the exclusively owned root removes interrupted staging artifacts" at line 66 — but defines no on-disk marker that `Open` could check, and `server/README.md:64` documents the variable as "mandatory for files and SQLite" without the precondition. The SQLite root's `catalog.sqlite3` and `.flyology-object-storage.lock` are created, not required, so they are not a marker either.

**Repro:** two programs, at both levels. The cheaper one is `tests/repros/src/repro_server_exe_3.adb`, exit status 1, two failed expectations: it creates a scratch directory with `tmp/keep.txt`, `tmp/nested/deep.txt` and `notes/readme.txt`, runs a control on a dedicated empty root (open plus create a bucket, which succeeds), then calls `Files.Open` on the foreign directory and asserts that `Open` either raised `Configuration_Error` or left the sentinels in place; `Open` returns normally, `tmp/keep.txt` and `tmp/nested/deep.txt` are gone, and `notes/readme.txt` survives. The second is `tests/repros/shell/repro_server_exe_3_server.sh`, exit status 1, four failed expectations, which drives the real executable through the documented variable for both backends: pointed at a files root holding `./notes/readme.txt`, `./tmp/keep.txt` and `./tmp/nested/deep.txt`, the server prints both `READY` lines and the root afterwards holds only `./notes/readme.txt`; pointed at a SQLite root holding `./notes/readme.txt`, `./objects/keep.txt` and `./staging/keep.txt`, the server prints both `READY` lines and the root afterwards holds `./.flyology-object-storage.lock`, `./catalog.sqlite3` and `./notes/readme.txt`.

**What is wrong:** `Configuration.Load` accepts any non-empty string as the storage root, and the executable passes it straight to the backend's `Open`, which treats the directory as a store it owns: the files backend recursively deletes `<root>/tmp`, and the SQLite backend deletes every ordinary file under `<root>/staging` and every ordinary file under `<root>/objects` that the freshly created catalog does not reference. Neither backend looks for any marker distinguishing a real store from an arbitrary directory, and no diagnostic is printed — the server prints `READY` and serves normally. An operator who mistypes the root (`/var` for `/var/lib/flyology`, a home directory with a `~/tmp`, a checkout with a scratch area) loses that content silently and irreversibly. On the macOS test host `/tmp` and `/var` are symlinks and are refused, so a Linux deployment is the exposed case. It is deterministic.

**Notes:** Shared responsibility. The destructive code belongs to the FILES and SQLITE backends (`src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3548-3564`; `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:304-305`); the reachable path from a documented, unvalidated configuration variable belongs to the executable (`server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_configuration.adb:42-45` and `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server.adb:64-68,78-82`). A fix could sit in either layer — an `Open` that refuses a non-empty directory without a layout marker, or an executable that validates the root before opening — and choosing between them, plus choosing whether to introduce a marker file, is a maintainer decision under "Decision authority", since a marker is a new on-disk artefact and a new externally visible precondition. Four refutation attempts failed: `Validate_Tree_Symlink_Free` does not protect a foreign tree, raising only for symlinks, non-ordinary files or depth above 64, and a sentinel tree of two ordinary files in two ordinary directories passed and was deleted; the sweep is not wider than the store's own directories, because the control shows `<root>/notes/readme.txt` surviving both backends, which is precisely what makes the loss invisible to an operator inspecting the rest of the root; the executable does not refuse a root it did not create, since both backends printed `READY` on the foreign roots; and no existing test covers it, because `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:458-471` always uses a fresh `$RUN_ROOT/<backend>-store` and no test points a store at a directory holding foreign content. SERVER-EXE-5 is adjacent but distinct and is counted separately: this finding is about a directory that was never a store, that one about a directory that is a store and is currently owned by a live process.

---

### SERVER-EXE-4: supervisor termination diagnostics are discarded

**Severity:** P2 (the discovery report said P2; unchanged). Wrong diagnostics on a valid and common input, a port already in use; no wrong data and no unsafety.

**Location:** `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:1075-1080`, where the report is `"object-storage server stopped: " & Result.Outcome'Image` and `Result.Termination` and `Result.Child` are never read; `:920-924`, where the management `Bind_Socket`/`Listen_Socket` have no handler, so `Socket_Error` escapes the generation; `flyology-supervision.ads:167-177,405-411`, where `Supervisor_Result` carries `Child`, `Generation` and a `Termination_Summary` with the exception id, exception name and up to 512 characters of message; the exception-swallowing handler at `runtime.adb:1082-1089`; the contrasting pre-supervisor path at `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server.adb:92-98`, which does print `Exception_Information`; the existing coverage at `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:343-385` (`expect_startup_failure`); the configuration gap at `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_configuration.adb:66-70`, which validates neither port availability nor equal S3 and management ports.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/shell/repro_server_exe_4.sh`, exit status 1, one failed expectation.
