It holds a loopback port with a `python3` listener, starts the executable with `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_PORT` set to that port, captures stdout and stderr, and asserts that the failure diagnostic names at least one of the failing child, the endpoint, or the exception. The diagnostic under test is exactly `object-storage server stopped: STARTUP_FAILED`; it names the failing child, no; the endpoint `127.0.0.1:53658`, no; the exception, no. Controls, all passing: the exit status is non-zero (1), the executable does report that the server stopped, and a pre-supervisor configuration failure does name its cause (`raised CONSTRAINT_ERROR : FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_BACKEND must be memory, files, or sqlite`).

**What is wrong:** When a child generation terminates with an exception during startup, `Flyology.Supervision.Static` copies the child's identity and a 512-character termination summary into `Supervisor_Result`, but the executable prints only `Result.Outcome'Image`. The exception never reaches GNAT's unhandled-exception reporter either, because the runtime's task termination hook captures it, so the operator gets one enumeral for every distinct failure: a port in use, a permission problem, a duplicated `FLYOLOGY_S3_PORT`/`FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_PORT` pair, or an exhausted restart budget all read as `STARTUP_FAILED` or `RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED`. The misleading half is visible in the reproduction output: the S3 child had already printed `READY s3 http://127.0.0.1:53670`, so an operator reading the output sees a successful listener followed by an unexplained `STARTUP_FAILED`. Anyone starting the server on a busy port hits it, and it is deterministic.

**Notes:** Three refutation attempts failed. `Exception_Information` does not reach stderr through the main procedure: `Runtime` catches everything at `runtime.adb:1082-1089` and returns normally, so main's handler is not involved for supervisor outcomes, and the control at the end of the program confirms the contrast, a pre-supervisor configuration failure naming its cause. The "admin" text that appears in the output is not a diagnostic: it is the `BOOTSTRAP ADMIN` banner and the credentials path, printed on every run, which is why the program scopes its assertion to the `object-storage server stopped:` line and everything after it — an earlier draft of the script matched the banner and produced a false PASS, and the scoped version is the one reported here. And `Configuration.Load` does not reject the conflict earlier, validating neither port availability nor equal ports (`configuration.adb:66-70`), so the supervisor is the first place the conflict is seen. `server/tests/scripts/test.sh:343-385` asserts only a non-zero exit and every case it runs fails before the supervisor starts, where `server.adb:92-98` does print `Exception_Information`, so no existing test asserts the wrong behaviour and none must change. A fix is a formatting decision — which of `Child`, `Generation`, `Termination.Exception_Name` and `Termination.Message` to print, and whether the child ids get names — rather than a new interface value, so it does not need a "Decision authority" ruling.

---

### SERVER-EXE-5: the executable enforces the documented exclusive-root precondition for SQLite but not for files

**Severity:** P2 (new; the verifier raised it from the discovery report's observations list, where the missing files-root lock was recorded and deferred to the FILES area). The deterministic part is the missing enforcement and the emptying of a live process's staging directory; the harmful part, destroying a staging file the first process is actually mid-way through writing, requires a timing race that was not demonstrated, and the destroyed data would be transient staging rather than acknowledged writes. It is not P1 because the exclusive-ownership statement in `docs/architecture/files-layout.md` is a precondition on the operator rather than a promise by the code; the verifier recorded that a maintainer who reads the SQLite backend's `flock` as establishing that enforcement is the contract would reasonably raise it to P1.

**Location:** `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:3514-3573` (`Open` takes no lock of any kind; the only guards are symlink and directory-kind checks); `src/flyology-object_storage-backends-files.adb:364-365` and the 18 uses of `Temp_Path (Item)` in the same body, with bucket-creation staging at `:3606` and publication staging at `:3902`, `:4142`, `:4388`, `:4686`, `:5195` and `:6122`, so `<root>/tmp` is the live staging target of ordinary operations rather than a startup-only directory; the multipart tree that is not swept, `files.adb:402-403,524-530`; the contrasting SQLite enforcement at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:107-125` (`Acquire_Root_Lock`), called from `Open` at `:301-302`, taking `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on `<root>/.flyology-object-storage.lock` and raising `Configuration_Error : could not acquire exclusive SQLite backend root lock`; the documented preconditions at `docs/architecture/files-layout.md:3` ("The files backend requires exclusive ownership of its configured root by one process") and `README.md:36` ("one process owning an exclusive storage root").

**Repro:** `tests/repros/shell/repro_server_exe_5.sh`, exit status 1, two failed expectations. It starts server 1 on a fresh files root, places a sentinel inside the live `<root>/tmp/`, starts server 2 on the same root, and asserts that server 2 is refused or that the sentinel survives. Server 2 reached `READY` (both lines), server 1's `tmp/owner-staging.tmp` did not survive, and server 1 was still alive. The sentinel stands in for server 1's transient staging artefacts and is created by the script — the program says so in its header, and it does **not** claim to have destroyed a real in-flight staging file. Control, passing: the identical two-process attempt on a SQLite root is refused, and the refusal names the reason (`raised FLYOLOGY.OBJECT_STORAGE.BACKENDS.SQLITE.CONFIGURATION_ERROR : could not acquire exclusive SQLite backend root lock`).

**What is wrong:** The server executable offers one `FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_ROOT` variable for both persistent backends, and the exclusive-ownership precondition the architecture documents is enforced for exactly one of them. Pointing a second server at a live files root — a restart that overlaps the old process, a misconfigured supervisor unit, an operator running a second instance for a different port — succeeds silently, and the second `Open`'s `Delete_Tree` of `<root>/tmp` removes whatever the first process has staged there at that instant. Ordinary publication paths stage in exactly that directory, so a concurrent PUT, tag replacement, bucket creation or multipart completion can lose its staged record and fail, or at the margins leave the first process to `Sync_Directory` a directory that was replaced underneath it. The narrow, timing-dependent nature of the harm is why this is P2 rather than higher, and it is why the deterministic part of the reproduction is the missing refusal rather than a corrupted object.

**Notes:** Shared responsibility with the FILES and SQLITE areas: the asymmetry is between the two backends' `Open` (`files.adb:3514-3573` takes no lock; `sqlite.adb:107-125` `Acquire_Root_Lock`, called from `Open` at `:301-302`), while what belongs to this area is that the executable offers one variable for both and enforces the documented precondition for only one of them. Four refutation attempts failed. The harm is not confined to startup: `Temp_Path` is the staging target of every publication path listed in the Location field, so the swept directory is in use for the whole lifetime of the first process. In-flight multipart state is not destroyed too, which would have made it worse: uploads live under `buckets/<bucket>/multipart/` (`files.adb:402-403,524-530`), which the sweep does not touch, and that bound on the damage is why the severity is P2 rather than P1. The executable does not refuse the second start for another reason, since the second server printed both `READY` lines while the first was still alive. And it is not the same defect as SERVER-EXE-3: that one is about a directory that was never a store, this one about a directory that is a store, currently owned by a live process, and the asymmetry with the SQLite backend is what makes it a defect rather than a design choice. `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` never runs two servers on one root, so no existing test changes with the fix. A fix is a maintainer decision on mechanism: the files backend could take the same `flock` the SQLite backend already takes, at the cost of making the files backend depend on a lock file inside its root, which is a new on-disk artefact and a new failure mode on filesystems without working advisory locks.

---

## MODEL: the pinned botocore model, the generator, the descriptor and the registry tools (no findings)

The area covers the pinned model `review/baseline/service-2.json`, the generator `tools/generate-s3-model.py`, the committed descriptor `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.ads` and its 78,882-line generated body `-s3-model.adb`, the registry and codegen tools `tools/s3_operation.py`, `tools/s3-operation.py`, `tools/s3_codegen.py`, `tools/verify-botocore-model.sh` and `tools/verify-coverage.sh`, the ledger files under `coverage/`, the five committed generated XML serializers `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-generated_*.adb`, and every descriptor consumer in `src/` — `client-low_level.adb`, `s3-listings.adb`, `s3-versions.adb`, `s3-multipart.adb`, `s3-multipart_uploads.adb` and `server-s3_applications.adb` — read against `docs/architecture/s3-operation-automation.md` and the CI wiring in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; the discovery report found no actionable finding and the verification upheld that.

The verification did not re-run the generator, on the ground that regenerating and diffing (what `generate --check` and the discovery both did) proves only that the generator is a function of its input and cannot detect a rule the generator itself gets wrong. It instead ran a generator-independent differential, `tests/repros/python/model_differential.py` — python3 standard library only, no Alire build, nothing tracked touched — which parses the committed Ada text directly with a line-exact parser that raises on any unrecognised line, re-derives every expected value from the pinned JSON with the REST-XML rules written out in its own source, and compares the two. It exits **0** (0 = no mismatch, 1 = at least one mismatch, 2 = missing input), runs in about a second and is byte-for-byte deterministic across repeated runs. It compared 116 operations, 718 shapes, 1,699 structure members, 279 enumeration values in 75 shapes and 38 error-shape links across 20 distinct error shapes, over all 41 accessors declared in the `.ads` (29 flat, 12 indexed) and 30,303 value arms plus 3,607 `when others` defaults — 33,910 of the 33,910 `return` statements in the body, an identity the program asserts so that "no mismatch" cannot mean "did not look" — together with 35 operations carrying `httpChecksum`, 96 using the `responseCode` 200 default, 99 `requestUri`s with a query literal and 30 with a greedy label, 8 shapes with `min`, 7 with `max`, 4 with `pattern`, 8 with `timestampFormat`, 1 with `xmlNamespace` and 35 flattened, 63 payload shapes, 7 streaming members and 6 header maps, and 7,615 emitted strings screened for non-ASCII (0 unsafe); the recomputed digest `429763d64912af5edae4c7a0f20a8ac3e6fecf734cde5fc465016bc8badcdef9` matches the `.ads` constant, `tools/generate-s3-model.py:15-19`, `tools/verify-botocore-model.sh:12` and `coverage/s3-operations.toml:5`. That the checker is not vacuous was established by a mutation control: twenty single-token mutations were applied to a copy of `src/` under the scratchpad directory — never the worktree — and all twenty were caught with exit 1 and a first-line diagnosis naming the right element, spanning a changed `Request_URI`, `Response_Code` and HTTP method, one member `locationName`, one enumeration value, the spec SHA pin and `Shape_Count`, a `Minimum`, an `Is_Flattened`, a `Timestamp_Format`, a `Request_Checksum_Required`, a deleted `Shape_Name` case arm, two swapped `Member_Name` values, a `Member_Required`, a `Member_Shape`, an `Error_Shape`, a `Location`, and three consumer member ordinals.

## BASELINE: maintained gates and the CI evidence behind them (BASELINE-2)

The area covers the gate run recorded in `review/baseline-notes.md` and its logs under `review/baseline/`, with `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh`, `tests/scripts/run-s3-server-slice.sh` and the three reference scripts `tests/scripts/test-{minio,rustfs,seaweedfs}.sh` read as the implementation, and the scheduled-run logs `review/baseline/ci-blackbox-job.log` and `review/baseline/ci-rustfs-job.log` as the evidence.

### BASELINE-2: The two s5cmd-driven CI jobs report success while executing nothing

**Severity:** P1. No verification report covers this area, so this is the drafter's assignment under the header rubric: the jobs are the repository's own interop gate, they report success on runs in which the conformance script demonstrably failed, and a maintainer reading a green scheduled run is told something untrue about a reachable, deterministic path. The assembler should confirm the severity before filing.

**Location:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:271-272` (`./tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh memory 2>&1 | tee obj/ci/s3-smoke.log`, the "Run digest-pinned s5cmd oracle against memory backend" step declared at `:268`) and `:304-305` (`./tests/scripts/test-${{ matrix.reference }}.sh 2>&1 | tee obj/ci/reference-${{ matrix.reference }}.log`, the "Run bounded permissive-reference slice" step declared at `:299`, matrixed over `rustfs` and `seaweedfs`); the whole file contains no `shell:` key and no `set -o pipefail`, so both steps run under GitHub's default `bash -e {0}`; the container-side endpoint at `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh:95` (`http://host.docker.internal:$PORT`) and the s5cmd invocation at `tests/scripts/run-s3-server-slice.sh:60`; the loopback publications at `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:26`, `tests/scripts/test-rustfs.sh:29` and `tests/scripts/test-seaweedfs.sh:34` (`--publish "127.0.0.1::<port>"`) and the loopback bind of the Flyology test server at `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb:120-121` (`Sockets.Loopback_IPv4`); the failure the script itself does detect at `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh:97-101`; evidence at `review/baseline/ci-blackbox-job.log:1611,1613` and `review/baseline/ci-rustfs-job.log:183`; recorded as note 3 of `review/baseline-notes.md`.

**Repro:** `tests/repros/shell/repro_baseline_2.sh`, exit status 1. It asserts the correct behaviour — a step written the way the workflow writes it must fail when the script fails — running the verbatim step shape under `bash -e` (fails the assertion), the same shape under `bash -eo pipefail` (control, passes), a demonstration of what `--publish 127.0.0.1::9000` binds to, and a grep showing `ci.yml` sets neither `shell: bash` nor `pipefail`.

**What is wrong:** Two independent causes make three CI jobs incapable of failing. First, both steps pipe the conformance script into `tee` under the default `bash -e` shell with no `pipefail`, so the step's exit status is `tee`'s and a failing script never fails the job. Second, the servers are reachable only on the host loopback — the reference servers are published with `--publish 127.0.0.1::<port>` and the Flyology memory server binds loopback directly — while the s5cmd oracle runs in a sibling container reaching `host.docker.internal`, which on Linux resolves to the docker0 gateway 172.17.0.1 and cannot connect. The observable consequence is that in the scheduled run 34031107803 of 2026-09-06 the jobs "Reference smoke (rustfs)", "Reference smoke (seaweedfs)" and "S3 black-box smoke (memory + s5cmd)" all report success while their logs show `ERROR "mb s3://...": ... dial tcp 172.17.0.1:<port>: connect: connection refused` followed by "Flyology memory endpoint runner failed" and nothing further. Everyone who reads a green scheduled run is affected: the s5cmd byte and deletion oracles that the repository relies on as its stock-client evidence have never actually run in CI, so no interop regression on those routes can be caught there. It is fully deterministic on a Linux runner, and both causes are visible statically in the maintained workflow and scripts. On macOS Docker the second problem does not arise, which is why the same scripts run far enough locally to expose CLIENT-LOW-1.

**Notes:** BASELINE-1 — the client's HeadObject post-check rejecting every third-party server — was reclassified as a client defect during the RustFS investigation (`review/baseline-rustfs.md`) and is filed as CLIENT-LOW-1, with the same predicate in the objects layer as CLIENT-OBJECTS-1; it is not listed under BASELINE. The two causes here are independent and should be fixed together: adding `shell: bash` (or `set -o pipefail`) to the two steps alone would turn the jobs red without making them meaningful, because the endpoint would still be unreachable, so a maintainer has to decide both how the step status is propagated and how the s5cmd container reaches the server on Linux (publishing on `0.0.0.0` and passing the runner's docker0 address, running s5cmd on the host network, or putting server and client on the same user-defined network). The local gate run recorded in `review/baseline-notes.md` shows what the CI jobs would have reported had they run: the three Flyology backends pass the full corpus, s5cmd byte and deletion oracles and restart routing, while all three third-party servers fail at the same exchange for the reason CLIENT-LOW-1 describes. Note 1 of the same record is not a defect: the first `tests/scripts/test.sh` run failed only because `FLYOLOGY_S3_SERVICE_MODEL` was unset locally, and CI sets it at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:118`.

---

## Appendix A: coverage evidence per area

### WIRE

- `Wire_Core.Parse_Natural` and `Parse_Byte_Count`: the overflow guard `Result > (T'Last - Digit) / 10` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-wire_core.adb:16-19,35-38` is exact for every digit, and the same guard in `Core.Parse_Decimal` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-core.adb:86-90`; measured, `0` and `007` are accepted, `+1`, `-1`, `" 1"`, `"1 "` and the empty string are rejected, and values above the target's range are rejected, matching the HTTP `1*DIGIT` production.
- `Wire_Core.Parse_Boolean` and `Valid_Base64` canonical padding-bit tables; every caller passes 16, 32 or an algorithm digest size, satisfying the precondition.
- `Core.Parse_Range_Header` length gate, case-insensitive unit, OWS handling, `bytes=-` and `bytes=-0`; `Resolve_Range` for all four kinds including the last-position clamp and `Byte_Count'Last` operands; the proved postcondition matches the GNATdoc.
- `Core.Multipart_Part_Count`, `Valid_Multipart_Plan`, `Valid_Completion_Order`, `Valid_Consecutive_Completion_Order`, `Can_Transition`, `Valid_Part_Size`, `Valid_Listing_Continuation_Syntax`.
- `Requests.Parse_Target`, `Decode` and `Valid_Escapes`: the 8192 gate, strict `%XX`, `+` as a literal, `#` rejection and the 1024-byte decoded key at the bound; measured classification of `/`, `//`, `///`, `/bkt`, `/bkt/`, `/bkt//`, `/bkt/.`, `/bkt/..`, `/bkt/%2e%2e/x`, `/./`, `/../`, `%2F` inside a key, `/%62kt/k`, `/bkt/k#frag` and a bare `?acl` is correct for S3, which treats keys as opaque and performs no dot-segment resolution.
- `IMF_Dates.Parse` and `Image`: measured weekday congruence, impossible and leap dates, second 60 normalization, and rejection of RFC 850, asctime, `0` and `-1`, all matching the deliberately non-generic contract at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-imf_dates.ads:1-3,16-19`; the conditional-date path that RFC 9110 does require to accept all three formats does accept all three at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-object_reads.adb:84-124`.
- `XML.Parse` DOCTYPE, processing-instruction and entity rejection, `Test_Valid_Chars`, depth, element, text and document limits with exact at-bound semantics, XMLAda parser-state release on both paths, BOM handling, and `Close` on every path; `XML.Escape`'s six-times expansion bound and attribute versus text escaping sets.
- `XML_Writers` state machine and limits; `Strict_XML_Codecs` root name, unknown member, duplicate singleton, collection and attribute-count limits, required-member check, `Valid_ISO_8601_Timestamp` and `Valid_Integer`.
- Root validators: `Valid_Bucket_Name` character, adjacency, prefix and IPv4 rules, `Looks_Like_IPv4`, `Starts_With` and `Ends_With` slice bounds, `Listing_Matches_Prefix`, `Valid_Object_Delete_ETag_Condition`, the read, write, copy and delete condition precedence rules, `Valid_Tag_Text`, `Valid_Object_Metadata` budgets, `Valid_Object_Annotation_Name` and `Merge_Bucket_Versioning`.
- Just-outside consumers: `max-keys`, `max-uploads`, `max-parts`, `partNumber` and `part-number-marker` range-check before the subtype conversion, the guarded `'Value` uses in `s3_applications.adb:2312-2316` and `client-objects.adb:2374-2376`, and `flyology_http`'s rejection of raw target bytes at or below 32, 127 and above 127, which keeps `Send_Error`'s `Escape_Text (Target_Text)` from raising inside the catch-all.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: `max-keys=0`, `1000` and `00001000` answer 200 and `1001`, `-1`, `+1` and a twenty-digit value answer 400, which is the documented `docs/compatibility/s3.md:863` contract implemented at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:105-121`; the bare query key `?acl` is accepted and `?zzz` rejected, which is the documented policy at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:581-582` and `s3_applications.adb:6659-6663`; Range shapes other than an unrecognized unit are classified and answered as documented.

### SIGV4

- `SigV4.Sign` canonical request: method token validation, single URI encoding of the decoded path with `/` preserved, canonical query encoding and sort order matching botocore's `_canonical_query_string_url`, canonical header lowercasing, trimming, folding, stable sort and repeated-name joining, exactly one `host`, and payload-hash validation; all four AWS S3 published examples are asserted byte for byte on canonical-request hash and signature in `Check_SigV4_Official_Vectors` at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:8905-9040`.
- `SigV4.Sign` string-to-sign and key derivation, including the full key chain and the wiping of every derived key and the prefixed secret on both normal and exceptional exit; `SigV4.Constant_Time_Equal` at `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4.adb:322-342`, re-derived by the verifier, folds the length difference into the accumulator and has no data-dependent exit.
- `SigV4_Encoding` under SPARK: `URI_Encode`'s three-times bound and loop invariant, ASCII-only `Lowercase`, `Normalize_Header_Value` trimming and folding, calendar-aware `Valid_Timestamp`, and the tchar and character-set validators.
- `SigV4_Verification.Parse`: prefix and single space, attribute splitting, rejection of duplicates and unknown attributes, the five-part scope with literal `aws4_request`, `SignedHeaders` required to be lowercase, tchar, strictly ascending and to include `host`, `x-amz-content-sha256` and `x-amz-date`, a 64-lowercase-hex signature, bounded inputs, and `Constraint_Error` mapped to `Malformed_Authorization`.
- `SigV4_Verification.Verify`: origin-form targets only, strict percent-escape validation that fails closed, query splitting before decoding so `%26` and `%3D` inside values are preserved, the `s3` service pin, the scope-date equality check, the payload-hash equality check, exact `SignedHeaders` agreement, constant-time signature comparison, and normalization consistent with MinIO's `EncodePath` and with the crate's own client.
- `Authentication.Verify_Request` admission rules: exactly one `authorization`, `x-amz-date` and `x-amz-content-sha256`; `STREAMING-*` hashes failing closed with 400; every physical `x-amz-*` header required in `SignedHeaders`; the session-token singleton rule; two-directional skew against a caller-supplied `Now`; the optional region pin; `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` only on `Secure_HTTPS`; per-occurrence header collection in wire order bounded at 256.
- Body-hash enforcement at `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:4055-4059` and every `Expected_Hash => Auth.Payload_Hash` site: the streamed body's SHA-256 is compared with the signed digest and a started response is failed rather than completed.
- `Static_Credentials` alphanumeric access keys, bounded copies into provider-owned fixed arrays, constant-time session-token comparison in both directions, and `Finalize` wiping all four buffers; `MFA` bounded copies and control-byte rejection; `Secrets` and its C shim volatile wipe loop with zero-length guards and wiping on exceptional paths.
- Client `low_level` signing on both preparers: the decoded path is signed and the encoded path sent, the query is canonicalised once, `host`, `x-amz-content-sha256`, `x-amz-date` and any session-token header are always signed, `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` is refused off HTTPS, and the 8,192-byte target bound mirrors the server.
- Verifier probe `tests/repros/src/repro_sigv4_probe.adb` passes with exit status 0 and is the evidence for seven checks: `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` is genuinely refused on plain HTTP with 400 and the body is not stored; two `Authorization` fields are rejected 400; header auth plus a presigned-style query is refused 400 by the operation's query validator without an internal error; a padded, mixed-case signed header verifies; a declared but absent signed header is rejected 400; an unsigned physical `x-amz-*` header is rejected 400; a scope date differing from `x-amz-date` is rejected 400; an uppercase `x-amz-content-sha256` is rejected 400 at admission; a presigned-style request is a clean 403 AccessDenied; and a body that does not match the signed hash is 400 XAmzContentSHA256Mismatch with nothing published.
- Read-only checks with no probe: request trailers are not an unsigned-input hole, because the `x-amz-trailer` header must itself be signed and `Reject_Unexpected_Trailers` at `s3_applications.adb:4073-4078` rejects any undeclared trailer; `x-amz-s3session-token` must be signed and there are no session credentials to confuse it with; `Verify`'s `Signed_Headers = Declared_Signed_Headers` check is a real guard only for third-party providers, since the server path builds the array from the declared list itself.

### SERVER

- Authentication ordering and route agreement: `Verify_Request` runs once at `:6697`, after classification and before any body byte or backend call, over the same target and method strings the application classifies, so the signed target and the executed bucket, key and query cannot diverge; every arm runs after the `Authenticated` check.
- Pre-authentication classification was probed directly and refuted as a disclosure concern: an unauthenticated request gets 403 `AccessDenied` for existing and absent buckets and keys, unknown and implemented subresources, and an object-only subresource on a bucket; only a target the parser rejects (400 `InvalidURI`) and `OPTIONS`/`PATCH` (501) answer earlier, both purely syntactic and independent of stored state, and nothing reaches a backend.
- Exception containment was measured over the loopback listener: a signed PutObject declaring `Content-Length: 16` with only 4 body bytes produces no escaping exception and no deadlock — the server waits for the missing bytes and closes, the next request on the listener is served normally, and in process the same shape is answered 400 `InvalidRequest`.
- Request bodies: every buffered `Request_Source` sets a `Maximum`; `Request_IO.Read` rejects over- and under-length bodies against `Content-Length` and verifies `x-amz-content-sha256`, Content-MD5, header and trailer checksums before `Finished`, and every commit path asserts `Source.Completed`, so a backend cannot commit an unverified body.
- Response framing: `Begin_Object` validates the backend's announced range against the snapshot, emits a quoted ETag, IMF-fixdate `Last-Modified`, `Accept-Ranges`, exact 64-bit `Content-Length` and 206/200; `Write` enforces the announced length; unsatisfiable ranges return 416 with `Content-Range: bytes */size`.
- Conditional reads: duplicate, empty and malformed `If-*` headers are 400; precedence is delegated to the core evaluators; 304 responses carry ETag and `Last-Modified`; CopyObject source conditions map `Not_Modified` to 412.
- Error XML and containment: `Send_Error` always emits Code, Message, Resource and RequestId; the status mapping never sends a second response once the wire has started (`Wire_Response_Started`/`Mark_Failed`), and every unexpected Ada exception is converted rather than escaping `Handle`.
- Header emission safety: PutObject, CopyObject and CreateMultipartUpload metadata values and `response-*` overrides are validated more strictly than `flyology_http`'s own check, so no stored value can make `Set_Header` raise on read.
- Multipart, DeleteObjects, listings and copy: part-number bounds and initiation reconciliation; quiet mode, request-order results and the 1,000-key bound; `max-keys`/`max-parts`/`max-uploads`/`max-buckets` mapping with no `Constraint_Error` path, v1 `NextMarker` only with a delimiter, v2 `KeyCount` including common prefixes and continuation-token validation; `x-amz-copy-source` parsed by the same strict target parser with the copy version headers emitted on success.
- Resource bounds: object bodies stream through 16 KiB reads and backend sinks with no whole-object buffering; every backend call receives the cancellation token and deadline; cancellation and timeout re-raise after `Mark_Failed` and are handled by closing the connection.

### CHECKSUM

- `Checksum_CRC.Make_Table`, `Update` and `Finish`: reflected table-driven CRC with all-ones init and final XOR for CRC32 (0xEDB88320), CRC32C (0x82F63B78) and CRC64NVME (0x9A6C9329AC4BC9B5, verified as the bit reflection of 0xAD93D23594C93659); the corpus "123456789" vectors encode the published check values.
- `Checksum_CRC.Combine`, `Matrix_Square` and `Matrix_Times`: zlib `crc32_combine` generalised to 64 bits, verified against an independent polynomial combiner up to 2^63-1, with a correct zero-length operand, 32-bit masking and correct loop variants; the corpus's large-length expected values were reproduced by a different method.
- `Checksums.Encode_Base64`, `Decode_Base64` and `Wire_Core.Valid_Base64`: exact encoded length per algorithm, standard alphabet only, required padding, zero unused pad bits, no whitespace; the decode loop was traced for 4, 8, 16, 20, 32 and 64-byte digests.
- `Checksums.Composite`, `Full_Object`, `Encode_Object` and `Decode_Object`: raw big-endian part digests concatenated in manifest order (which completion requires to be strictly ascending and, for composites, consecutive from 1), the `Combine` left-fold using each right part's stored size, the exact `-<N>` suffix including `-1`, and strict suffix and part-count rejection on decode.
- `Checksum_Policy` and `Checksum_Engine`: the multipart algorithm matrix, `Default_Type`, `Ordinary_Object_Supported`, and the `Valid_*`/`Matches_Stored_Object_Digest`/`Multipart_Object_Value` predicates, including that the multipart-only `Policy.Supported` gate is never reached for direct-put full-object digests.
- `Checksums.Context` state machine: `Initialize` resets, `Finish` is non-destructive for every algorithm, and `Update` after `Finish`, `Reset`, empty slices and arbitrary chunk boundaries (210 corpus combinations) are correct; no per-context byte counter exists to overflow.
- xxHash bridge: the vendored header is v0.8.3 as claimed, the struct size and alignment are checked at every reset, `XXH3_128bits_reset` is literally `XXH3_64bits_reset` so sharing it is correct, NULL is passed only with length 0, and the canonical byte orders match the `xxhsum` oracles the corpus generator uses.
- Server trailer path: `x-amz-trailer` must name exactly the SDK algorithm's header, a value header and a trailer cannot be combined, trailer access happens only after `Finished`, the trailer value is canonical-validated and compared with the streamed digest, and the response echoes the backend-computed value with `x-amz-checksum-type`.
- Stock-client probe (boto3 1.43.89, exit status 1 on the two `requestChecksumRequired` cases only): `put_object` with defaults and with an explicit algorithm, `create_multipart_upload`, two `upload_part` calls, `complete_multipart_upload`, and `head_object`/`get_object` with `ChecksumMode=ENABLED` over three keys all returned 200, with the composite `-2` value surfaced and 10,485,760 bytes streamed without a validation error.
- Two discovery questions settled by that probe: over plain HTTP with a seekable body botocore sends a plain body with a precomputed checksum header rather than `aws-chunked`, so the 501 trailer boundary was never exercised; and botocore sends no object-level checksum header at all on `CompleteMultipartUpload`, only `x-amz-checksum-type: COMPOSITE` plus per-part elements, so the server's raw-form requirement is never exercised by boto3.

### BACKEND-MEMORY

- `Owned_Bytes`: `Adjust` deep-copies, `Move` transfers ownership and zeroes the source, `Append` and `Reserve_Capacity` guard `Natural'Last`, and no aliasing exists between the store and returned snapshots.
- Reservation protocol: every path in `Put_Object_Internal`, `Put_Multipart_Part`, `Put_Object_Annotation`, `Copy_Object`, `Copy_Multipart_Part` and `Get_Object` releases exactly `Data.Capacity`; after a successful commit the buffer is moved so no double release is possible, and failed commits leave the reservation for the caller.
- `Commit`: conditions are evaluated against the current non-marker generation inside the protected action, overwrite capacity is computed correctly, the publication order is burned before the first mutation, and the unconfigured, suspended and enabled paths match the contract.
- Delete paths: unversioned physical delete, enabled marker append (also for absent keys, as S3 does), suspended null-marker replacement releasing the null generation's bytes, exact and null removal with legal-hold and retention refusal, MFA gating, and a full slot reset on every removal; batch behaviour matches the architecture document.
- Versioning transitions: `Merge_Bucket_Versioning` never returns to unconfigured, suspension is refused under Object Lock, and Object Lock, legal hold and retention all require enabled versioning.
- Listing builders apart from BACKEND-MEMORY-1: bytewise key ordering, prefix filter, delimiter projection, multi-byte delimiters, key-equals-prefix listed as an object, deduplicated common prefixes, exclusive `After` on the projected key, and the smallest `Maximum + 1` retention that makes `Is_Truncated` exact; `List_Versions` key-ascending, publication-descending, `Is_Latest` across markers, and paired-marker resume.
- Multipart lifecycle: unique per-store upload IDs, part overwrite releasing the old capacity, strictly-after `List_Parts` with exact truncation, completion validating ascending unique numbers, existence, ETag and checksum match, the 5 MiB non-final minimum and the destination conditions before publishing atomically, and `Delete_Bucket` refusing with active uploads.
- `Get_Object`: the snapshot is copied under the lock so a concurrent overwrite or delete cannot change the streamed bytes, `Resolve_Range` clamps and rejects as documented, and `Begin_Object` is called exactly once.
- Bucket configuration byte accounting: every put charges base plus incoming and every delete and bucket delete subtracts the same lengths, with no asymmetric path found; and no potentially blocking operation appears inside the protected object, with every compound operation a single protected action.
- Probe `tests/repros/src/repro_backend_memory_probe.adb`, exit status 0 (PASS): `Bytes_Used` returned to exactly the retained baseline after three successful GETs, a GET whose sink raised in `Begin_Object`, a GET whose sink raised in `Write`, a conditional GET rejected after reservation, an unsatisfiable-range GET, a copy followed by a delete, a conditional PUT rejected at publication, a capacity-rejected PUT and a multipart stage-then-abort, and to 0 after teardown — so the `Adjust` accounting anomaly below has no reachable underflow or leak.

### BACKEND-FILES

- Key path encoding is injective (uppercase hex only, no `.`, `..` or separator components, no case-folding collisions, distinct `.fos`/`.versions`/`.annotations` suffixes), and every path component derived from an untrusted identifier goes through SHA-256 or `Valid_Bucket_Name`; exact version IDs are compared against a directory scan and never composed into a path.
- Symlink handling checks every path symlink-first before kind or existence, `Validate_Immediate_Directory` uses raw `readdir` so dangling links are detected, and TOCTOU against a hostile local process is explicitly out of contract (`docs/architecture/files-layout.md:80-85`).
- Publication gate discipline: all 55 `Acquire_Publication` sites release on every normal and exceptional path (235 `Release` calls inspected), `Acquire_Publication` itself releases on `Validate_Required_Roots` failure, and the timed entry loop honours deadline and cancellation.
- Publication sequences (temp under `tmp/`, `Validate_New_Temp_Target`, `Sync_File`, rename, `Sync_Directory` of destination then `tmp/`) for object PUT, tags, object-lock rewrite, versioning, Object Lock enablement, bucket tags, policy, CORS, every singleton and named configuration document, metadata state, bucket create and delete, multipart initiate, part, completion and abort; temp files and directories are removed on every failure path inspected.
- Conditional writes are evaluated under the gate against the freshly read header immediately before rename in PUT, COPY and multipart completion, and two concurrent create-if-absent writers serialize correctly.
- Generation selection: current, null and exact semantics; the legacy `objects/` file as null version; delete-marker placement for enabled and suspended buckets; tombstone-based exact-null deletion with `Recover_Null_Tombstones` on `Open`; `Remove_Superseded_Null_Generations` also removes sidecars.
- ListObjectVersions bytewise key order, newest-publication-first within a key, `IsLatest` for markers, key and version marker resume, and common-prefix cursor advancement and dedup; ListObjects lists only the current non-marker generation per key.
- Record format: bounded lengths for every header field, exact file-size check against `Body_At + Size`, in-place patch offsets (`Body_Size_Position` 29, `Metadata_Position` 37) verified against the layout, part metadata monotonic and size-summed, checksum and method consistency.
- Streaming reads hold the file descriptor across gate release, so a concurrent unlink, tag rewrite or abort cannot tear a reader on POSIX; every `File_Type`, `Search_Type` and `Dir_Type` inspected is closed in its matching handler; the durability bridge frees its C string on every path and uses `F_FULLFSYNC` with an `fsync` fallback, and `secure_erase` is a volatile byte loop.
- Verifier checks with no finding: the S3 adapter does not clamp policies before they reach the backend, GNAT does not heap-allocate a large dynamically sized local, versioned buckets do not share the annotation-sidecar identity, tombstones do not keep the generation counter high, and no key check exists before `Read_Manifest` in any of the four multipart entry points.

### BACKEND-SQLITE

- `Databases` binding layer: text and blob binds copy through `sqlite3_malloc64` with `SQLITE_TRANSIENT`, all integers use `bind_int64` and `column_int64`, object keys, version IDs, tags, metadata, entity tags and documents bind as BLOB while bucket names and payload names bind as TEXT with `COLLATE BINARY`, `column_text` and `column_blob` are read before `column_bytes`, every statement is a stack-local controlled object finalized on scope exit so none outlives an exception, `Reset` clears bindings, and `Step` maps every non-ROW and non-DONE code to `SQLite_Error`.
- `Open`: `trusted_schema=OFF`, `foreign_keys=ON` verified, application id and `user_version` checked, an empty database created under `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE`, a nonempty unrecognized database and a newer schema refused, the V1 to V22 upgrades each in one exclusive transaction with rollback on failure, WAL and `synchronous=FULL` verified after migration, the table and column validators, and `quick_check`; the catalog handle is closed and the gate released on any failure. The root lock is acquired before the catalog opens, staging and unreferenced objects are reclaimed before serving, and both directories are synced.
- Versioning merge: `CASE WHEN ?2=0 THEN versioning_status ELSE ?2` and the MFA twin match `Merge_Bucket_Versioning`'s postcondition exactly, because `Versioning_Unconfigured` and `MFA_Delete_Unconfigured` are position 0 (`src/flyology-object_storage.ads:230-240`), the columns are `NOT NULL` with `CHECK ... BETWEEN 0 AND 2`, and both parameters bind as integers so no NULL can enter the CASE. MFA and Object-Lock admission is read and the row published under the same gate.
- PUT publication order matches `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:8-14`: exclusive staging create, fsync (`F_FULLFSYNC` then `fsync`), rename, objects-directory sync, then one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that evaluates write conditions against the current mirror, upserts the mirror, replaces metadata, tags and parts, publishes the generation per versioning mode, copies annotations and commits with `synchronous=FULL`; a non-success or raised catalog result deletes the new payload, and the previous payload is removed only after commit. Payload names are SHA-256 of bucket, key, sequence, pid and clock rather than of content, so equal bodies never share a name and `payload UNIQUE` cannot fire on valid input.
- Delete paths: `Delete_Selected_Object` and `Delete_Objects` run `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, read versioning and MFA once, evaluate `Require_Unversioned` and MFA before the first entry, evaluate conditions per entry, publish opaque or null markers with publication-order verification, refresh the current mirror, and roll back the whole batch on any raise; retired payloads are reclaimed only after commit and only when unreferenced.
- Multipart: `Put_Multipart_Part`, `Complete_Multipart_Upload` and `Abort_Multipart_Upload` each run one transaction; completion re-validates every selected part's payload, size, modified time, entity tag and checksum inside the transaction (`Conflict` on drift) and enforces ascending order, composite contiguity, the 5 MiB minimum for nonfinal parts, capacity, expected size and expected checksum, then retires the upload and its parts atomically.
- Listing: bucket, part, annotation and point-configuration pages use keyset predicates with `LIMIT Maximum+1`; object and version listings order by BLOB `object_key` bytewise and by `publication_order DESC`; delimiter projection and the paired key and version cursor are computed in Ada; delete-marker rows are validated to carry no object metadata.
- Object Lock, legal hold and retention: the bucket must be versioning-enabled for Put, retention may only be extended in the same mode, and permanent deletion checks legal hold and unexpired retention inside the delete transaction.
- Bucket metadata state CAS (`Create_Bucket_Metadata_State`, `Replace_Bucket_Metadata_State`, `Delete_Bucket_Metadata_State`) compares the complete expected snapshot inside a transaction under the gate.
- Unix durability C shim: `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on a persistent inode with `O_CLOEXEC`, `F_FULLFSYNC` with a fallback to `fsync`, and errors reported through the return code and mapped to `Device_Error` in Ada.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: the vendored build defines `SQLITE_THREADSAFE`, `SQLITE_DQS`, `SQLITE_DEFAULT_MEMSTATUS`, `SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_API_ARMOR`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_COLUMN_METADATA`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_DBSTAT_VTAB`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_RTREE` and `SQLITE_USE_URI` and suppresses no CHECK constraint (`sqlite/flyology_object_storage_sqlite.gpr:19-36`); `Get_Object` is the only reader of `Find_Selected_Object` without a `Check` hook, across the three call sites at `backends-sqlite.adb:2448`, `:2581` and `:2666`; `Delete_Bucket` leaves no partial state, because it runs inside `Begin_Transaction` with `Safe_Rollback` on the handler.

### CLIENT-LOW

- Probe `tests/repros/src/repro_client_low_probe.adb`, exit status 0 (PASS): a single read-only program that records the current behaviour of the discovery report's uncounted observations without asserting a defect. It shows `Valid_Model_Scalar` accepting `"1_000"`, `"16#3E8#"`, `"1E3"` and `"+1000"` and carrying each into the wire target (escalated to CLIENT-LOW-7); an unversioned two-entry `DeleteObjects` through the in-repo server over the loopback listener returning `Objects_Deleted` with `<Deleted> Key=entry-a VersionId=""` and `Key=entry-b VersionId=""`; and the three HeadObject header demands — a 200 without `ETag` and a 200 without `Last-Modified` both rejected as `invalid HeadObject response headers`, a 200 carrying `Transfer-Encoding` rejected as `HeadObject response uses transfer coding`, and a control with both headers present accepted as `Object_Found`. Four exchanges served.
- Executor guard invariant: all 110 `Execute_*` bodies were enumerated; each performs the `Prepared.Operation` / `Prepared.Modeled_Operation` test as its first statement or delegates to one of the four guarded shared executors (`Execute_Bucket_Configuration_Deletion` `:8245`, `Execute_Bucket_Control_Get` `:9798`, `Execute_Bucket_Control_Mutation` `:10892`, `Execute_Object_Tagging` `:12452`), with `Execute_Create_Multipart_Upload` the single exception (CLIENT-LOW-6).
- Model projection (`Prepare_Model_Request_Internal` / `Prepare_Model_Request` / `Prepare_Model_Streaming_Request`, `low_level.adb:999-1381`): member lookup, duplicate and keyed-member rejection, `Content-Length` binding to the payload, required-member enforcement, URI-template expansion with greedy `{Key+}` preserving slashes and matching what `SigV4.Sign` canonicalises, fixed-query splitting, the virtual-hosted host-prefix check, payload-hash selection (explicit, `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` over HTTPS only, or computed) and the generated request checksum header. Header injection through user metadata or any header value is blocked by `Flyology.HTTP.Client.Add_Header` raising `Constraint_Error` (`flyology-http-client.ads:207-215`), mapped to `Invalid_Request`.
- Request assembly (`Prepare_Object_Request`, `Authority`, `Wire_Query`, `:1410-1553`): the host field is byte-identical to `flyology_http`'s `Host_Field` (`flyology-http-client.adb:1876-1890`), default port omitted and IPv6 bracketed; `=` is stripped only for empty values after canonicalisation, matching SigV4's `name=` canonical form, and both sides canonicalise identically.
- The three client generics (`bounded_rest_xml_reads.adb`, `paginated_rest_xml_reads.adb`, `rest_xml_mutations.adb`): the response limit accepts exactly `Maximum_Document_Bytes` and rejects one byte over; sink overflow becomes a typed `Response_Sink_Failed`; saved exceptions are re-raised at `Finish`; the prepared request is cleared only once the child is inactive; cancellation is forwarded to an active child; a restart is possible only through `Start` after `Finish`; one absolute deadline covers the child exchange; the in-memory source is one-shot with an exact declared length.
- `S3.Paginated_REST_XML_Reads` envelope decoding: root, container and item structure, namespace consistency, whitespace-only text and scalar close matching; duplicate `IsTruncated` and token members fall to `Set_Extra_Scalar`, which every instantiation (`metrics`, `analytics`, `inventory`, `intelligent_tiering`) implements as a rejection.
- Response-echo bindings for ListObjects v1 and v2, ListObjectVersions, ListParts, ListMultipartUploads, ListBuckets, CreateMultipartUpload (present-and-different form), CompleteMultipartUpload, the PutObject and UploadPart checksum echo and the DeleteObjects entry binding are consistent with the model's default echoes (`MaxKeys` default 1000 = `Page_Size'Last`; `PartNumberMarker` and `MaxParts` always sent explicitly), and the real-server corpus passes v1 and v2 listings with a prefix.
- 200-with-`<Error>` detection for CompleteMultipartUpload, CopyObject and UploadPartCopy (`:3824-3835`, `:14740-14750`, `:14389-14399`).
- Non-replayable sources for every conditional or non-idempotent mutation (PUT tagging, versioning, lock, legal hold, retention and bucket controls, CreateBucket, CompleteMultipartUpload, DeleteObject, DeleteObjects, AbortMultipartUpload, DeleteObjectAnnotation); PutObject and UploadPart stream the caller's source with a caller-supplied payload hash.
- Status-class handling: 3xx is never followed (`flyology_http` defaults to `No_Redirects`) and is reported through the rejection variant when an `<Error>` body is present; the whole-exchange timeout and the cancellation token are applied to both `Execute` and `Read_All`; `Response_Too_Large` is mapped to `Invalid_Response` at every `Read_All` site, with a bound of 1 for HEAD and for PutObject and UploadPart successes.
- Singleton-header rules, `Valid_Content_Range` (a 206 must cover `Content-Length` exactly), the `Valid_Read_Checksum_Headers` composite and full-object arithmetic, `Content_MD5` base64, `Valid_ISO_8601_Timestamp`, `Valid_Response_Entity_Tag`, and the list, enum and bounds branches of `Valid_Model_Scalar` were each checked against their specifications and match.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: the control legs of `repro_client_low_1`, `repro_client_low_2` and `repro_client_low_3` show that adding only `x-amz-version-id` to otherwise identical responses turns every failing leg green, so status, ETag, `Content-Length` and body decoding are not what fires; `repro_client_low_4`'s HEAD and well-formed-`<Error>` legs show both already-tolerant paths working; `repro_client_low_5`'s absent and explicit-true legs show the CreateSession serialiser is otherwise live; `repro_client_low_6`'s guarded-executor and genuine-DeleteObject legs prove the origin exchange counter is sound; `repro_client_low_7`'s `"500"` and `"not-a-number"` legs show the scalar validator is reached and does reject genuine garbage.

### CLIENT-OBJECTS

- Probe `tests/repros/src/repro_client_objects_probe.adb`, exit status 0 (PASS): a `Content-Length` and body-length disagreement is handled cleanly by both `Get_Whole` overloads. Served a `200 OK` declaring `content-length: 64` with 100 body octets, the read is framed at the declared 64 octets and the excess is discarded, which is correct HTTP; served the same header with 32 octets followed by a close, the owned-bytes overload raises `Flyology.HTTP.Protocol_Error` ("peer closed before Content-Length was received") and the bounded overload produces `Whole_Get_Exchange_Failed` with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` and a zero-length destination. No unhandled exception and no partial body left readable. One contract note recorded as a documentation gap rather than a finding: `Protocol_Error` is the documented framing exception of the pinned `flyology_http_0.1.3_eb09a80a` `Read_All` (`src/flyology-http-client.ads:1174`), while `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ads:3231-3233` names only `Response_Too_Large` and `Low_Level.Invalid_Response` for a larger or malformed successful body; propagating the transport and framing exception class is consistent with `docs/architecture/client-objects.md:66-68`. Whether an over-long body desynchronises a *reused* connection was not covered and belongs to `flyology_http`, since the loopback helper closes the peer after each exchange.
- Range binding (`Bind_Response_Range` `:2327-2377`, `Range_Header` `:2311-2325`, `Valid_Range_Request` `:2302-2307`, `Complete_Range_Child` `:2502-2587`): a server that ignores `Range` and returns `200` with the full body is refused, an oversized body first fails as `Response_Body_Too_Large`, suffix, open-ended and bounded requests are resolved against the `Content-Range` total with `Resolve_Range` and the returned interval must equal the resolution, the received byte count must equal `Content-Length` and the ETag must equal the `If-Match` sent; the low-level `Valid_Content_Range` (`client-low_level.adb:5933-5979`) has already required strict digits and `First <= Last < Total` before this layer runs.
- Destination ownership and clearing for whole, range and torrent reads: the pinned `flyology_http` `Finish (…, Destination)` contract (`flyology-http-client.ads:1018-1033`) restores length zero on every non-success, so a transport failure, timeout, cancellation or `Response_Body_Too_Large` mid-body cannot leave partial bytes readable, and the Objects layer additionally clears on every invalid or rejected decode (`:2413`, `:2433`, `:2444`, `:2536`, `:2555`, `:2565`, `:2580`, torrent `:5361-5504`).
- Non-replay of mutations: the eight mutating operations implement `Operation_Request_Body_Source` and none implements `Rewindable_Request_Body_Source`, and `flyology_http` replays a streamed body only when the source is rewindable (`flyology-http-client.ads:502-506`, `:558-562`); the synchronous put overloads reject a rewindable source up front (`:690-694`), and source cursors are reset only at `Start_*`.
- Conditional put buffer ownership (`Start_Put` `:1996-2045`, `Finish` `:2198-2220`, `Finalize` `:1984-1994`): validation and signing precede `Move_From`, a failed start moves the token back and rolls the operation back, `Finish` checks `Same_Pool` and moves the token back before re-raising a saved exception, matching `.ads:294-305`; `Read_Now` (`:1823-1849`) never reads past the detached buffer.
- Certainty normalizers for put, delete, delete-objects, tagging, legal hold, retention and annotation deletion: an admission other than `Response_Observed` is always `Outcome_Unknown`, only exact modeled status and code pairs are conclusive, and the put table matches `tests/corpora/composable-client/put-certainty.tsv` row for row.
- Composable state machines, all 18 operations: the child is released exactly once on every path, `Clear_Prepared_Request` is guarded by `not Is_Active (Item.Child)`, `Response_Limit_Exceeded` becomes `Response_Sink_Failed` with the pre-`Finish` admission, saved exceptions are re-raised only from `Finish` after `Consume`, and a restart requires the same `HTTP`, `Destination` and `Cancellation` owners.
- Synchronous `Get_Whole` (`Whole_Get_Outcome` overload, `:889-978`): requires `200`, a strong quoted ETag and a present `Content-Length`, rejects `Content-Length > Maximum` before reading as documented at `.ads:3232-3233`, reads at most `Maximum` bytes and requires the received length to equal `Content-Length`; the `Response` local is a `Limited_Controlled` lease, so the early raise paths release it.
- Entity-tag validation (`Valid_Exact_Entity_Tag` `:96-119`), `Timestamp` (`:83-94`), `Apply_Complete_Put_Options` (`:542-671`) and the HeadObject sink (`Write` `:2906-2918`, any body octet on a HEAD is refused as `Response_Sink_Failed`) were each checked against their specifications and match.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: the control legs of `repro_client_objects_1` and `repro_client_objects_2` show that adding only `x-amz-version-id: null` to otherwise identical responses turns every failing leg green, so status, ETag and `Content-Length` checks are not what fires; `repro_client_objects_3`'s `Get_ACL` leg shows the sibling normalizer classifying the identical `503`.

### CLIENT-BUCKETS

- Probe `tests/repros/src/repro_client_buckets_probe.adb`, exit status 0 (PASS): an observation-only program with no assertions, run against a MinIO container started by hand from the digest pinned in `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` (`quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9a34af32f524e538b8346cf79f3321eff8e708c1e2960462bd8936e`, `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`, container `flyology-review-buckets-460`, `--publish 127.0.0.1::9000`, health wait, stopped afterwards). It settles four external facts the discovery report held as unverified memory. First, the two credential rejections, taken with the crate's own `Flyology.Object_Storage.S3.SigV4.Sign` over a raw loopback socket for `GET /flyology-review-buckets?location`: an unknown access key gives `403 Forbidden` with `<Code>InvalidAccessKeyId</Code>` and a wrong secret gives `403 Forbidden` with `<Code>SignatureDoesNotMatch</Code>`; neither is 401, and a bodyless `HEAD /flyology-review-buckets` with the unknown key gives `403 Forbidden`, `Content-Length: 0` and the non-AWS header `X-Minio-Error-Code: InvalidAccessKeyId`, which the client does not read. Second, the same calls driven through the public convenience API against the live server reach `CORRUPT_OR_INVALID_RESPONSE` and `BUCKET_TAG_MUTATION_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, with a valid-credential control reaching `BUCKET_TAGS_REPLACED` and `NO_FAILURE`, so CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 is shown live and not only through scripted bytes. Third, MinIO does not redirect: a HeadBucket signed for `eu-west-1` against a bucket MinIO reports as `us-east-1` (empty `LocationConstraint`) answers `200 OK` with no `x-amz-bucket-region`, and `Head` then reports `Region = "eu-west-1"` from the documented signing-region fallback, which is why CLIENT-BUCKETS-2's AWS premise stays unverified and its repro must script the header. Fourth, `GET /?max-buckets=10&prefix=flyology` returns a `200 ListAllMyBucketsResult` with no `<Prefix>` element, and the live `List_Page` with that prefix raises while the no-prefix control succeeds, which is CLIENT-BUCKETS-3.
- Refutation attempts on CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 that failed: there is no status-normalisation step between the `Low_Level` decoders and the classifiers that could remap 403 to 401 (the classifiers read `Value.Status` straight out of `Decode_*_Response`, and the live run shows `Status = 403` with `Error.Code = "InvalidAccessKeyId"` reaching `Normalize_*` unchanged); the 401 pairing does not even match the in-repo server, which emits `403 SignatureDoesNotMatch` for both a bad key and a bad secret, so the client cannot classify its own server's credential rejection; no compatibility or qualification document states that only 401 is recognised; and a caller can still recover the true code from `Result.Response.Error.Code`, which is why the finding is P1 and not P0.
- Shared derivations: the `Failure_Reason` and disposition derivations are internally consistent once the missing 403 pairs are set aside, and the classification tables for the sampled reads and mutations match the pinned model's error shapes.
- `Get_Location`'s compatibility normalisation (`""` to `us-east-1`, `EU` to `eu-west-1`, everything else verbatim) matches the documented behaviour at `client-buckets.ads:10214-10216`.
- The PutBucketTagging preparation computes Content-MD5 and the requested checksum header as documented (`client-low_level.adb:200-245`, `Checksums.Compute` at `:227`).
- Exchange hygiene: every scripted exchange in the three repro programs was served exactly once (eleven in `repro_client_buckets_1`, four in each of `repro_client_buckets_2` and `repro_client_buckets_3`), so no operation leaked or replayed a request.
- Nothing tracked was modified during verification; `git status --short` shows only `review/`, `tests/repros/` and `sqlite/tests/repros/`.

### TRANSFERS

- Multipart planning arithmetic: `1 + (Size - 1) / Part_Size` is an exact ceiling with no addition overflow, non-final parts are always exactly `Part_Size`, the last part is never empty, `n x Part_Size` yields exactly `n` parts, and plans above 10,000 parts or the 50,000 GiB bound are rejected before any network I/O; `Byte_Count` and `File_Offset` accumulation are guarded and each part's range source is delimited correctly.
- Structured task lifetime: every `Transfer_Many` wave is a `Task_Scopes.Scope` whose `Join` closes admission and awaits all workers, `Finalize` cancels and joins when `Join` was skipped, `Execute_Transfer` catches every exception so a worker never terminates the scope early, and children receive the scope-owned token — no detached task outlives the call.
- In-flight bounds: worker count is min(objects, requests, `Maximum_In_Flight_Bytes / 64 KiB`) as documented, each worker holds at most one 64 KiB buffer owned by this package, and parts within one subject are sequential.
- Result aggregation: per-subject results are stored by input index, `Cancel_Remaining` marks later waves cancelled, and deadline expiry and pre-cancelled batches produce terminal results, all asserted by `tests/src/s3_transfer_many_corpus.adb`.
- Multipart assembly: ETags appended in part-number order, per-part checksums verified against the UploadPart echo, composite and full-object values computed as specified, the final echo and optional `ChecksumType` verified before success, no part ever replayed (`One_Shot_Range_Source` is non-rewindable), and `Abort_Best_Effort` run on every failure after initiation with its own budget.
- Downloads: single range only, `Content-Range` coherence enforced in `Low_Level`, the body byte count compared with `Content-Length`, temp-file plus `rename(2)` publication, the temp deleted on every failure path, and `Files.Close` resetting the descriptor before raising so no double close is possible.
- `S3.Multipart` codecs: `Parse_Query` rejects malformed escapes, oversize queries and duplicate or inconsistent selectors without unhandled exceptions, and the three XML handlers bound depth, part count and listed-part count and map `XML_Error` to `Malformed_Multipart`.
- MinIO probe `tests/repros/src/repro_transfers_minio_probe.adb`, exit status 0 (PASS), against the digest pinned by `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh` (`RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z`), started by hand so no repository test script ran: nothing the high-level client sends by default was rejected, so there is no TRANSFERS-2.
- That probe settles the `-N` suffix question for MinIO: the unsuffixed completion header `Upload_Multipart_File` sends is accepted for CRC32 and SHA256, MinIO echoes the `-N` form the client then requires, and a deliberately wrong bare digest is answered 400 `XAmzContentChecksumMismatch`, so the acceptance is real validation and not indifference.
- The probe also shows the client's `IsTruncated = (NextPartNumberMarker > 0)` strictness never bites on a real two-part upload (truncated, final and past-the-end pages all satisfy it), and that `max-parts` above 1000 is not expressible through the typed client at all.

### S3-DOMAIN-A

- ListObjectsV2 and the rest of `S3.Listings` outside S3-DOMAIN-A-2 and S3-DOMAIN-A-6 are sound: the v1 and v2 query parsers strict-percent decode with a literal `+`, accept exactly the modeled member sets and require `list-type=2`; the decoders are forward-compatible (unknown elements skipped), require `Key` and `Size` per object, validate `StorageClass`, `ChecksumAlgorithm` and `ChecksumType` against the model enumerations — including all fifteen `ObjectStorageClass` values, which is the sibling behaviour S3-DOMAIN-A-1 diverges from (`listings.adb:648-653`) — enforce `KeyCount = Contents + CommonPrefixes` and `NextContinuationToken` if and only if truncated, and bind continuation tokens to bucket, prefix and delimiter with SHA-256; the serializers escape every text and preserve presence.
- `S3.Versions` outside S3-DOMAIN-A-1 and S3-DOMAIN-A-7: the query parser accepts exactly the eight modeled members and rejects `version-id-marker` without `key-marker`; the decoder covers all 13 body members, interleaved `Version` and `DeleteMarker`, `Owner`, `RestoreStatus`, the `ChecksumAlgorithm` list against the model enumeration, `EncodingType=url` only, duplicate-identity rejection, `IsLatest` booleans and timestamps; the serializer is presence-consistent.
- `S3.Lifecycle`, `S3.Replication` and `S3.Notifications` decoders and encoders match the model member for member (all nine `Rule` members with the full `Filter`/`And` shape, flattened transitions, arbitrary-precision integers and ISO-8601 with fraction and offset; `Role` and a non-empty `Rule` list required with nested `Status` exactly where the model requires it and all fifteen storage classes; the three notification configuration kinds with all 30 `Event` values, both `FilterRuleName` values and `EventBridgeConfiguration` as a presence element), and each encoder emits model order, escapes every text, omits unset optionals and round-trips its own output. The only defect found in them is the one-byte range of S3-DOMAIN-A-3.
- `S3.Versioning` outside S3-DOMAIN-A-5: both members optional, both enumerations exact, and `Parse_Response` accepts unqualified roots as documented.
- `S3.ACL`, `S3.Bucket_Controls`, `S3.Encryption`, `S3.Website` and `S3.Logging` were read in full against the model and match: the ACL policy including `xsi:type` required exactly once in the XML-Schema-instance namespace and all three grantee types and five permissions; the five scalar bucket-control documents plus `OwnershipControls` and `CORSConfiguration` with required method and origin lists; the encryption rule with all five algorithms and both blocked-encryption types; the website redirect, index, error and routing rules with `http`/`https` only; the logging configuration with all three permissions, both date sources and the disabled state.
- `S3.Inventory`, `S3.Analytics`, `S3.Metrics` and `S3.Intelligent_Tiering` outside the page root of S3-DOMAIN-A-4: the single and paged decoders cover every modeled member, enforce the required members at close (including `Filter/Prefix`, `Format`, `Bucket`, the 16 `OptionalFields` values, `OutputSchemaVersion=V_1`, `Format=CSV` and the non-empty flattened `Tiering` list), and the serializers re-parse their own output. Intelligent-Tiering's root is the one case where the shape name really is the AWS root, and the S3-DOMAIN-A-4 program uses it as its passing control.
- `S3.Metadata_Configurations` and `S3.Metadata_Tables`: the result decoders, the create request and the three update request parsers restricted to the modeled `...Updates` members, `EncryptionConfiguration` with `SseAlgorithm` required, and all writers through `XML_Writers` in model order with presence invariants; both already hard-code the true wire roots (`metadata_configurations.adb:211`, `metadata_tables.adb:295`), which is the pattern S3-DOMAIN-A-4's four decoders depart from.
- The generated request encoders `Generated_Put_Bucket_{ACL,Inventory_Configuration,Logging,Website}_XML` take their roots from the payload `locationName`, write the S3 namespace on the root, emit `xsi:type` with its `xmlns:xsi` declaration (`xml_writers.adb:123-137`), pass every text through `Escape_Text`, omit unset optionals and always write required members; the generated `List_Directory_Buckets` decoder is correct apart from its root name, covering `Buckets/Bucket{Name,CreationDate,BucketRegion,BucketArn}` with the min/max and pattern constraints and rejecting unknown members and foreign namespaces.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: every program carries at least one passing control, and the controls are what isolate each cause — `STANDARD` decodes where the other four classes do not (A-1); the MinIO page with `delimiter=/` decodes where the byte-identical page without one does not (A-2); Replication accepts the same U+007F byte in the same position that Lifecycle and Notifications refuse, and Lifecycle accepts U+0080, so the boundary is "one-byte forms above 0x7E" rather than "control characters" (A-3); Intelligent-Tiering accepts its shape-name root (A-4); the namespaced `?versioning` PUT returns 200 from the same server that refuses the unqualified one, and the three lenient sibling parsers accept their unqualified bodies (A-5); all three listings accept `max-keys=1000` (A-6); MinIO's truncated envelope with both markers non-empty decodes (A-7).
- MinIO probe measurements recorded with no finding of their own: PutObject accepts `x-amz-storage-class: REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` with 200 and rejects `STANDARD_IA`, `GLACIER`, `ONEZONE_IA` and `INTELLIGENT_TIERING` with `InvalidStorageClass`, so `REDUCED_REDUNDANCY` is the reachable non-`STANDARD` version class there; `GET /bucket?inventory`, `?metrics`, `?analytics` and `?intelligent-tiering` all answer 501 `NotImplemented` with an `<Error>` root, which is why S3-DOMAIN-A-4 rests on the AWS API Reference rather than on a pinned server.

### S3-DOMAIN-B

- `Errors.Parse` and `Serialize` (`errors.adb:20-147`): root and depth checks, the `RequestId`/`RequestID` and `HostId`/`HostID` spellings, duplicate detection, unknown members ignored including the nested `Endpoint`, `Bucket` and `StringToSign*`, required non-empty `Code` and `Message`; HTML or empty bodies raise `Malformed_Error`, which every caller maps to `Invalid_Response`.
- `Tags.Valid_Bucket_Tag_Set` and `Valid_Tag_Text` (`flyology-object_storage.adb:822-955`): the UTF-8 decoder rejects overlongs, surrogates and code points above U+10FFFF, counts scalars rather than bytes, enforces 1..128 and 0..256, the `aws:` prefix rule and case-sensitive uniqueness, and its L/N/Z plus `_.:/=+-@` repertoire matches AWS's rule.
- `Object_Lock`, all six subprograms: the consistent-namespace rule, exact `ON`/`OFF`, `GOVERNANCE`/`COMPLIANCE` and `Enabled` values, strict but AWS-compatible ISO-8601 (`Z` or `±hh:mm`, up to nine fraction digits), arbitrary-precision `Days`/`Years` text, presence-preserving serializers with correct depth, element and text budget checks, and an empty payload for an absent outer member. `Days` versus `Years` exclusivity is deliberately not a codec rule (`object_lock.ads:118-121`) and the server refuses any `Rule` with `NotImplemented` (`s3_applications.adb:10556-10560`).
- `Deletions.Parse_Delete_Object_Query`, `Valid_Version_ID`, `Parse_Result` and `Serialize_Result`: quiet mode suppresses only `<Deleted>` entries (`s3_applications.adb:17884-17897`) and errors are always reported, interleaved `<Deleted>`/`<Error>` and unknown members are handled, `DeleteMarker` and `DeleteMarkerVersionId` round-trip, the 1000-entry and 1001st-entry bounds hold on both sides, keys are XML-escaped on output and decoded on input, and an empty `<Delete/>` is rejected as AWS does.
- `Object_Reads.Parse_Query` and `Parse_Conditional_Date`: strict percent decoding with a literal `+`, bounded and unique members, CR/LF/NUL rejection in response overrides, `partNumber` in 1..10000, all three RFC 9110 date forms with the fifty-year rule, and the exact `x-id` values.
- `Tagging.Parse_Query`, `Parse_Header`, `Serialize_Header`, `Serialize`, `Serialize_Bucket` and the object `Parse` structure checks: the ten-tag bound, key and value size bounds, percent-encoding with the RFC 3986 unreserved set, duplicate-key rejection, and an empty `<TagSet/>` accepted for objects.
- `Attributes.Parse_Selection`, `Image`, `Parse_Query` and the SAX handler for `Checksum`, `ObjectParts` and `Part`: exact enum values with OWS, unique selection, the `PartsCount` location name, 64-bit `Size` and `ObjectSize`, marker and count bounds, strictly increasing part numbers, composite count consistency and single-algorithm parts.
- `Multipart_Uploads.Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads_Query` and the SAX handler: the required `uploads` marker, the `url` encoding type, flattened `Upload` entries, `CommonPrefixes/Prefix`, `Owner` and `Initiator`, the model-driven `StorageClass`, and the `ChecksumAlgorithm`, `ChecksumType` and `EncodingType` enumeration checks, with duplicate-entry and grouped-key consistency.
- `Annotations` entry handler, `Encode_Continuation` and `Decode_Continuation` (a scope-bound SHA-256 token whose length is pre-checked by `Core.Valid_Listing_Continuation_Syntax`), `Parse_List` through the shared paginated envelope, and `Serialize_List` member names and order against the model (the `AnnotationEntry` container, flattened `ChecksumAlgorithm`, required `AnnotationName`, `LastModified` and `Size`, the five `ReplicationStatus` values, `MaxAnnotationResults` in 1..1000).
- `Copies` SAX handler and `Validate_Checksum`: all 13 members, duplicate rejection, unknown members ignored, `CRC64NVME` never composite, the composite `-N` suffix in 1..10000, and exact Base64 digest lengths.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: the isolating replay table, in which adding only `xmlns` to `mc`'s and s3cmd's exact bodies turns `400 MalformedXML` into `200` on both the DeleteObjects and the PutBucketTagging route, so neither SERVER-1 nor the UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD rule is what fires; the control legs of programs 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 8 and 9, each of which passes and isolates the single property under test; and the last block of program 7, in which the crate's own server answers a single-PUT `GetObjectAttributes` without `ObjectParts` and its own client reads that answer.
- MinIO oracle observations recorded with no finding of their own: MinIO accepts and echoes `max-uploads=0` and `max-uploads=5000` (`<MaxUploads>0</MaxUploads>`, `<MaxUploads>5000</MaxUploads>`, both `200`); MinIO emits *empty* `<NextKeyMarker></NextKeyMarker>` elements on a non-truncated page, which decode to zero-length strings and pass `Validate`; and MinIO answered `max-uploads=1` with three `<Upload>` entries alongside `<MaxUploads>1</MaxUploads><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>`, which `Validate`'s first branch (`Uploads.Length > Max_Uploads`) correctly rejects.

### SERVER-EXE

- Two questions the discovery's earlier pass left open were resolved in the same discovery report and neither is a finding. Shutdown classification: `Static.Request_Stop` (`static.adb:823-851`) moves the phase to `Stopping_Children` at once and fixes the terminal outcome at `Shutdown_Completed`, and every later generation termination, whatever its `Kind`, only advances the stop order (`static.adb:741-742`), so a SIGTERM during startup — including the window where `Mark_Ready` would raise `Program_Error` "after stopping begins" (`supervision.ads:304-309`) — and a deferred `Server_Failed` raised by `Structured_Servers.Serve` at stop (`structured_servers.adb:536`) both end with exit status 0. Handler exceptions during shutdown: resolved by the same classification, and no reachable handler exception escapes to `Structured_Servers` in the first place.
- Credential bootstrap in C (`..._credentials_native.c`): CSPRNG source (`arc4random_buf`/`getrandom` with EINTR retry), 24 random bytes to 48 hex characters (192 bits), 32-byte random salt; the temp file is created with `O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0600` before any write, fully written and `fsync`ed, published by `link()` that never replaces an existing name (EEXIST makes the caller reload the racing process's file), with a parent-directory `fsync` and a distinct status when only cleanup failed so the password is still revealed.
- `Secure_File`: `lstat` rejects symlinks and directories as non-regular, the effective uid must own the file and the mode must be exactly `0600`; the TOCTOU window between the check and `Ada.Text_IO.Open` needs directory write access by an attacker who could equally pre-create the file, which then fails the uid check.
- Credential file `Load`: fixed 160-character single line, `File_Prefix` pinning version, username and iteration count, lowercase-hex-only decoding, more than one line rejected; GNAT 15.3.1 `Index` (Forward) with `From` past the end returns 0 through a null slice and raises `Index_Error` only for `From < Source'First` (`a-strsea.adb:610-621`), so short lines hit the length check.
- PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 `Derive` (single block, `INT(1)` big-endian, XOR chain) and its iteration-1 self-test vector `120fb6cf…0be17b`, confirmed with `python3` to equal `hashlib.pbkdf2_hmac('sha256', b'password', b'salt', 1, 32)`; constant-time comparisons for the verifier and session tokens; work factor 600,000 matching the OWASP figure the README cites. The work factor itself is correct and is not what SERVER-EXE-1 is about.
- Plaintext lifetime: `Password`, `Password_Bytes`, `Salt`, `Derived`, `U`, `Next`, `Candidate`, the HMAC context and the session `Token` are wiped on normal and exceptional paths; the password is printed exactly once, only when `Generated`, only to standard error (`server.adb:31-42`), and no other `Put_Line` in `server/src`, `src/flyology-object_storage-server*.adb` or the HTTP server library prints request material.
- Sessions and cookies: 32 random bytes to 64 hex (256 bits), 12-hour absolute expiry, slot reuse only of unoccupied or expired entries with oldest-expiry eviction when all 64 are live, `Revoke` wipes; `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=43200` with no `Secure` (documented, loopback HTTP only) and `Max-Age=0` on logout; a single `Cookie` header is required and duplicate `flyology_admin` pairs fail closed.
- CSRF and rebinding: `Local_Request` compares the parsed request authority with the exact bound loopback authority; mutations require an exact `Origin`; login and logout accept an absent `Origin` for non-browser clients but reject a mismatching one; every `/api/*` route checks `Local_Request` and `Authenticated` (or `Same_Origin`/`Strict_Same_Origin`) before touching the backend.
- Asset serving: three fixed routes with no path parameter, contents read once at startup and SHA-256 pinned against compiled digests, the three pinned digests confirmed byte-for-byte equal to the SHA-256 of the shipped files; `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_ASSET_ROOT` changes only where assets are read from, not what is served.
- Response and error surface: `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, a CSP of `default-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; form-action 'self'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin` on every handler response; JSON bodies escape `"` and `\` and map control characters, object keys are emitted URI-encoded, and `app.js` uses `textContent` only; error responses are fixed JSON tokens and a fixed 421 text, with no paths or exception text reaching the client.
- Bounded bodies and ingress budget: login 256 bytes, create and delete 68 bytes, GET routes reject bodies, a 4 KiB shared ingress budget, 30-second request and 5-second header timeouts on the management listener.
- Configuration validation other than the storage root: the backend name is lower-cased and closed, the bind address must be numeric IPv4, ports go through `Port'Value` in 0 .. 65535, capacity is 1 .. 4096, AWS credentials are required as a complete non-empty pair with the session token only alongside them, and the region is length- and scope-segment-validated. The management listener is hard-bound to `Loopback_IPv4` (`runtime.adb:920-924`) and no option can move it.
- Signals and lifecycle: the SIGTERM handler is a protected `Attach_Handler` that only latches a flag, a stop latched before `Supervisors.Run` is sticky, the supervisor stops the dependent management child first, each child stops accepting and drains (2 s and 10 s) before forced cancellation, the 12 s grace exceeds the drain, `Shutdown_Completed` yields exit 0 and any other outcome or exception sets a failure exit status, the files and SQLite `Store` is a block-local object finalised after `Run` returns on every path, and the SQLite root lock is an OS lock released on process death. SIGPIPE is suppressed at the socket layer.
