- Restart behaviour: `On_Failure` with bounded default recovery limits (3 per 5 s, 10 per incident, 60 s), so a port already in use ends in `Startup_Failed` or `Recovery_Exhausted` rather than an infinite loop; the missing cause text is SERVER-EXE-4. `Structured_Servers.Serve` always closes the transferred listener.
- Handler exception containment: `Routing.Serve` answers any escaping management-handler exception with a plain 500 and keeps the connection loop; `Connection_Handlers.Serve` absorbs transport, timeout, payload and protocol exceptions around both the read and the `Handle` call; the S3 application's `Handle` ends in a `when others` that answers 500 `InternalError` or marks a started response failed. Only `Operation_Cancelled` reaches `Structured_Servers`, where it is counted as cancelled rather than failed, so `Server_Failed` is not reachable from S3 or management traffic.
- Management continuation tokens: `Core.Valid_Listing_Continuation_Syntax` (`core.adb:8-24`) enforces a 70-character minimum, an even remainder and a maximum before `Decode_Continuation` indexes `Raw (Digest_Last + 1)`, and `Listings.Decode_Continuation` (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-listings.adb:329-376`) returns `Valid => False` for malformed input and contains no `raise`, so the `token` query parameter cannot make `Object_Inventory` raise.
- Credential path versus store layout: buckets live under `<root>/buckets/` and staging under `<root>/tmp/` (files) or `<root>/objects/`, `<root>/staging/`, `catalog.sqlite3` and `.flyology-object-storage.lock` (SQLite), so the default `<root>/admin.credentials` is neither enumerated as a bucket nor swept at `Open`, including on the SERVER-EXE-3 path.
- Verifier measurements with no finding: the plain TCP connect alongside every stalled S3 request in `repro_server_exe_1.sh` completes in 0.001 s, showing the outage is invisible to a socket-only health check; the `FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE=8` run shows the coupling surviving a larger pool; the `notes/readme.txt` control in both SERVER-EXE-3 programs shows the sweep confined to the store's own directories; and the SQLite control in `repro_server_exe_5.sh` shows the enforcement that the files backend lacks.
- Process hygiene: nothing tracked was modified during verification (`git status --short` shows only the untracked `review/`, `tests/repros/` and `sqlite/tests/repros/` trees), and every server process started was stopped (`pgrep -fl flyology_object_storage_server` returns nothing).

### MODEL

- The differential reads `review/baseline/service-2.json` with an `object_pairs_hook` that reports duplicate object keys (none exist) and preserves member insertion order, which is the order the descriptor's ordinals and every consumer literal depend on; it reads `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-model.adb` with a line-exact parser for the two emitted shapes (`when KEY => return VALUE;` and the nested `case Member/Index is`), decoding Ada string literals including `""` escapes, so a layout change cannot be silently mis-read as agreement; and it reads `-s3-model.ads` for `Model_SHA256`, `Operation_Count`, `Shape_Count`, the four enumeration types and the declaration text of every visible function.
- Per operation it compares `Operation_Name`, `Method`, `Request_URI`, `Response_Code` (with the 200 default re-derived rather than copied), `Input_Shape`, `Output_Shape`, `Error_Count`, the whole `Error_Shape` list, `Unsigned_Payload`, `Authentication_Type`, `Request_Checksum_Required`, `Request_Checksum_Algorithm_Member`, `Request_Validation_Mode_Member` and `Response_Checksum_Algorithms` (including that no algorithm name contains the `;` join separator, which would make the image ambiguous); independently of the descriptor it checks that every `{label}` in a `requestUri` corresponds to exactly one `uri` member whose `locationName` is that label, that no label repeats, and that every `httpChecksum` member name is an actual member of the input shape.
- Per shape it compares `Shape_Name`, `Kind`, `Location_Name`, `Payload_Member`, `Timestamp_Format`, `Pattern`, `Minimum`, `Maximum`, `Is_Flattened`, `Is_Sensitive`, `XML_Namespace`, `List_Member_Shape`, `Map_Key_Shape`, `Map_Value_Shape`, `Enumeration_Count`, the whole `Enumeration_Value` list and `Member_Count`, plus the model-side invariants that a `payload` names a member with no `location`, that `required` names existing members, that a list has a `member` and a map has `key` and `value`, that no enum list contains a duplicate, and that members appear only on structures.
- Per member it compares `Member_Name`, `Member_Shape`, `Location` (with the `body` default re-derived), `Member_Location_Name` (with the REST-XML "member name when absent" default re-derived), `Member_Required`, `Member_Flattened`, `Member_Streaming`, `Member_XML_Attribute`, `Member_XML_Namespace` and `Member_Context_Parameter`.
- Structurally it requires every flat function to have exactly one arm per operation or per shape 1..718 with no extras, every indexed function to have no arm outside its domain, the emitted function set to equal the declared set, every emitted string to be printable ASCII, and every indexed accessor to carry a `Pre` bounding the index — which is what makes the `when others` defaults unreachable under the declared contract, notably `Member_Shape => Shape_Index'First`, which would otherwise silently answer shape #1; the twelve indexed accessors were checked against their matching count accessors at `.ads:397-400`, `:499-501` and `:511-576`, and SPARK proves the callers. Any JSON attribute the checker does not classify is reported; the only one is `synthetic`, new to this run, on `AccessDenied`.
- Consumer ordinal cross-check: 74 literal member ordinals were resolved in 6 consumer files by evaluating each call site's Ada shape expression against the JSON (`Input_Shape`/`Output_Shape` of a named operation, `Member_Shape` with a literal ordinal, `List_Member_Shape`, `Map_*_Shape`, `Shape_Index` conversions, and local helper functions taking a `Positive` member index — including the three same-named nested `Valid_Enumeration` helpers in `server-s3_applications.adb`, disambiguated by nearest preceding declaration). All 74 rows are `OK`: the call-site name agrees with the member or wire name in every case, and each of the 57 ordinals that reaches an `Enumeration_Count` or `Enumeration_Value` lookup lands on a member whose shape does carry enumeration values — a load-bearing invariant, since an off-by-one onto a non-enum member would make the validator reject every non-empty value. The resolved set is a superset of the discovery's list: `PutObjectOutput` 14 (`low_level.adb:7661`) and `UploadPartOutput` 1 (`:13982`) were not enumerated there and are correct, and the discovery's `CreateMultipartUploadOutput` list (12, 14) is incomplete — 6 and 13 are also passed and are also correct.
- Descriptor omissions were accounted for exhaustively rather than spot-checked, as a summary with the consumer each would need: `operation.endpoint` (`hostPrefix`) 1, `member.hostLabel` 1, `shape.error.httpStatusCode` 38 links over 20 shapes, `shape.error`/`shape.exception` 20/20, list-item `locationName` 9, `shape.xmlNamespace.prefix` 1, `operation.input.locationName`/`.xmlNamespace` 1/1, `operation.staticContextParams` 77, `operation.deprecated`/`member.deprecated` 4/5, `shape.box`/`member.box` 43/1, `shape.union` 1, `shape.event`/`eventstream`/`member.eventpayload` 5/1/3, `member.idempotencyToken` 1, `shape.synthetic` 1. Map key and value `locationName`, member `xmlName`, `queryName`, `requiresLength`, `eventheader`, `jsonvalue` and per-member `timestampFormat` do not occur in this model at all. None of the omissions has a `src/` consumer at this commit, so none can propagate a wrong value today; each becomes a gap only if the named consumer is written against the descriptor rather than the JSON.
- The hand-typed element names in the five committed generated serializers were checked by extracting every string literal and matching it against the reachable shape closure of that operation's input and output in the pinned JSON — element names, member names, `locationName`s and enumeration values. `PutBucketAcl`, `PutBucketLogging`, `PutBucketWebsite` and `PutBucketInventoryConfiguration` have zero unmatched literals; `ListDirectoryBuckets` has only the ARN prefixes `arn:`, `s3:` and `s3express:`, which are validation text rather than element names. This refutes "a typo in an element name would not change the closure digest" for the sources as committed; it does not prove the converse, that a *missing* element would be detected, which remains gated by the signed-socket bodies and the server corpora.
- Registry and ledger consistency was re-derived independently of `tools/s3_operation.py`: `coverage/aws-s3-operations.tsv` has 117 lines whose 116 operation names are exactly `sorted(model["operations"])` in `LC_ALL=C` order, `coverage/s3-operation-counts.json` reports `operations: 116`, and `coverage/s3-operations.toml:4-5` pins botocore revision `36c34f15391da01cd717c73c0fffa747c9889768` with the same SHA-256 as the generator, the `.ads` and `tools/verify-botocore-model.sh:12`. No drift. Stated limit of this check: it establishes the internal consistency of the pin, not the pinned file's fidelity to upstream botocore at that revision. No network fetch was made, so "the pinned JSON is the right JSON" rests on the CI job that fetches commit `36c34f15` and re-runs `verify-botocore-model.sh`, not on this verification.
- The discovery independently regenerated the whole spec and body from `review/baseline/service-2.json` into the scratch directory and found them byte-identical to the committed files, with `generate --check --output-dir src` exiting 0; it also confirmed that `--check` compares the full regenerated text of both files rather than a subset, that a changed JSON cannot regenerate silently (the digest and the 116/718 counts are checked in the generator, the registry loader, the shell verifier and the registry TOML), and that a duplicated, renamed or missing operation is caught at three independent layers (`load_registry`, `verify_registry_model_inventory` and `verify-coverage.sh`, with `test-coverage-verifier.sh` as a negative oracle proving a ledger-only promotion is rejected).

### BASELINE

- Every maintained gate was run before review work started and passes: root build (`alr -n build`, 55 s, no warnings), root test suite with `FLYOLOGY_S3_SERVICE_MODEL` set as CI does (8 min 16 s), SQLite tests (1 min 43 s), the GNATprove gate (`tools/prove.sh`, nine forced units, level 0, warnings as errors, 999 of 999 checks proved), the repository integrity check (4 min 19 s) and the botocore model verifier (hash, 116 operations and generated schema match).
- The Flyology backends pass their black-box lane: `tests/scripts/test-flyology-server.sh memory`, `files` and `sqlite` all pass (47 s, 48 s, 57 s) over the full corpus, the s5cmd byte and deletion oracles and restart routing.
- The three third-party servers fail identically and before any oracle-mode branching, at the corpus's third HeadObject case, with `INVALID_RESPONSE : HeadObject response does not match prepared request`; the cause is the client-side predicate filed as CLIENT-LOW-1, not a gate defect.
- Logs for every gate above are retained under `review/baseline/`; the two CI job logs cited by BASELINE-2 are `ci-blackbox-job.log` and `ci-rustfs-job.log`.

## Appendix B: observations not counted

### WIRE

- `max-keys` above 1000 is rejected with 400 where AWS is believed to clamp to 1000, and `max-uploads=0` is rejected while `max-keys=0` is accepted (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-multipart_uploads.adb:125-142`); both are policy values inside a documented range.
- `Listing_Follows_Cursor` applies the exclusive cursor to the projected key, so a user-supplied marker strictly inside a collapsed group omits the CommonPrefixes entry; documented at `src/flyology-object_storage.ads:366-368`, and AWS behaviour is unverified.
- `Valid_Bucket_Name` rejects the suffix `-an` (`src/flyology-object_storage.adb:452`) with no authority recorded near the declaration, which `.claude/rules/decision-authority.md` asks for; it only over-restricts bucket names.
- `IMF_Dates` is canonical-only by design, so `Expires` values in RFC 850 or asctime form and the cache-busting `0` and `-1` are rejected at `s3_applications.adb:4794`; current AWS handling is unverified.
- `XML.Escape` allocates a stack buffer of six times the value length (`xml.adb:218`); server-reachable inputs are bounded to roughly 96 KiB per call.
- `XML.Parse` funnels every non-`XML_Error` exception raised inside a receiver into `XML_Error` (`xml.adb:203-207`) and `Strict_XML_Codecs.Parse` relabels it `Malformed_Document`, so a genuine `Program_Error` or `Storage_Error` is reported as 400 MalformedXML; diagnostics only.
- XMLAda honours in-document `encoding=` declarations and transcodes to UTF-8, so a body declaring ISO-8859-1 with UTF-8 key bytes yields a mojibake key; standards-conformant, and the sender chose the declaration.
- `Read_Entity_Tag_List` treats `""` as matching an empty stored tag (`src/flyology-object_storage.adb:142-146`); unreachable because stored tags are nonempty.
- `Parse_Target` percent-decodes the bucket segment, so `/%62ucket/` resolves; harmless, and AWS behaviour is unverified.
- `HTTP_Last_Modified` (`s3_applications.adb:1836-1858`) goes through `Duration` and `Ada.Calendar`, so it is bounded by year 2262; `Modified` comes from the backend clock, not from a peer.
- Virtual-hosted addressing is outside the documented path-style claim (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:473`), so a client configured for virtual-host style gets NoSuchBucket or 400 rather than a diagnostic naming the cause.
- The remaining bespoke query parsers for `tagging`, `annotations`, `attributes` and `object_reads.Parse_Query` were only sampled through the shared decode pattern; no live S3, MinIO or RustFS behaviour was exercised in this area.
- Harness caveat for later work, not a defect: `Repro_S3_Server.Signed_Request` signs the same path string it puts on the wire, while the server percent-decodes the wire path before signing (`src/flyology-object_storage-s3-sigv4_verification.adb:309-310,420-423`), so a wire path carrying a percent escape needs the local `Signed_Wire` helper that `repro_wire_1`, `repro_wire_1b` and `repro_wire_4` each carry.

### SIGV4

- `aws-chunked` and the `STREAMING-*` payload hashes are rejected at admission with 400 and, for `Content-Encoding: aws-chunked` with a plain hash, NotImplemented; this is a documented boundary (`docs/compatibility/s3.md:452-460`, `docs/qualification/put-object.md:9-11,166`), but botocore 1.36 and later default to `STREAMING-UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD-TRAILER` over HTTPS (from memory, unverified), so an HTTPS deployment would see 400s from those clients while loopback conformance passes.
- Presigned URLs are not implemented on either side and `docs/compatibility/s3.md:78` records them as planned and not claimed; a presigned request arrives without `Authorization` and gets 403 AccessDenied.
- The credential scope service is hard-coded to `s3` (`sigv4_verification.adb:335`); if AWS SDKs sign `WriteGetObjectResponse` with `s3-object-lambda` (from memory, unverified), a stock SDK cannot call that operation, while the crate's own client signs it with `s3` so the pair is self-consistent.
- `x-amz-s3session-token` must be signed but is neither bound to a credential nor rejected, so a long-term key plus an arbitrary session token is accepted under `Static_Credentials`; whether `Create_Session` issues credentials that would need binding is a SERVER-area question.
- The access-key character set is `[A-Za-z0-9]` in `Sign`, `Parse` and `Create`, so MinIO and Ceph RGW identities using `-` or `_` can be neither used by this client nor configured as static server identities; policy value.
- Only `x-amz-date` is accepted, with no `Date` header fallback, and `x-amz-content-sha256` must be present and signed; documented boundary at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:452-457`.
- Timestamps with year 0001 to 1900 pass `Valid_Timestamp` and fail `Year_Number` inside `Timestamp_Time`, yielding 400 AuthorizationHeaderMalformed rather than 403 RequestTimeTooSkewed; same family as SIGV4-1 but caught, so diagnostics only.
- Canonical-URI normalization is not injective on the raw target, so `/b/a%2Fc` and `/b/a/c` share a signature; identical to MinIO and harmless as long as the router decodes the target the same way.
- Best-effort erasure gaps consistent with `README.md:407-412`: the secondary-stack copy returned by `Static_Credentials.Secret_Key`, the return copies of `SigV4.HMAC`, and GNAT.SHA256's internal context copy are not wiped.
- `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` requires the listener's own scheme to be HTTPS, so behind a TLS-terminating proxy the SDK default is refused with 400 InvalidRequest; policy.
- `Static_Credentials.Authenticate` compares the access key with ordinary `=` before the constant-time checks (`static_credentials.adb:81`), so timing reveals whether an access key exists; AWS distinguishes this case explicitly with `InvalidAccessKeyId`, so it is not counted.
- Bounds that no stock client approaches: more than 128 query parameters returns False and 403; more than 256 signed physical header occurrences or more than 128 signed names returns 400.
- Diagnostic drift on input no stock client produces: a declared but absent signed header yields 400 AuthorizationHeaderMalformed where AWS answers 403 SignatureDoesNotMatch, and a trailing `&` or empty query segment yields 403 SignatureDoesNotMatch.
- Query sort order is by encoded name, which is botocore's behaviour, while aws-sdk-go sorts raw keys before encoding, so the two SDK families disagree for keys mixing unreserved and reserved bytes in the 0x2D to 0x2F range; ecosystem ambiguity, not a defect of this crate.
- `tools/s3-signed-socket.py` checks the request line, `Host`, header presence and `SignedHeaders` membership only and never recomputes a signature, so the signed-socket qualification does not itself evidence signature correctness; the AUnit vectors do.

### SERVER

- `Apps.Request_Scheme` is always `Plain_HTTP` because neither `server/src/flyology_object_storage_server_runtime.adb:168-170` nor `tests/src/s3_server_harness.adb:89-91` passes a `Scheme`, so `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` is always answered 400, and the WriteGetObjectResponse admission at `:6733` and the SSE gate at `:5371` can never be satisfied by this repository's own server; consistent with a crate that ships no TLS listener, but worth writing down.
- SigV4 presigned (query-string) authentication is not implemented and such requests get 403 `AccessDenied`; documented as planned and not claimed at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:78`.
- `OPTIONS` and `PATCH` on any target are 501 rather than 405 or 403, and a target the parser rejects is 400 `InvalidURI` where AWS answers 403; a deliberate deviation from AWS's uniform 403 that discloses only route recognition.
- HeadObject with a satisfiable `Range` answers 200 with the range's `Content-Length` and no `Content-Range`, which matches the pinned model's HeadObject `Range` documentation.
- CopyObject with the default COPY metadata directive plus any replacement metadata header answers 400 `InvalidRequest` (`:14396-14411`), and CopyObject onto itself with COPY is accepted; both AWS comparisons are from memory and unverified.
- CreateMultipartUpload rejects user metadata, cache and content headers, storage class, tagging and object-lock headers with 501, so `aws s3 cp --metadata ...` above the multipart threshold fails; documented capability rejection at `docs/compatibility/s3.md:71`.
- CreateBucket on an existing bucket returns 409 `BucketAlreadyOwnedByYou`, where AWS us-east-1 returns 200 for the owner and MinIO returns 409.
- GetObjectAttributes never emits `StorageClass` even when requested (`:16305-16330`); omitted rather than fabricated, per the documented profile.
- PutObjectTagging answers 501 `NotImplemented` to any SDK checksum (`:15889`); folded into SERVER-1 rather than counted separately, because it is the same client-facing failure with a different status code.
- The 400 for PutBucketVersioning came back with `Connection: close` while the otherwise identical 400 for DeleteObjects came back with `Connection: keep-alive`, over keep-alive connections neither of which asked for a close; botocore reconnected transparently, so this is an inconsistency rather than a defect.
- Per-request budget is `Timeout => 60.0` in both the harness and the runtime, so a 5 GiB PutObject must complete within 60 s; a policy value.
- `Read_Document` appends byte-by-byte into an `Unbounded_String`, bounded by each route's `Maximum` (at most 2 MiB); performance rather than correctness.
- `flyology_http` answers 204 when a handler returns without responding; no arm was found that returns silently, but the fallback would mask such a regression as a success.
- PutObject `If-Match` against a missing key yields 412 where the model documents 404 `NoSuchKey` for the DeleteObject case (and, from memory and unverified, the same for PutObject); the evaluator lives in the core package, outside this area.

### CHECKSUM

- The server requires the raw Base64 (no `-N`) in `x-amz-checksum-<alg>` on CompleteMultipartUpload and the typed client strips the suffix; botocore never sends the header at all, so the requirement remains unverified against a client that does (aws-cli/CRT).
- XXHASH128 byte order follows xxHash's canonical big-endian (high64, low64) form; no AWS-published vector exists in the repository and whether AWS uses the same order is from memory and unverified.
- The C bridge checks `_Alignof <= 64` but not that the actual storage address is 64-byte aligned, so correctness relies on GNAT honouring `Alignment => 64` for the stack-allocated `XXH_Storage`; low confidence, no evidence of a failure.
- UploadPart echoes the backend-computed `x-amz-checksum-<alg>` even when the client sent no part checksum (`s3_applications.adb:12717-12721`), where the model says the header is present only when provided; confirmed harmless with the stock client.
- Trailer-borne checksums are reachable only with plain HTTP/1.1 chunked trailers because `aws-chunked` is answered 501 (PutObject `:14882-14886`, UploadPart `:12618-12622`); a documented capability boundary, narrowed by the probe to non-seekable or non-plain-HTTP bodies. See SERVER-5 for the diagnostic on the modern sentinel.
- Ranged GetObject on a full-object-CRC object returns the full-object checksum header with the 206 while composite values are suppressed on ranges; AWS behaviour for ranged responses is from memory and unverified, and stock SDKs skip validation on 206.
- `Request_Source.Read` rejects any request whose trailer block contains more than the one declared checksum trailer; a strictness choice, not a rule in the model.
- CompleteMultipartUpload accepts and validates an `x-amz-checksum-algorithm` request header the pinned model does not define for that operation (`s3_applications.adb:13048-13060`); a lenient extension.
- Policy values not counted: the CRC64NVME/FULL_OBJECT default for PutObject and CreateMultipartUpload without a selection, the 5 GiB PutObject and maximum part-size caps, the 10,000-entry caps in `Hash_File`, and `Maximum_Checksum_Length = 96`.
- Not reviewed: the HTTP crate's own chunked-trailer parser (outside this repository; only the accessor contract and the `Finished`/`Body_Done` coupling were read), and SQLite schema handling beyond the `Multipart_Object_Value` call site and `Checksum_From_Columns`.

### BACKEND-MEMORY

- Documented policy: an overwrite, multipart completion or CopyObject needs coexistence headroom and every nonempty GET or copy reserves a full body snapshot, so reads can return `Capacity_Exceeded` near the byte cap (`docs/architecture/memory-backend.md`, asserted at `tests/src/object_storage_test_cases.adb:5230-5240`).
- Documented policy: unknown-length sources are charged allocator capacity through `Growth_Capacity` rather than logical size, and `Bytes_Used` reflects it.
- An unknown, stale or prefix-excluded paired `ListObjectVersions` cursor is `Invalid_Request` (`memory.adb:2675-2690`), pinned at `tests/src/versioned_object_conformance.adb:776` and named in `docs/qualification/list-object-versions.md:167`; measured, a client whose own cursor generation is permanently deleted between pages gets 400 and must restart the listing, while MinIO returns the remaining versions — but MinIO is a weak oracle here, because its `NextKeyMarker` is an opaque internal token rather than an AWS-shaped key marker, so settling this needs a real AWS account.
- A missing bucket is `Not_Found` from `Head`, `Attributes`, `List`, `List_Versions`, `List_Uploads` and `Start_Multipart` but `Bucket_Not_Found` from `Fetch`, tags, annotations and the delete paths; the contract requires the distinction only for `Delete_Object_Tags` (`backends.ads:2620-2622`) and the routes that care call `Head_Bucket` first, so it is contract drift with no reachable wrong answer today.
- In-place replacement does not reset a reused slot's `Legal_Hold` and `Retention` while a freed slot is fully reset; unreachable, because in-place replacement happens only under unconfigured or suspended versioning while a hold requires enabled versioning — worth a defensive reset if Object Lock is ever permitted under suspension.
- `Adjust` sets `Capacity := Length` before allocating, so a `Storage_Error` there could make the caller's `Release_Buffer` release a second time, either tripping the "memory reservation underflow" guard or silently borrowing a concurrent operation's accounting; re-derived precisely and shown unreachable by the probe above, since provoking it needs a real allocation failure at one specific instruction.
- `Include_Part_Digest` raises `Constraint_Error` for a part ETag that is not 32 hex characters (`memory.adb:219-241`); unreachable, because part ETags are always `GNAT.MD5.Digest` output, so it is an internal assertion.
- `List_Objects` and `List_Object_Versions` re-raise only cancellation and timeout and map everything else, including a `Program_Error` from an internal invariant, to `Backend_Unavailable`; that hides invariant failures behind a retryable status, a debugging and operability concern rather than a wrong answer.
- Linear scans in the index lookups and the O(n^2) `List_Versions` insertion are performance characteristics of a bounded reference oracle; they were not measured, since the host's power profile was not established.

### BACKEND-FILES

- `docs/compatibility/s3.md` annotation rows say "server absent" (for example `DeleteObjectAnnotation` at `:48`) while `src/flyology-object_storage-server-s3_applications.adb:3189-3200` routes `?annotation` and `:15622`, `:15653`, `:15683`, `:15736` dispatch all four operations; documentation drift that does not change the reachability of BACKEND-FILES-1 or -4.
- `docs/architecture/files-layout.md:123-136` still describes the FOSOBJ05 single-file layout, `Not_Implemented` for enabled-bucket PUT and no retained generations, while the code implements FOSOBJ07 with a generation history; `docs/qualification/copy-object.md:55` says the backend cannot contain annotations while `Store` implements `Object_Annotation_Backend`.
- `files-layout.md:137-139` describes DeleteObjects as "a complete nonmutating preflight before the first removal"; the preflight (`:8461-8476`) is structural only and each entry's conditions are evaluated inside the removal loop, which matches S3 per-entry semantics, so the wording overstates it.
- `Complete_Multipart_Upload` (`:11554-11954`) and `Copy_Object` snapshot creation (`:7693-7725`) hold the publication gate while copying the whole body, so every other operation on the store blocks for that duration and deadline-bound callers fail with `Timeout_Error`; a bounded stall and a latency concern, not a hang.
- `List_Object_Versions` (`:10604-10610`) and the memory backend (`memory.adb:2677-2687`) both return `Invalid_Request` when a supplied key-marker/version-id-marker pair no longer exists; consistent across backends, and AWS behaviour is not verifiable from the pinned model.
- `Put_Object_Internal` samples `Info.Modified` before the body is streamed (`:7113`), so `Last-Modified` reflects upload start; ordering uses publication numbers, so listings stay correct.
- `Open` takes no cross-process lock, so two processes opening one root corrupt each other's staging assumptions; documented as unsupported.
- `Has_Objects` (`:3508-3510`) calls `End_Search` in its handler even when `Start_Search` never ran; harmless with GNAT's implementation and shadowed by the outer handler.
- `List_Objects` and `List_Object_Versions` call `Select_Generation`, a directory scan, per file, making listings O(files x generations); performance only.
- `Get_Object` reports `Partial => False` for a range request on an empty object (`Empty_Object_Range`, `:8042-8050`); range resolution policy lives in the core package, outside this area.
- Policy values not counted: `Maximum_Object_Path_Depth` = 32, `Maximum_Metadata_Length` = 8 KiB, `Maximum_Checksum_Length` = 96, 64 KiB streaming buffers, the 10 ms gate poll interval and the 16 MiB document bounds.

### BACKEND-SQLITE

- The same-key copy predicate confirmed as BACKEND-MEMORY-2 is duplicated verbatim at `sqlite/src/flyology-object_storage-backends-sqlite.adb:2436-2445`; counted once, in that area, but a fix has to change this backend too.
- O1 `backends-sqlite.adb:2884-2886`: `Delete_Objects` calls `Delete_Payload_If_Unreferenced` without the per-payload `begin ... when others => null` that `Delete_Selected_Object` uses (`:2823-2831`) and without its `Sync_Path (Objects_Path, Directory => True)` (`:2831-2833`). Below the rubric, because `Delete_Payload_If_Present` (`:224-233`) swallows every filesystem error, so only a SQLite failure inside the catalog call can raise, and the `Backend_Unavailable` result is explicitly ambiguous by contract; worth fixing for symmetry, not a demonstrable defect.
- O2 `catalogs.adb:8776-9040`: `Complete_Multipart_Upload` fills `Retired_Payloads` only from `Collect_Multipart_Payloads` and never calls `Append_Version_Annotation_Payloads_Internal`, unlike `Put_Object` (`:6066`, `:6087`) and the delete paths (`:6500`, `:6554`), so annotation payload files of replaced generations are orphaned. The catalog rows go away with the generation and startup reclamation removes the files, so this is a bounded disk leak, not incorrectness.
- O3 `catalogs.adb:7981-7988, 8122-8218`: `List_Objects` scans every object of the bucket per page and `List_Object_Versions` appends every prefix-matching generation into an Ada vector before projecting the page, both while holding the gate; per-request memory and latency grow with bucket size. Scalability rather than correctness, but it compounds BACKEND-SQLITE-3, because a long listing is what makes another request's deadline expire while it waits for the gate.
- O4 `catalogs.adb:8221-8237`: a `Version_ID_Marker` that no longer exists yields `Invalid_Request`; the memory backend does the same (`backends-memory.adb:2677-2691`), so this is a cross-backend design choice outside this area.
- O5 `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:20-23`: superseded bodies are said to be retained until the recovery pass, while the code unlinks them immediately after commit. Confirmed and promoted into BACKEND-SQLITE-1 as the documented invariant the code breaks, so it is not counted separately here.
- O6 `flyology_object_storage_sqlite_durability.c:35-38`: the Windows `sync_path` really does open with `GENERIC_READ` only, which would fail `FlushFileBuffers`. Unverifiable in this environment, because `docs/architecture/sqlite-backend.md:159` names Linux and macOS as the CI-qualified targets; it would need a Windows host.
- O7 `backends-sqlite.adb:2220-2229, 2279-2282`: if `COMMIT` itself raises after SQLite has durably written the WAL commit frame, the backend deletes the new payload while the catalog may reference it after recovery. The contract already calls `Backend_Unavailable` ambiguous; theoretical and unverifiable here.
- O8 `backends-sqlite.adb:2209-2212`: `Rename_File` would silently replace an existing `objects/<name>.blob`; the name derivation makes a collision practically impossible, and a `Create_New_File` on the destination would make it impossible.
- O9 `catalogs.adb:918-928`: `Gate.Acquire` is an unconditional entry and ignores the caller's `Token` and `Deadline` while waiting, and long listings hold the gate. On its own it is latency, but it is the amplifier that makes BACKEND-SQLITE-3 easy to trigger under load, so a fix for that P0 should consider it.
- O10 `databases.ads:14`: `Busy_Timeout_Millis := 5_000` and the 64 KiB stream buffers are policy values; with the root lock there is no second writer, so busy handling is exercised only by external tools.
- O11 `backends-sqlite.adb:312-313`: `Open` rewraps every failure as "could not open SQLite backend", discarding the catalog's specific message for an unsupported schema, an integrity failure or an unavailable WAL. Diagnostics only.
- O12 `backends-sqlite.adb:2224-2229`: a `Delete_File` raise inside the `Result /= Success` branch re-enters the `Published` cleanup in the handler and would escape `Put_Object`; it requires an external actor removing the just-renamed file.
- O13 `catalogs.adb:2923`: bucket listing prefix matching uses `substr(name,1,length(?2))` on TEXT with character semantics, which is correct for the ASCII bucket names `Valid_Bucket_Name` admits.
- The bucket-configuration wrappers at `backends-sqlite.adb:320-2025` were inspected by outline only, and the catalog schema validators at `catalogs.adb:1000-1742` and the bucket tag, public-access-block, CORS and document wrappers at `:3397-4327`, `:4428-4677` and `:5025-5454` were not read line by line; the vendored SQLite amalgamation was not read, and no Docker server was used in this area.

### CLIENT-LOW

- Synchronous `Execute_Copy_Object` (`:14865`) and `Execute_Upload_Part_Copy` (`:14506`) may be replayed once by `flyology_http` on a stale reused transport: both send through the retained-request path, and `x-amz-copy-source-if-*` are not in the dependency's mutation-condition set. Stands, unreproduced — it needs a transport-fault injection the harness does not offer, namely a proxy that closes a reused keep-alive connection between request and response. Worst case is a duplicate copy or an extra version, not data loss.
- `Validate_Head_Object_Headers` requires a quoted ETag and a parseable `Last-Modified` on every 200/206 although the pinned model marks `HeadObjectOutput.ETag` and `.LastModified` optional. Stands as an observation with the client side now pinned by the probe (both omissions rejected as `invalid HeadObject response headers`; `Valid_Response_Entity_Tag` at `:5569-5586` returns False for `""` and `Object_Reads.Parse_Conditional_Date ("")` is not `Valid`). The demand exceeds the contract, but no server was found that omits either, so it is not called a defect; settling it needs a live probe against MinIO, RustFS and SeaweedFS for SSE-KMS, restored-archive and directory-bucket objects.
- `Validate_Create_Multipart_Headers` (`:3273`) requires `x-amz-checksum-algorithm` and `x-amz-checksum-type` to be both present or both absent. Stands, unverified; same shape as the previous row and needs live-server evidence.
- Listing echo comparison under `URL_Encoding`, and ListObjectsV2's requirement that `Has_Delimiter` match the request presence exactly (`:2022-2023`), unlike v1's tolerant `Matches_Optional_Echo`. Stands, unverified: the real-server corpus never sets `URL_Encoding`, so nothing exercises it.
- `Valid_Read_Checksum_Headers` rejects a 200 carrying two `x-amz-checksum-*` headers. Stands, unverified.
- `Decode_Delete_Objects_Complete_Response` (`:12777-12820`) requires an exact key and version pair match for every `<Deleted>` and `<Error>` entry. **Refuted for the reachable case that could be tested:** the probe drove a real unversioned two-entry `DeleteObjects` through the in-repo server over the loopback listener and it returned `Objects_Deleted` with `VersionId=""` on both entries, so the server does not echo a `null` version for an entry that carried none and the binding holds. It would still trip on a server that echoes `<VersionId>null</VersionId>`; no such server is known.
- HEAD responses carrying `Transfer-Encoding` are rejected (`:5757-5759`, `:4935-4937`) although RFC 9110 permits the field on a HEAD response. Stands as an observation with the client side pinned by the probe (`HeadObject response uses transfer coding`); no known server sends it.
- CreateSession secret material is not zeroized (`:2674-2703`): neither the raw XML payload nor the intermediate `Raw_Create_Session_Credentials` unbounded strings holding the secret key. Documented limitation — the architecture documentation states that allocator memory is not zeroed — and therefore not a finding.
- `Wire_Query` sends a genuinely empty query value as `name` rather than `name=`. Not a defect as stated: an empty `Version_ID` means the member is omitted entirely, so `versionId=` is not reachable from the typed preparers, and both sides canonicalise `name` identically for SigV4.

### CLIENT-OBJECTS

- `Normalize_Put_Response` (`:1708-1792`) treats every modeled rejection outside its table, for example `404 NoSuchKey` or `400 BadDigest`, as `Outcome_Unknown` with `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response`. This is the maintained policy of `tests/corpora/composable-client/put-certainty.tsv` ("status alone does not prove semantic rejection"), so it is a documented limitation; the `Failure` label is unflattering for a well-formed S3 error whose code is simply not in the put table, and it forces a reconciliation read after, for example, a `Put_If_Matches` on an absent key.
- Whole and range reads bound the entire response body, error XML included, by the destination capacity, which `src/flyology-object_storage-client-objects.ads:442-443` states ("Destination capacity is the response body bound"). A tight destination therefore reports a large `NoSuchKey` error body as `Response_Too_Large` and hides the S3 code. Real, but specified; a documented limitation.
- `Failed_Reason` (`:32-46`) maps `Response_Body_Too_Large` to `Response_Too_Large` while the mutation normalizers override it to `Corrupt_Or_Invalid_Response` (`Normalize_Get_Object_ACL_Failure:4943-4962` and peers). Only the bounded-buffer reads can receive that HTTP result, so no caller can observe the divergence today; a latent inconsistency worth a comment, not a defect.
- `Bind_Response_Range` rejects `Content-Range: bytes a-b/*`, but `client-low_level.adb`'s `Valid_Content_Range` rejects the `*` total first and S3 always sends the total, so there is no reachable effect.
- The ListObjects v1 marker fallback uses the last `Contents` key when `NextMarker` is absent on a delimiter listing (`:391-416`). It is reached only for a truncated page, prefers `NextMarker`, and raises `Invalid_Response` rather than looping when a truncated page has neither; S3 requires `NextMarker` whenever a delimiter is used, so this is a server-deviation path only.
- `Singleton_Header` in `Get_Object_ACL` (`:4980-4998`) and `Delete_Object_Annotation` (`:6924-6944`) refuses a present but empty `x-amz-request-id:` or `x-amz-id-2:` value. Both return `""` for `Count = 0`, so an *absent* header is accepted and this is not another instance of the CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 and CLIENT-OBJECTS-2 class; no observed server sends an empty id header.
- SigV4 timestamps are taken at `Start_*`, so a deadline exceeding the SigV4 skew window under a saturated client pool would sign before admission and fail authentication. A timeout and pool policy choice, not an incorrectness.
- `docs/qualification/get-object-attributes.md:41-42` carries the same strict-echo requirement CLIENT-OBJECTS-1 shows is not satisfiable against real servers, but the code it describes lives in `Low_Level` and belongs to CLIENT-LOW.
- `Delete_Object_Annotation` and `List_Annotations` are an annotation extension absent from the pinned S3 model; they were reviewed only for state-machine consistency, not for wire conformance.

### CLIENT-BUCKETS

- HeadBucket statuses 301, 307, 400 and 501 are labelled `Invalid_Request` (`client-buckets.adb:4806-4807`), which `client.ads:26` defines as a "Local or modeled service request rejection"; a redirect is not one. Folded into CLIENT-BUCKETS-2 rather than counted separately, because it has no observable consequence beyond the discarded region.
- The generated result records carry a synthesised `Status => 500` sentinel in the exchange-failed case. `Put_Bucket_Replication_Result` and its siblings are flat records (`client-buckets.ads:6763-6772`), so on a transport failure `Normalize_Put_Bucket_Replication_Failure` (`client-buckets.adb:8765-8769`) stores `(Kind => Put_Bucket_Control_Rejected, Status => 500, Error => (others => <>))` into a field the spec documents as the complete modeled S3 response, and a caller who reads `Result.Response.Status` without first testing `Result.Kind` sees a fabricated 500 for an exchange in which no response was received. The variant-record results in the same file raise `Constraint_Error` instead, which is the safe behaviour; the inconsistency between the two families is the part worth raising with the maintainer. Not counted: it needs caller misuse, and the code comment at `:8765-8766` shows the choice was deliberate.
- `Pre_Admission_Rejected` becomes `Constraint_Error` with no `@exception` documentation: `client-buckets.adb:95` and `:124` (and the parallel mappers) `raise Constraint_Error with "HTTP request was rejected"`, while `grep -c '@exception' src/flyology-object_storage-client-buckets.ads` returns 0 for a spec whose convenience overloads can raise `Constraint_Error`, `Operation_Cancelled`, `Timeout_Error`, `Client_Closed`, `Connection_Error`, `Device_Error`, `Request_Body_Error` and `Invalid_Response`. A documentation gap rather than a behaviour defect; using a language check exception as a transport signal is a design choice a maintainer may want to revisit.
- Stale architecture prose: `docs/architecture/client-buckets.md:73-74` still says bucket-tagging "checksum selection currently fails locally until paired checksum generation is implemented", while `client-low_level.adb:224-245` computes and signs a digest for all ten modelled algorithms and `docs/qualification/bucket-tagging.md:51-52` says all ten are covered. Documentation only.
- CreateSession retains the temporary secret in `Response_Data`: the raw XML is accumulated at `client-buckets.adb:3505` and released with `Flyology.Bytes.Clear` on every exit path (`:3616`, `:3640`, `:3715-3776`). The pinned dependency documents `Clear` as "Release all retained elements and backing capacity" (`flyology_0.1.1/src/flyology-bytes.ads:91-93`), which is release, not zeroisation; the spec promises zeroisation only for the decoded identity (`client-buckets.ads:10915-10917`, `:10939-10941`), so no contract is violated. Noted for a security reviewer.
- `Head` returns the signing region when `x-amz-bucket-region` is absent (`client-buckets.adb:2846-2849`), documented at `client-buckets.ads:10174-10176`, so not counted; measured against the live MinIO it means a `us-east-1` bucket is reported as `eu-west-1` purely because of the signing region, so `Head`'s `Region` is not a trustworthy region source on any server that omits the header — which is every server in the matrix.
- CLIENT-LOW-4 (a bodyless or non-XML 4xx/5xx becoming `Invalid_Response`, `src/flyology-object_storage-client-low_level.adb:1956-1958`) is reachable through every convenience overload in this file, because the `Raise_*_Exchange_Failure` mappers re-raise `Invalid_Response` for `Response_Invalid | Response_Body_Too_Large | Response_Sink_Failed` (`client-buckets.adb:108-112`, `:137-141`). Not duplicated here; it belongs to CLIENT-LOW, and CLIENT-BUCKETS-1 is the structured-error half of the same classification gap.
- Not reviewed in this area: ListObjects v1/v2 and ListObjectVersions cursors, `encoding-type=url` decoding, the `Contents`, `CommonPrefixes`, `Owner`, `RestoreStatus` and `ChecksumAlgorithm` typed outputs, and DeleteObjects. None of these live in `Client.Buckets`; they belong to CLIENT-OBJECTS and the S3 domain areas. Also not read line by line: the 23 generated read and mutation instances beyond the sampled ones, whose shape was verified structurally, and the `Bounded_REST_XML_Reads`, `Paginated_REST_XML_Reads` and `REST_XML_Mutations` generic bodies themselves.

### TRANSFERS

- `docs/architecture/client-transfers.md` says the planner rejects plans above "the supported 50 TiB object bound" while `Core.Maximum_Object_Size` is 50,000 GiB = 48.83 TiB, exactly `10,000 x 5 GiB`; no object in the gap can satisfy the 10,000-part rule, so the wording has no observable effect.
- `S3.Multipart.Validate` (complete request, `:544-551`) demands consecutive part numbers whenever any part carries a checksum, while the server policy requires that for composites only and AWS is believed to allow non-consecutive parts with full-object part checksums (from memory, unverified); `Upload_File` always numbers parts 1..N, so only a hand-built manifest reaches the stricter client rule.
- `S3.Multipart.Validate` (ListParts result, `:1635-1637`) requires `IsTruncated = (NextPartNumberMarker > 0)`; whether AWS echoes the last part number on an untruncated page is unverified, and the pinned model's prose covers only the truncated case.
- One correction to the discovery observation on `max-parts`: the pinned MinIO release does **not** clamp an over-large value, it echoes `<MaxParts>2000</MaxParts>` verbatim; the client cannot emit such a request at all, since `Max_Parts` is typed `Core.Page_Size` (0 .. 1,000), so the rejection at `s3-multipart.adb:145-158` is this repository's *server*-side rule.
- Whether AWS accepts a raw, a suffixed, or no completion checksum header for composite uploads is still unsettled: the pinned model documents the request member as "the Base64 encoded, N-bit checksum of the object" with no suffix while warning that the output member may be a calculation over the parts, which is suggestive but not decisive, and MinIO's acceptance does not prove AWS's.
- `Upload_File` hashes the entire file before `Valid_Multipart_Plan` can reject an over-10,000-part plan, and `Hash_File` silently stops appending digests at 10,000; the outcome is correct, with wasted I/O on a rejected plan only.
- `Upload_File`'s handler calls `Files.Close` directly, so a failing close would replace the original exception, where downloads use the swallowing `Close_After_Failure`; a close failing on a read-only descriptor is not a reachable input in practice.
- After a successful completion whose echoed checksum or type mismatches, `Initiated` is still True, so the handler issues an `AbortMultipartUpload` against an already-completed upload; the expected `NoSuchUpload` is swallowed and the caller gets the `Invalid_Response` the `.ads` classes as ambiguous.
- `Transfer_Many` allocates fresh worker tasks per wave and the next wave cannot start until the slowest subject of the current one finishes; documented as bounded structured waves, performance only.
- `Abort_Best_Effort` uses a null token and a 5 s budget, so a cancelled or timed-out multipart subject can hold its wave open up to 5 s past the batch deadline; documented as an independent short cleanup budget.
- Resume and reconciliation are not implemented in `Upload_File` and are documented as caller-owned, with the direct `Upload_Part` and `List_Parts_Page` primitives as the documented path.

### S3-DOMAIN-A

The discovery report's twelve uncounted observations, each re-checked against the source at this commit. Two became findings.

- `max-keys` above 1000 rejected by the three query parsers — **promoted to S3-DOMAIN-A-6**. The discovery report's "documented policy value" framing does not hold: nothing documents it as a request rule, and the interop consequence it guessed at is confirmed with aws-cli and MinIO.
- `Versions.Validate`'s marker pairing — the discovery report raised the "`NextKeyMarker` present, `NextVersionIdMarker` empty" direction; the opposite direction is real and reachable and became **S3-DOMAIN-A-7**. The direction the discovery report named is not reproducible against MinIO, whose truncated pages always carry both markers non-empty.
- `Listings` requires `Size` on every object entry (`listings.adb:862-868`, `if not Item.Seen_Object_Key or else not Item.Seen_Object_Size … raise Malformed_Listing`) while the model and the AWS `Object` data type make it optional. Confirmed by reading; not promoted, because MinIO always emits `<Size>` and no server was found that omits it. Low interop risk, as the discovery report said.
- `Versions` rejects unknown elements anywhere in `ListVersionsResult` (`versions.adb:578-580`, "unknown ListObjectVersions response field", and `:632-634` inside `<Version>`) while `Listings` and `Buckets` skip them. Confirmed by reading; not triggered by MinIO, whose page uses only modeled elements. It does mean any future server extension inside `<Version>` would fail the whole listing, the same total-loss mode as S3-DOMAIN-A-1 and S3-DOMAIN-A-7.
- `Replication.Valid_Integer` rejects a leading `+` (`replication.adb:504-505`) while `Lifecycle`, `Bucket_Controls`, `Intelligent_Tiering` and `Metadata_Configurations` accept it. Confirmed by reading; harmless, since no server emits `+1`.
- `Lifecycle` and `Replication` do not enforce the model's `Tag.Key` minimum length of 1 while `Analytics`, `Metrics` and `Intelligent_Tiering` do. Confirmed by reading; harmless drift.
- `Buckets.Parse_Create_Configuration` rejects any `LocationConstraint` outside the pinned AWS enumeration (`buckets.adb:444-446` via `Valid_Location_Constraint`, with the `Location`/`Bucket` pairing rules at `:447-467`). Confirmed by reading; it mirrors AWS's own rejection of `us-east-1` in a `CreateBucketConfiguration`, so it is a defensible policy. It does refuse self-hosted region names, which is worth a maintainer decision but is not a defect against AWS.
- `Buckets.Serialize_List_Buckets` emits `<Bucket></Bucket>` for an empty `Name`. Server-side only, and the server never stores an empty bucket name.
- `Generated_Put_Bucket_ACL_XML` writes `Owner` before `AccessControlList` while the model orders `Grants` first. Confirmed by reading the generated body. S3's ACL request parser is order-insensitive here, but that was not verified against a server, so it stays an observation with the discovery report's "from memory" caveat intact.
- `ACL.Grantee_Type_Seen` is dead: set unconditionally at `acl.adb:193`, so `not Item.Grantee_Type_Seen` at `:324` can never be True. Confirmed by reading; harmless, because the attribute requirement is enforced at `:138-144`.
- Versioning `Default_Limits` (4 KiB, depth 2, 3 elements, 64 text bytes, `versioning.ads:10-17`) and the 10,000-bucket cap in `Buckets` are documented policy values. Confirmed; the 64-byte text limit is comfortably above anything the two modeled members can contain, so it is not reachable in practice.
- The server-side emitters share the S3-DOMAIN-A-4 root names (`server-s3_applications.adb:9599-9608` for the four configuration listings, `:7237` for `<ListDirectoryBucketsOutput/>`), and `docs/qualification/list-directory-buckets.md:14` documents the wrong root as the server's contract. Confirmed by reading; carried into S3-DOMAIN-A-4 as the SERVER-area half of that fix rather than counted separately.

Two further observations were recorded by the verifier and are not counted:

- Codec diagnostics are erased by the shared XML boundary. `S3.XML.Parse` catches `others` and re-raises as `XML_Error "malformed S3 XML document"` (`s3-xml.adb:203-207`), so a `Malformed_Version_Listing` raised inside a SAX callback — "invalid ObjectVersion storage class", "unknown ListObjectVersions response field", "incomplete ObjectVersion entry" — reaches the caller as `malformed ListObjectVersions XML: malformed S3 XML document`, which is visible in the S3-DOMAIN-A-1 output. Exceptions raised from `Validate`, which runs after parsing, keep their message, as S3-DOMAIN-A-2 and S3-DOMAIN-A-7 show. The least diagnosable failures are therefore exactly the per-entry ones. Wrong diagnostics, P2 by the rubric; not promoted, because it is shared infrastructure outside this area's codecs.
- `Generated_List_Directory_Buckets_XML.Parse` re-raises with no message (`generated_list_directory_buckets_xml.adb:272-274`, `raise Malformed_Document;`), so the caller sees a source location instead of a reason. Same class as above, and named in S3-DOMAIN-A-4 because that is where it shows.

### S3-DOMAIN-B

- `Tagging.Parse_Header` keeps `+` literal, and the oracle the discovery asked for is now recorded: MinIO decodes a plus sign as a space, so `PUT /obj` with `x-amz-tagging: a+b=c+d` followed by `GET /obj?tagging` returns `<Tag><Key>a b</Key><Value>c d</Value></Tag>` (and `a%20b=c%20d` gives the same result), while Flyology stores the key `a+b`. This is explicitly documented policy — `src/flyology-object_storage-s3-tagging.ads:27-30`, "unlike HTML form encoding, a plus sign is not decoded as a space" — so under the rubric it is a documented limitation rather than a numbered finding. The verifier's note is that this is the one observation in the area whose consequence is *silently different data* rather than an error: any client that builds `x-amz-tagging` with a form encoder, which is the usual `urlencode(...)` recipe, will store different tags on Flyology than on MinIO or AWS, with no diagnostic on either side. An explicit maintainer decision is recommended.
- `Copies.Validate` (`copies.adb:336-340`) requires both `ETag`, strong and quoted, and `LastModified`, strict ISO-8601, although every `CopyObjectResult` member is optional in the model, so SeaweedFS's bare `ETag` is rejected. Confirmed as stated and confirmed to be deliberate: `docs/qualification/copy-object.md:215` says "the production decoder continues to require one strong quoted entity-tag". A documented limitation.
- Object `Tagging.Parse` requires `<Key>` before `<Value>` inside `<Tag>` (`tagging.adb:78`: the `Value` arm tests `Item.Seen_Key`), while the bucket parser accepts either order (`tagging.adb:485`: the `Value` arm tests only `not Item.Seen_Value`). Re-derived from source and confirmed as an internal inconsistency, but every SDK serialises in model order and every captured client body above does, so no reachable client is affected.
- `Multipart_Uploads.Parse_List_Multipart_Uploads_Query` rejects `max-uploads` outside 1..1000 with `InvalidArgument`, documented at `multipart_uploads.ads:33-35` ("max-uploads is in 1 .. 1_000"). MinIO accepts and echoes both `0` and `5000`, so the divergence is real, but no captured client sends such a value. A documented limitation.
- `Object_Reads.Parse_Conditional_Date` and the `Ada.Calendar` limits: out-of-range values yield `Valid => False`, which callers treat as an ignorable invalid HTTP-date per RFC 9110. Harmless.
- `Multipart_Uploads.Validate:642-654` rejects a non-truncated page that carries next markers, and a truncated ungrouped page without `NextUploadIdMarker`. Not reproducible against MinIO, which emits empty `<NextKeyMarker></NextKeyMarker>` elements on a non-truncated page; those decode to zero-length strings and pass. Separately noted from the same run: MinIO answered `max-uploads=1` with three `<Upload>` entries and `<MaxUploads>1</MaxUploads><IsTruncated>false</IsTruncated>`, which `Validate` correctly rejects on its first branch — a MinIO defect rather than a crate defect, though it does mean the crate's client cannot list multipart uploads against this MinIO release.
- `Annotations.Parse_List` requires the root `ListObjectAnnotationsOutput` although the model gives that output no `locationName`. Confirmed as stated; both sides of the crate agree on that root, so it is consistent today, and S3-DOMAIN-B-3 shows that agreement on the root is not evidence of general model conformance for this operation.
- `Annotations.Decode_Continuation:246-250`, a candidate `Constraint_Error` on tokens shorter than 70 bytes: refuted and re-verified. `Core.Valid_Listing_Continuation_Syntax` (`s3-core.adb:8-23`) rejects any token shorter than the 70-byte header before the slices are taken.
- `Deletions.Serialize_Request` emits `ETag` before `Key` (`deletions.adb:407-412`), out of model member order. Re-derived from source; receivers are order-insensitive — the crate's own `Parse_Request` is, and MinIO accepted `mc`'s body whose `<Quiet>` comes first.
- Mandatory `Content-MD5` on DeleteObjects is out of area (SERVER-1) and is not counted here. It was independently re-observed in this area: current boto3 1.43.89 and aws-cli 2.36.34 send `x-amz-checksum-crc32` instead of `Content-MD5` and are answered `400 InvalidRequest` "DeleteObjects requires one valid Content-MD5 header". The same gate applies to PutBucketTagging ("PutBucketTagging requires a valid Content-MD5"), which the SERVER report may want to note.
- MinIO's lowercase `getObjectAttributesResponse` root, which `attributes.adb:405-408` rejects, is already a documented exclusion: `tests/scripts/test-minio.sh:64,74` sets `FLYOLOGY_GET_OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES_ORACLE_MODE=minio-2025-lowercase-root`, "a non-AWS lowercase root element". Not a new finding, but it is the reason both S3-DOMAIN-B-7 and S3-DOMAIN-B-8 were never exercised in-tree, and the reason both programs re-root MinIO's captured bytes under the AWS root.

### SERVER-EXE

- **Corrected by the verifier.** The discovery recorded that "With `FLYOLOGY_S3_BIND=0.0.0.0`, `SO_REUSEADDR` on the S3 listener lets another local user on BSD-derived kernels bind `127.0.0.1:9000` more specifically". The hijack is real but the stated mechanism is wrong. All four combinations were measured on this macOS host: wildcard `SO_REUSEADDR` True with specific True SUCCEEDED, wildcard True with specific False was refused (48 Address already in use), wildcard False with specific True SUCCEEDED, wildcard False with specific False was refused. Only the *attacker's* socket needs `SO_REUSEADDR`; the option the server sets on its own listener (`runtime.adb:214-215`) is irrelevant to the outcome, and the more-specific listener does receive the loopback connections, verified by accepting one. It is therefore a platform property of any wildcard bind on BSD-derived kernels, not something the server's socket options cause or can remove, and removing `SO_REUSEADDR` from the S3 listener would not help. It stays an observation about the documented `FLYOLOGY_S3_BIND=0.0.0.0` option on multi-user macOS hosts and is not a defect in the executable's code.
- Credential-file default drift, confirmed. `server/README.md:72-73` says the file defaults "to `admin.credentials` inside a persistent backend root, or `./flyology-admin.credentials` for memory"; `configuration.adb:47-54` uses `<root>/admin.credentials` whenever `FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_ROOT` is set, memory backend included. Documentation drift only.
- Main's handler does not cover its declarative part, confirmed. Running the built executable with `FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_BACKEND=not-a-backend` prints GNAT's `Execution of … terminated by unhandled exception / raised CONSTRAINT_ERROR : FLYOLOGY_OBJECT_STORAGE_BACKEND must be memory, files, or sqlite` with a traceback rather than the `object-storage server startup failed:` prefix, exit status 1. Fail-closed and the cause is named, so cosmetic; it is also the control that makes SERVER-EXE-4's silence a gap rather than a house style. Within `Load`, a non-numeric or out-of-range `FLYOLOGY_S3_PORT`, `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_PORT` or `FLYOLOGY_S3_CAPACITY` is re-raised with GNAT's `'Value` or range-check text rather than a variable name, and based or underscored literals are accepted.
- `Local_Request` is a textual authority comparison, confirmed against a live server on port 64932: `Host: localhost:64932` receives `HTTP/1.1 421 Status` while `Host: 127.0.0.1:64932` receives `HTTP/1.1 200 OK`. Only hand-typed URLs are affected; the printed `READY` line gives the numeric form.
- The server crate is not built or tested in CI, confirmed. `grep -n 'server' .github/workflows/ci.yml` matches only lines 271 and 301, both about `tests/scripts/`, so nothing in the workflow builds `server/` or runs `server/tests/scripts/test.sh`; the lightweight handler configuration that produces SERVER-EXE-1, the management API, the credential bootstrap and the SIGTERM path are gated only by a local `alr test`. A process gap that belongs with the CI and baseline area rather than with this crate's code.
- The assets loader requires a working directory or `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_ASSET_ROOT`, confirmed. `assets.adb:16-27` looks only at `./assets/index.html` and `./server/assets/index.html`, so started from any other directory the executable fails with `CONSTRAINT_ERROR : management assets not found`. Documented at `server/README.md:74-76` and fail-closed; every reproduction script above sets `FLYOLOGY_ADMIN_ASSET_ROOT` explicitly for that reason.
- `Assets.Read_All` rebuilds files through `Ada.Text_IO.Get_Line`, so the pinned digest is over a normalised image: a missing final newline is added and one trailing empty line is dropped. Only trailing whitespace of an asset can differ from the pinned bytes, and the served bytes equal the digested bytes, so the integrity of executable content is intact.
- Policy values, not counted as defects: memory store `Bucket_Capacity 256`, `Object_Capacity 100_000`, `Byte_Capacity` 16 GiB (`server.adb:52-54`), S3 handler timeout 60 s, 10,000 requests and 600 s per connection, management capacity 32, session capacity 64 and 12 h, bucket inventory 256, object page 64 and 128, drain 10 s and 2 s, grace 12 s, readiness 15 s, ingress budget 4 KiB, clock skew 900 s, torrent piece 256 KiB.
- Credential-file edge cases, all fail-closed: a zero-length file fails startup with `End_Error` from `Get_Line` instead of "invalid administrator credential file"; a directory at the path is reported as "must be owner-owned mode 0600"; `umask` bits in the owner triple make `open(..., 0600)` create a `0400` file that the next startup rejects, as does an operator `chmod 400`; and `link(2)` publication fails on filesystems without hard links, or against a stale `<path>.tmp.<pid>` from a crashed process with a reused PID.
- Password and secret copies survive on the secondary stack (the `Hex` result, the `"BOOTSTRAP … password=" & Password` concatenation, the `Set-Cookie` header string) after the named buffers are wiped; no reachable path exposes them. Bootstrap ordering also leaves a persisted credential whose password was shown once if a later configuration or bind failure occurs, which commit `4401e0e` already isolates in tests. With `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` absent the executable prints `BOOTSTRAP S3 access_key=FLYOLOGYLOCAL secret_key=<64 hex>` to standard error on every start as the README documents, and the `S3_Secret_Key` constant is retained unwiped for the process lifetime alongside the provider's wiped copy.
- Same-site cookies: because `SameSite` treats every port of `127.0.0.1` as one site, a page on another loopback port can attach the session cookie to cross-origin requests; mutations require an exact `Origin`, login and logout reject a mismatching one, and the GET inventory routes are unreadable cross-origin.
- `Status` reports `s3_address` as bound, for example `0.0.0.0`, and `app.js:157` renders `http://0.0.0.0:<port>` as the copyable endpoint. Cosmetic.
- `Run_S3` does not close `Listener` when `Bind_Socket` or `Listen_Socket` raise before `Serve` takes ownership, so the descriptor leaks once per failed generation, bounded by the recovery limits at most ten per process; it is not separately reproducible without also triggering SERVER-EXE-4.
- A second SIGTERM re-latches the flag with no forced exit; SIGINT and SIGHUP take the default action and terminate without drain. Backends are crash-safe by design and the README documents only SIGTERM.
- Stop policy `Request_Abort => False` with a 12 s grace (`runtime.adb:989-992`): a handler that ignores cancellation for longer than the grace is classified `Stop_Timeout` then `Stuck`, and `Supervisors.Run` cannot return while the task is alive, so the process would not exit on SIGTERM. No reachable handler was found that blocks that long — the PBKDF2 derivation of SERVER-EXE-1 is far shorter and backend I/O observes the token — leaving a wedged disk as the only scenario, outside the executable's control.
- The S3 request deadline of 60 s is a monotonic deadline for each complete request including the streamed body, so a single-request upload or download needing more than 60 s over the configured link is cut; multipart clients are unaffected, and the in-tree harness uses the same value, so this is a consistent policy value rather than drift. During the 10-second S3 drain only admission stops, and keep-alive connections keep serving until forced cancellation then cuts an in-flight request without acknowledging it, consistent with the README's "allows a ten-second S3 drain".
- `Mark_Ready` after a stop request raises `Program_Error`, reachable only by a SIGTERM in the milliseconds between a child's bind and its readiness call; the generation ends with an unlogged exception, the listener descriptor is not closed, and the process exits 0. Harmless.
- Gaps in `server/tests/scripts/test.sh` beyond the four named in the findings above: a credential file with a foreign owner, a symlink, a directory or zero length (only `0644` is tested), a root that is a file or symlink, `FLYOLOGY_S3_BIND` other than the default, `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` with the complete pair, `AWS_REGION` other than `us-east-1`, session expiry, session-slot eviction, ingress-budget exhaustion, a second SIGTERM, an S3 upload in flight at SIGTERM, a SIGTERM before readiness, the `FLYOLOGY_LOOP_POOL_SIZE` variable, and the exit status after a child failure. The reload run asserts only login and status, not that objects written before the restart are still listed.

### MODEL

- **O-1 (dead `querystring` branch, `tools/s3_operation.py:1228-1231`):** confirmed verbatim by reading — the branch does `target = target.replace(marker, encoded)` while `encoded` is bound only in the `uri` branch at `:1219` and `:1222` — but refuted as a finding by enumerating all 116 operations, none of whose `requestUri`s contains a `{<locationName>}` for a `querystring` member, so the branch cannot execute; a latent `UnboundLocalError` in a test tool, not a product defect.
- **O-2 (diagnostic precision, `tools/s3-operation.py:237-247`, where `generate --check` without a model prints "current" after a narrower check):** outside what a JSON-versus-descriptor differential can settle and deliberately not re-litigated; nothing in the verification contradicts the discovery's disposition that CI always supplies the model and the full gate fails closed, so a stale generated Ada file cannot reach `main`.
- **O-3 (member-ordinal fragility):** upheld and now bounded — all 74 literal ordinals are correct today and the SHA pin plus the full-text `generate --check` make silent drift impossible for this model; the residual risk is a future model bump that inserts a member before one of them, which would compile and validate against the wrong shape, and `low_level.adb:11669-11673` already shows the name-based alternative (`Find_Model_Member`). `tests/repros/python/model_differential.py` is a ready regression gate for exactly that bump: standard-library-only, no build required, and its consumer table fails closed on an ordinal that moves.
- **O-4 (descriptor omissions with no consumer affected):** upheld and made exhaustive — the verification replaced the discovery's list with a complete attribute-by-attribute table with instance counts and the consumer each omission would need, and confirmed that none has a `src/` consumer at this commit.
- **O-5 (the ARN pattern on a header member, `low_level.adb:927-929`):** refuted as unreachable. All four pattern-carrying shapes were enumerated: `CopySource` and `RenameSource` are the only ones on request-serialized non-body members; `S3RegionalOrS3ExpressBucketArnString` is a header member of `CreateBucketOutput` and `HeadBucketOutput`, and `NonEmptyKmsKeyArnString` and `Bucket.BucketArn` are body members that `Prepare_Model_Request_Internal` rejects at `low_level.adb:1072-1074`. The ordinal table shows `Valid_Model_Scalar` is applied to response headers only for CreateSession (`:2735-2737`) and CopyObject (`:14725-14734`), never to HeadBucket or CreateBucket, and the residual behaviour would be a fail-closed `return False` rather than a wrong accept.
- **O-6 (XML list item-name fallback, `tools/s3_codegen.py:967-969`):** confirmed verbatim — `member.get("locationName", item.get("locationName", item_shape))` falls back to the item shape name where REST-XML uses `member` — and refuted, because the only two non-flattened lists whose member lacks `locationName`, `ObjectAttributesList` and `OptionalObjectAttributesList`, are used exclusively as header members of `GetObjectAttributesRequest`, `ListObjectVersionsRequest`, `ListObjectsRequest` and `ListObjectsV2Request`, so neither ever appears in an XML body.
- **O-7 (`Long_Long_Integer'Value` leniency, `low_level.adb:949-963` with the `'Value` call at `:955`):** confirmed present but ruled a `Valid_Model_Scalar` behaviour rather than a descriptor question, and routed to the CLIENT-LOW area, where it was escalated to CLIENT-LOW-7; the descriptor supplies the bounds correctly, comparing `Minimum` and `Maximum` as 64-bit values for both `integer` and `long` kinds, so no model-side defect contributes to it.
- **O-8 (unread checksum traits — `Request_Checksum_Required`, `Request_Validation_Mode_Member` and `Response_Checksum_Algorithms` emitted with no `src/` consumer):** outside what a JSON-versus-descriptor differential can settle and not re-litigated; it belongs to the CHECKSUM area by the discovery's own routing, which owns whether every `requestChecksumRequired` operation actually sends one.
- **O-9 (test-harness ordering, `tools/s3_operation.py:1273-1279`, where the expected request target sorts encoded `name=value` strings lexically rather than by (name, value) as SigV4 does):** not addressed by the verification, whose differential compares the descriptor with the JSON and does not reach the signed-socket harness; the discovery's disposition therefore stands unchallenged — the two orders differ only when one name is a prefix of another followed by a byte below `=`, no reviewed fixture contains such a pair, and a mismatch would fail the signed-socket test rather than ship, since the client signs with `SigV4.Canonical_Query` (`sigv4.adb:128-154`).

### BASELINE

- The first `tests/scripts/test.sh` run failed at the GetObjectAnnotation model verifier only because `FLYOLOGY_S3_SERVICE_MODEL` was unset in the local shell; CI sets it at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:118` and the rerun passes, so this is an environment prerequisite rather than a repository defect.
- The Docker conformance matrix and the individual MinIO and SeaweedFS scripts fail locally at the same exchange as RustFS; that failure is CLIENT-LOW-1 and is not counted again here.
- The scheduled-run logs also carry a GitHub deprecation warning about Node.js 20 actions being forced onto Node.js 24; unrelated to the gate's evidential value.

Every reproduction program was run once more in one uninterrupted sweep after all
verification and drafting work had finished (logs under `review/sweep/`):

- `tests/repros` (Ada): 54 of 54 programs printed `BUG REPRODUCED`.
- `sqlite/tests/repros` (Ada, SQLite backend): 5 of 5 programs printed `BUG REPRODUCED`.
- `tests/repros/shell` (shell programs): 6 of 6 programs printed `BUG REPRODUCED`.

Python probes:

```
model_differential.py exit=0 (0 expected: no model finding)
checksum_boto3_probe.py exit=1 (1 expected: stock boto3 rejected)
```
